Carrick expects to remain at Man Utd beyond international break

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Michael Carrick expects to still be in charge of Manchester United after the international break, despite the Red Devils sitting in the Premier League's relegation zone.

United will spend the upcoming hiatus 18th in the table, despite launching two stirring fightbacks to take a point from a chaotic 4-4 draw with Aston Villa on Sunday.

The Red Devils trailed 2-0 after just three minutes and 40 seconds – the earliest they have ever trailed by multiple goals in a Premier League game – before roaring back to go into half-time at 2-2.

Villa then hit another quickfire double in the first eight minutes of the second half to again leave United up against it, and they still trailed 4-2 going into the second minute of second-half stoppage time.

However, Patrick Dorgu teed up Joshua Zirkzee to cut the deficit, then headed Bruno Fernandes' 96th-minute corner home to salvage the unlikeliest of points for Carrick's team.

But even with United becoming the first team in Premier League history to fight back from two goals down to equalise on two occasions in a single game, Carrick's future was still a hot topic after full-time.

United had been tipped to make a change in the dugout during the international break, having gone eight league games without a victory for just the second time, previously enduring such a run in April and May 2025 under Ruben Amorim.

Before facing Liverpool on November 22, United are guaranteed to spend at least 15 consecutive days in the relegation zone, marking their longest run of days in the drop zone since a 28-day period in September and October 1986.

But when asked if he could step away from the Old Trafford hot seat, Carrick told Sky Sports: "It's not going to happen, no."

That was part of a tense exchange with interviewer Patrick Davison, who suggested to Carrick that a change in management was thought to be a possibility.

Carrick replied: "Is there? We understand the situation; we haven't won enough games.

"But I expect to move forward and keep working on things we've been working on. We showed a lot of good in the game.

"If we tighten up on a few mistakes, we will win football matches."

15 – Manchester United are now guaranteed to spend at least 15 consecutive days in the Premier League relegation zone. It is their longest run of days in the top-flight drop zone since September/October 1986 (28). Dejected. https://t.co/wtpy0nCLOx

The last time United were in a lower position at least 10 games into a top-flight season was in 1986-87, when they were 20th at the same stage.

United's supporters protested against the way the Glazer Family and Ineos are running the club throughout Sunday's game, and forward Matheus Cunha said the fans' frustrations are keenly understood in the dressing room.

"Before we played, everyone was a fan," Cunha said. "You understand why they are frustrated. This is completely fine; we understand.

"We are on the pitch, and we need to manage this. We need to change the mentality. I don't think the fans are against us [players]; it's just that they want to see us perform and win games.

"Before anything else, we need to look inside – the players and the staff. It's a huge club, and to feel something like this [being in the relegation zone] is not normal. We need to come back."

Kyle Larson Secures His Third NASCAR Cup Championship in Opening Stage of Championship Race

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

With his fourth-place finish in the first stage of the championship race at Homestead-Miami, Kyle Larson is a three-time NASCAR Cup Series Champion.

Chase Briscoe blew up with three laps remaining in the opening stage of the NASCAR Cup Series Season finale at Homestead-Miami, ending the stage early while Kyle Larson ran fourth.

With NASCAR’s new rule to end the stage if the caution is within 10 laps of the end, Larson took home the minimum seven points needed to clinch the Championship.

Larson was told he was one point shy over the radio, but he was 64 points above Christopher Bell and 76 points above Bell. He clinched the championship.

Kyle Larson joins the likes of Lee Petty, David Pearson, Cale Yarborough, Darrel Waltrip, Tony Stewart, and Joey Logano as a three-time NASCAR Cup Series Champion.

Through The Chase, Larson had an average of 49.5 points per race, easily rising to the top of the standings and then pacing the field from there.

Entering Homestead, it wasn’t given, but it was likely he could clinch the championship in stage one.

Victoria Beaver is a nomadic sports writer who spends her time hopping between race tracks and hippie farms. She’s covered every corner of motorsports that will let her in from 410 Sprints to NASCAR to Supercross. Her daily driver is a 2010 Subaru that she refused to do the smallest amount of preventative maintenance on. Instead, she spends her free time and money building a 42-foot Skoolie to one day travel the country full time.

Spalletti: ‘Juventus wallow in doubt and lose confidence’

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Luciano Spalletti reiterated Juventus ‘can’t seem to be consistent’ and ‘lose confidence, so we wallow in doubt’ after a 2-2 draw with Fiorentina, but remained enigmatic on penalty appeals.

The Bianconeri had to fight back twice for a point in Florence this evening, as they went behind within four minutes to a Mateo Pellegrino header, then once Nick Woltemade had equalised with his first Serie A goal, Alieu Njie had restored the Viola advantage.

Gleison Bremer rose to meet a Douglas Luiz corner kick for the final 2-2 result, although both teams had late complaints against the referee.

“We tend to drown in a glass of water sometimes, and lose confidence in the way we play,” Spalletti told DAZN Italia.

“We conceded so early, but then reacted well, we just can’t seem to be consistent throughout a match, as a team or as individuals. We do really well for 10 minutes, then are lacking in something else the next, we aren’t consistent.”

The Njie goal raised eyebrows, because while Guglielmo Vicario had made the initial save, his teammates weren’t reactive enough to clear, allowing the Fiorentina player to bundle in the loose ball.

“In some moments we decided not to make decisions, we just stay there and accept the situation, without taking action or the initiative. Doing something takes away the doubt, but when you wallow in the doubt, you never manage to be in control of a situation,” confessed Spalletti.

It was a new trident attack this evening with Jeremie Boga and Randal Kolo Muani taking the wings, leaving Woltemade as the centre-forward.

“We made this choice because Fiorentina are aggressive, and seeing as we don’t build out too well from the back, at times we need to be more direct going for the centre-forward. It is not his greatest strength, holding up the ball, as he likes to combine with his teammates, but he’s the best at it in the squad.

“Kolo Muani is doing better, and with these fitness levels and rediscovered verve, you can put him anywhere and he’ll deliver a performance. He moves around a lot more and seems more engaged with the rest of the team.”

Radu Dragusin’s goal was disallowed for a push on Bremer, while Juve had penalty appeals rejected for a potential Joao Mario handling offence.

“I don’t know, do you have an alternative question?” replied Spalletti with a smile when shown the replay of the incident.

“I didn’t need a replay, I already saw it on the pitch.”

The result means that Juventus go into the break for international duty in seventh place, falling six adrift of Milan and the Champions League qualifying spots.

A tally of 18 points after 11 rounds is their worst start to a Serie A season since 15 points in 2021/22 under Max Allegri.

Patrick Mahomes to get more tests on right elbow after Sunday's injury

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Chiefs quarterback Patrick Mahomes didn’t miss any offensive snaps until garbage time in Sunday’s 35-12 win over the Jets, but it looked like he might have suffered a serious injury late in the first half.

Mahomes lost the football when he took a shot to the right elbow from Jets first-round pick David Bailey and he went to the sideline holding his arm in a way that led to fears of a serious injury. Mahomes grimaced while throwing on the sideline, but returned to the game after the Jets missed a field goal and then led the team to 21 points in the third quarter to put the win away.

After the game, Mahomes said at his press conference that it was a scare for him and he didn’t feel great but “if I’m able to play, I’m gonna play.” He said that he wasn’t “throwing the ball the way I wanted to” after halftime, but felt better as the game went on. He’ll still be getting more tests done to make sure the injury isn’t anything bigger than something that will make him sore on Monday.

“From the stories you hear, whenever you can grab the ball and try to throw it, you can’t throw,” Mahomes said. “Once I threw the football, I knew it wasn’t — hopefully, I haven’t done the test yet, it wasn’t ligament-type stuff, it was more muscle-type stuff. That’ll be important to kinda get worked out this week.”

Mahomes finished the game 23-of-31 for 242 yards and two touchdowns.

'Not many sports teams have done it' - Wolff 'proud' of Mercedes turnaround

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Mercedes won their ninth F1 Constructors' Championship with three races to spare of the 2026 season; Mercedes won eight in a row between 2014 and 2021 before a fallow period and Toto Wolff is particularly proud of how the Silver Arrows bounced back

Sports Journalist @NigelCJourno

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

Toto Wolff says he's "proud" of Mercedes' turnaround to win the 2026 Formula 1 Constructors' Championship and feels very few sports teams have bounced back so quickly.

Mercedes won their ninth constructors' title on Sunday at the Sao Paulo Grand Prix, five years after their last championship, which was their eighth in a row at the time.

Regulation changes in 2022 ended Mercedes' dominance as they struggled to get on top of the so-called ground effect cars, but they have won the first season of the new 2026 cars, and also wrapped up the Drivers' Championship earlier this month with Kimi Antonelli.

"The thing is when you are in these difficult moments, like we all are sometimes in our lives, it's not that easy to keep going," said Wolff.

"Looking back in retrospect, you're saying, 'we needed these tough moments in order to grow as people or to grow as an organisation'. But when you're down, that is quite difficult to feel.

"I think those last four years were very difficult between getting it wrong, then kind of understanding why it was wrong and then realising no we didn't understand. That was a bit back and forth.

"But we will be looking back in 10 or 20 years. When you look at the statistics, it wasn't that we were complete losers over those four years, because we finished second, (second), third, fourth [in the championship], we won races, won many races. So it wasn't that bad at all when you look at the sheer numbers, but it felt not good."

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

As Wolff points out, Mercedes still won races between 2022 and 2025, with George Russell and Lewis Hamilton winning seven Grands Prix between them.

"The resilience of the team, the people that held together in those tough times, is a factor, I think," continued Wolff.

"It feels really good sealing those two titles. Not many sports teams have done that after consecutive run of championships, then a difficult spell and then coming back, so I'm really proud that we are part of this very small group of teams that have done that in all sports."

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

Mercedes move level with Williams on nine constructors' titles - only McLaren (10) and Ferrari (16) have more in Formula 1 history.

Wolff says his pride comes from the hard work in Mercedes' UK factories in Brackley and Brixworth to produce the best car, on average, this season.

"Sometimes the drivers, or myself, we are so visible because we are in in front of the TV cameras, but there's so many people that are part of this," he told Sky Sports F1.

"I always say there are not two superstars, the drivers, but there are 2,500 superstars, all of them delivering a great job, being all of us contributing to such a championship."

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

Mercedes were unable to finish with either of their drivers on the podium in Brazil as Lando Norris dominated in mixed conditions, while Antonelli and Russell both spun.

Russell finished in fourth, just ahead of Antonelli who says everyone at Mercedes "deserves" this year's success.

"They have done an incredible year and it's an amazing group of people, who push every day to do their best and give us the best opportunity to fight for wins and championships," the Italian told Sky Sports F1.

"This year they have done an incredible job and that's why they deserve this championship and we will enjoy it together."

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

Russell added: "We achieved what we came here to do and won the Constructors' Championship. We would have liked to have done it in a bit more style, but it's an incredible achievement for everyone in the team.

"We've had four difficult years and the whole organisation put so much work into getting us back into this position. When I joined Mercedes in 2022, we hoped we'd be fighting for championships regularly, so to have gone through that journey and now get back to the top is something we should all be incredibly proud of."

12 Most Universally Loved Sitcom Characters Of All Time

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Sitcoms are famous for producing a whole host of lovable characters, but there are a few that come out as clear favorites among wider audiences. The sitcom is one of the most endearing formats on TV, providing audiences with comfort watches that they can revisit time and time again, and a cast of characters who largely remain familiar throughout the show.

With that said, every sitcom character typically needs a set of flaws that make them more interesting and serve as a building block to create narratives around these individuals. And while some flaws are innocuous, there are others that can make a sitcom character insufferable.

On the flip side, there are a handful of sitcom legends who have cemented their place in audiences' hearts thanks to pretty flimsy flaws, or their charming and quirky personalities, or simply being a figure who often sees the best in others, and thus ends up having their best shine through.

With that said, the first person on this list is, of course, Ted Lasso. Played by Jason Sudeikis, Ted is a charming American man who moves to the UK to coach a football team, and while he has little to no knowledge about the sport or English customs, his endless positivity, warmth, and ability to draw out people's best serves him well in his new position.

Ted is also incredibly bright, with a high degree of emotional intelligence that allows him to tap into what his peers are feeling and connect on a level that many others struggle to replicate. Ted's charm and optimism earns him a spot as one of the most beloved characters on TV.

Next up, Phil Dunphy is remarkably similar to Ted Lasso in some ways, due to his incredible positive outlook and his warmth, but he does also possess a great deal more naïveté than Ted. Phil is clumsy, corny, and desperate to get approval from others, whether that be his wife, kids, father-in-law, or clients.

However, despite his surprising lack of self-awareness, Phil's depth of love and support for his family is the thing that makes him an undeniably endearing character that people can't help but fall in love with. Ty Burrell plays the part with incredible confidence and energy, and it all comes together in perfect harmony on the show season after season.

Matthew Perry may have been a more colorful character in life whose choices didn't always lead him down the healthiest paths, but his time as Chandler Bing on Friends will forever keep his memory alive in the hearts and minds of audiences everywhere. The interesting thing about Chandler is the fact that he is such a guarded character, always hiding behind humor and sarcasm.

However, despite his tendency to try and keep his emotions at a distance, Chandler becomes one of the most vulnerable and real characters on the show, especially in the moments he shares with Monica, or as his friendship with Joey evolves. Chandler is a beautiful soul in a bristly package, and it makes him all the more lovable.

Another character who likes to keep his emotions buried deep, Ron Swanson from Parks and Recreation, is the Director of his local Parks and Recreation department of government, which makes his unabashed contempt for all forms of politics and red tape all the more intriguing.

Ron Swanson is a simple man who enjoys simple things, and he has no respect for how government bodies are supposed to run, instead holding onto the job because he finds it to be an easy and low-effort role where almost nothing ever happens, that is until Leslie Knope sets her mind to opening a new park in Pawnee.

Nick Offerman is absolutely fantastic as Swanson, and the character remains a wonderful contradiction whose desire to not work is rivaled only by his intense loyalty to his friends and his commitment to supporting their dreams.

The late and great Catherine O'Hara left her mark on the TV landscape with her spectacular turn as Moira Rose, the matriarch of Schitt's Creek. While Moira is initially a hard woman to love or respect, and her self-absorbed and theatrical nature make her a bit of a pain, Moira's growth throughout the series is something to behold.

As the character begins to warm up to small-town living, and become someone that fans could engage with on a deeper level, it's hard to look back at those earlier moments without a tenderness and admiration for this character, who is ultimately on a journey of growth and understanding.

The 1950s sitcom, I Love Lucy, was a foundational entry in the genre that set the tone from which many other TV comedy shows would draw inspiration. Lucille Ball plays the titular lead, and brings a tremendous physical comedy that, when paired with Lucy's wacky schemes and overtly expressive face, makes her a character that is impossible not to fall in love with.

With that, the show earned its title, and the series continued to prove just how popular it was over six seasons. Today, I Love Lucy may not be the most familiar show for younger viewers, but anyone who picks up the series will ultimately find themselves captivated by the charm of Lucy Ricardo.

A few decades later, in 1985, The Golden Girls launched on TV and became an instant classic. And in no small part, that was thanks to the incredible ensemble cast that brought together four strong and vocal personalities who all had their own distinctive voice, and explored their wonderful maturing friendship.

Still, among these four charming women, Betty White's Rose Nylund stands out as one of the most lovable, thanks to her innocence, naïveté, and her ability to balance out the rest of the girls when things got heated. Rose is a literal treasure who remains one of the most beloved characters from TV, even all these decades later.

Rowan Atkinson struck gold in developing this silent character, Mr. Bean, who became a global sensation without ever saying a word. Employing a great deal of slapstick humor, intense facial expressions, and a certain innocence, Mr. Bean is a character that seems to transcend time and other barriers to engage with fanbases the world over.

He is a simple man, who sets out to complete simple tasks. However, due to his lack of awareness, and his clumsy nature, Mr. Bean often gets caught up in trouble that would never impact on typical people. But all of that simply makes him more engaging and adorable.

Andy Samberg's Jake Peralta is the embodiment of golden retriever energy in the form of an immature New York City detective. Jake is an eternally youthful and playful character who wants to get the best out of life, and is always ready to gamify his situation.

Jake also experiences profound personal growth over the course of the series, as he becomes more vulnerable and forms closer relationships with people like Captain Raymon Holt and Amy Santiago.

Community is a show that is brimming with colorful characters, but while the flaws for many of these individuals are initially overwhelming, Abed's (Danny Pudi) quirk is that he views everything through a meta lens and constantly references pop culture.

While everyone else around him embarks on a journey to become the best version of themselves, Abed is often the anchor that helps others to recognize where they are making mistakes, and course correct. He is intensely loyal, curious about human connection and boldly honest. All of which adds up to make him one of the best characters on TV.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

Kings send Nique Clifford to Thunder for draft assets: Trade grades

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The Sacramento Kings have agreed to trade guard Nique Clifford to the Oklahoma City Thunder in exchange for an unprotected 2028 second-round draft pick via Utah and an unprotected 2030 second-round draft pick via Minnesota, the team announced Sunday, Nov. 8.

Clifford, 24, is a 6-foot-5, 210-pound guard out of Colorado State that was originally selected by Oklahoma City with the 24th overall pick in the first round of the 2025 NBA Draft. However, Clifford never logged a minute in a Thunder uniform following a trade to the Kings that sent Oklahoma City a protected 2027 first-round pick.

He appeared in 75 games for Sacramento during the 2025-26 season and started 28. Clifford averaged 8.6 points, 3.8 rebounds and 2.4 assists in about 25.1 minutes during his rookie campaign with the Kings, while shooting 41.8% from the field and 33% on 3-pointers.

Clifford was being molded into a perennial "three-and-D" type of player for the Kings. He saw a lot of playing time in his rookie campaign, in part due to the numerous injuries Sacramento sustained to its roster that year.

He scored a career-high 30 points in a 132-126 loss to the Cleveland Cavaliers on Feb. 8. Throughout the 2025-26 season, Clifford also spent some time down in the G-League, playing for the Stockton Kings, the Kings affiliate.

In his collegiate years, Clifford averaged 10.1 points and just under a steal. He shot 46.9% from the field and 36.4% on 3-pointers.

Clifford signed a four-year rookie contract with Kings worth up to $15.7 million with $6.3 million guaranteed, according to Spotrac. Clifford's average annual salary is about $3.9 million.

He'll earn another $3.2 million in base salary.

Clifford appeared to be a contributing piece to the Kings' rebuild. The consummate professional, Clifford came into work, did what he was asked and pushed himself to be the best version of himself.

There was an expectation that Clifford would take a leap into his second-year but instead it came with setbacks. Clifford saw his minutes diminish early in the season. In his last game against the San Antonio Spurs on Friday, Nov. 6, Clifford only logged 4 minutes, 30 seconds and scored five points on 2-of-2 from the field.

He only played more than 10 minutes once this season – against the Memphis Grizzlies on Oct. 31. Clifford appeared in five of their nine games, earning four DNPs by coach's decision.

It appeared that Clifford was on the opposite end of head coach Doug Christie's rotations and a change was warranted. The Kings get to open a roster spot while picking up assets.

The Thunder have had misfortune early in the season, facing season-ending injuries to key rotation players, and additional injuries that will keep guys out long-term. Jalen Williams suffered a fractured left fibula and a dislocated ankle that required surgery. He's out for the year.

Cason Wallace has a torn labrum in his left shoulder and he is also out indefinitely. Chet Holmgren was ruled out 8 to 10 weeks after a biological treatment for a small patellar tendon tear in his right knee.

Oklahoma City needs bodies. Clifford was being buried on the bench early this season, the Thunder believe there's a spark that he can bring to to the team.

The organization has built a reputation of maximizing reserves and role players getting the best out of them, the latest project will be Clifford.

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Manchester United chiefs face a huge decision to make over the future of manager Michael Carrick, who had two games to save his job and has overseen two calamitous performances and two remarkable fightbacks. Sunday’s thrilling 4-4 draw with Aston Villa was dismal on the whole, but the players showed they are up for the fight.

The pressure has been mounting on Carrick following a disappointing start to the season, and the situation took a turn for the worse this weekend as, without kicking a ball, Man Utd slipped into the Premier League relegation zone after Nottingham Forest beat Manchester City.

Disaster starts to both the first half and second half against Aston Villa appeared to leave Carrick on the brink of the sack, but Patrick Dorgu’s 96th-minute equaliser could give club chiefs something to ponder heading into the international break. Follow all the latest Carrick sack news below!

A big day ahead for Michael Carrick and Manchester United.

We will be covering all the breaking news right here, so stick with us throughout Monday and the rest of the week.

Manchester United will hold a meeting at Carrington early next week to discuss Michael Carrick's future.

Acccording to TEAMtalk, director of football Jason Wilcox will hold the meeting and deciding Carrick’s fate will be top of the agenda.

United have assessed potential replacements, including Eddie Howe, Julian Nagelsmann, Antonio Conte and Unai Emery.

Wilcox is understood to have significant influence over the outcome, having previously backed Carrick.

The Athletic’s David Ornstein is unsure if Michael Carrick will be sacked by Manchester United.

Ornstein said on NBC Sports, before today’s game: “I think the truth here at Old Trafford is that they do want Michael Carrick to succeed.

“Think the truth is, there isn’t a firm plan here at Old Trafford for what to do if they were to make a change.

“No doubt they would’ve had conversations with representatives in recent weeks, and they’ll have more time to do so over the international break.

“A disastrous result today and maybe you think there will be a change, but as things stand, they’re continuing with Michael Carrick.

“I don’t know of a clear alternative plan.”

Manchester United legend has been on pundit duty for NBC Sports.

"Michael Carrick did that last season - so you can paper over the cracks and get a new manager bounce,” Neville told NBC Sports. "When the new manager comes in, everybody's happy for a couple of months and all of a sudden, six months later, it happens again.

"You can keep changing the manager and thinking that's going to be the answer. But this has been proven now over eight managers in 12 years, that's not the answer.

"Michael could leave, Michael might stay, we don't know, the ownership don't have a clue what to do.

"It's rinse and repeat. Amorim, Ten Hag, Ole Gunnar Solskjaer, Carrick, Jose Mourinho, Louis van Gaal, David Moyes, Ralf Rangnick - some of them are world class managers who have had success, there are a lot of promising young managers there, ex players who know the club as well.

"None of it has worked because if you recruit badly, if you've got poor performance department, poor recruitment department, you've not got leadership that is strong right through the spine of the football club you will fail.

“Every organisation in the world will fail if you've not got an aligned leadership from top to bottom - your board and your ownership right the way through to the person who is stood on the pitch trying to play football.

"There are real issues within that football club, you've got people doing those jobs who have never done those jobs before in senior positions."

Big movement in the betting markets, as Mauricio Pochettino is stormed into favouritism to replace Michael Carrick.

Carrick: “Would you? ... You’re telling me, what do you think?”

Carrick: “You know the situation, we haven’t won enough games.

“But I expect to move forward with things we’ve been working on, showing a lot of the good from today tighten up a few mistakes and win some football matches.”

The pair were both in attendance at Stamford Bridge last weekend and at Old Trafford on Tuesday, amid reports of being involved in discussions over potential replacements.

Michael Carrick has been speaking to BBC Sport.

“[We’re] making it hard for ourselves. Again, I thought a lot of the performances were very good. We made it challenging for ourselves. Mistakes that the boss knows shouldn't happen.

“We built our way back into the game and played well, we probably should score more than we did today. The goals are coming from individual mistakes. We need to tighten up.

"I thought we were the better team. We played some very good football and got back into the game. Two goals again put us on the back foot. In the end, we could have even won it.

“[At the end I thought] if we get one, then anything can happen. We never gave up and never stopped believing.

"That's the minimum you should be giving the supporters.

Javier Hernandez’s Passionate Man Utd Speech Goes Viral

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

With higher-ups Jason Wilcox and Omar Berrada under just as much pressure as Michael Carrick at Manchester United fans are convinced his former teammate Javier Hernandez is worthy of becoming a board member.

United rescued a point right at the death on Sunday after Patrick Dorgu headed home a crucial equaliser that may have saved Carrick's job. His side drew 4-4 against Aston Villa at Old Trafford and he has maintained that he is looking forward to rather than contemplating whether he'll still be in charge beyond that game.

Carrick's men produced another comeback just days after a remarkable turnaround against AS Roma, coming from behind to beat the Italians 3-2. Many of the club's legends have had plenty to say about the dire situation they find themselves in, sitting in the relegation zone in November.

Hernandez never experienced such tough times during his United career, instead winning two Premier League titles under Alex Ferguson. The Mexican striker still plays his football at the age of 38 for US outfit Atlantic Dallas but his motivational pre-match talk with Sky Sports hints at a future job in management or as a director.

After sharing the pitch with Carrick throughout his spell at Old Trafford, he gave an interesting perspective on the current state of the club. He explained why he is backing his former teammate to turn things around:

"I am confident, I am a believer that sometimes these decisions cannot be in your own way, I'm not saying that because I am in completely favour, I'm saying it in the world. I am more in favour of if a decision is taken already, instead of staying and complaining, go with it and let's see if it was right or not."

Hernandez recalled Carrick's success as interim coach: "Last season he did an amazing job, he brought United back to the Champions League. Now the start of the season wasn't like what we expected, we are used to normally when you have a really good season to maintain that, but football is like that."

Delving into the United boss' personality, he spoke highly of the characteristics that make Carrick a good fit: "I think Michael is the correct man. His personality helps a lot because he has a lot of composure and he's a very calm guy. I think he's the correct guy."

Hernandez particularly gave an inspiring assessment of what is going wrong and how the players need to take more responsibility for their own careers:

"The players need to perform over there, after this white line, when the whistle blows, there's nothing much more the manager can do. They can tell you defend more, run more, but putting on this Manchester United shirt, you shouldn't be looking at what is lacking, you need to be giving your life for this club. It can be one of your best or the best chapter in your career."

Hernandez spent five years at United and quickly became a fan favourite with the nickname 'Chicarito'. A cult hero, he scored 59 goals in 157 games and clearly still has a lot of affection for the club, disappointed to see Carrick's players not giving their all for the Old Trafford outfit.

United fans liked what they heard from Hernandez and have talked him up as a possible candidate for a high-ranking role with the club. That probably isn't something under his consideration right now given he's still playing out in the United States.

One fan pointed out his passion compared to today's coaches: "Chicharito delivered more fire through television than some managers."

Another supporter wants the Mexican to return: "Bring chicha back to united as an manager assistant or to the board!!"

One suggested he takes up a position upstairs: "Put him in that MUnited Board!"

Another feels he embodies what United is about: "ChichaGoat should be involved in the board, his mentality matches this club's spirit."

One added: "Roy Keane would love this lad."

While most of the talk following the draw against Villa is about Carrick and his future as United head coach, there is growing discontent in the fanbase about co-owners INEOS. Particularly sporting director Wilcox and CEO Berrada whose recruitment and direction for the club have raised alarm bells.

Putting a former player in a board member position is an idea that has been floated throughout the post-Sir Alex Ferguson era. There had been talk of Sheikh Jassim wanting the Class of 1992 to be part of his ownership of the club when he rivaled INEOS in a takeover race.

7 Sleek Sports Cars Ranked Worst To Best By Drag Coefficient

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Equipping a sports car with a big and powerful engine is all well and good. But if the car in question is designed like a cinder block, then it's just wasting the engine's potential. As a car speeds up, the air resistance around it increases. And a car with a sleeker design can slip through the air with less resistance, thus granting it better performance. That, in a very basic nutshell, is why aerodynamics matter.

Now, drag coefficient (Cd) isn't the be-all and end-all when it comes to measuring a car's aerodynamics as a whole. Something like drag area, which combines that metric with frontal area measurements, provides a more complete picture. Nevertheless, drag coefficient scores still provide a solid impression as to just how slippery a car is — and such figures are more readily available from automakers, too.

Sleek designs certainly aren't reserved exclusively for performance models; economy cars like Honda's little hybrid that could, the Insight, can also be marvelously slippery. But this article will be focusing on some of the sleekest sports and performance cars out from across the last century, covering old-school favorites such as the Tatra T87 with modern marvels like Volkswagen's XL1.

This Czechoslavkian masterpiece is a genuinely fascinating piece of design. Before moving onto the aerodynamic aspects of the T87, it's first worth covering what else makes it such an intriguing automobile. Motivation is sourced from a rear-engined 2.9-liter V8, which kicked out an impressive-for-the-era 85 horsepower. Back in the '40s, that was a serious amount of power to be dealing with, and Tatra ensured its effectiveness by complimenting it with a seriously slippery design.

German Zeppelins provided plenty of influence behind the T87's iconic shape, and the end result was a drag coefficient of just 0.36 Cd. This enabled the 85-horse V8 to propel the T87 to an impressive 100 mph, although the handling characteristics of a rear-engined machine with independent swing-axle suspension resulted in a fair number of accidents. Rumor has it that, due to so many Nazi officers meeting their maker in T87 accidents, the car was actually banned for use – but the accuracy of these claims has been contested over the years. Still, it all adds to the fascinating history of Tatra's aerodynamic masterpiece.

Production spanned from 1936 to 1950, albeit with a gap during the war years. Despite such a wide production window, though, just over 3,000 T87s were ever built. It proved to be a significant influence to Ferdinand Porsche, and today, it's a cherished classic that attracts collectors willing to pay over $100,000.

The Huayra replaced the awesome Zonda in 2011, and it boasted a design that was just as striking. It was a million-dollar masterpiece at launch, packing a 720-horsepower twin-turbocharged 6.0-liter V12, capable of propelling it along to a top speed of 224 mph. Aerodynamic trickery helps the Italian to remain stable at speed, with front- and rear-mounted flaps that rise and fall to alter downforce levels.

It's important to remember that a supercar like this needs to be comfortable performing through the corners as well as the straight lines. Here's where that active aero really proves its relevancy. Those four flaps allow the Huayra to transform itself depending on the given requirements. While flat, the Huayra boasts a drag coefficient of just 0.31 Cd (as proven by a Mercedes wind tunnel). But they can also flip up to assist the car with braking and keep it planted through the corners.

Much like Pagani did with the Zonda, as the Huayra began to age, the automaker began to produce numerous special editions. Among them was a rolling $7.3 million air piece dubbed the Codalunga. The svelte model sports various ducts and diffusers, plus a longtail design – which is a known method of reducing the amount of drag a car's body produces. However, the Codalunga has not been individually tested in a wind tunnel, so whether it beats the standard Huayra's 0.31 Cd isn't officially known.

While on the topic of longtail designs, it seems appropriate to mention the McLaren Speedtail. This car categorically proves the effectiveness of such a design, with a drag coefficient of just under 0.29 Cd. The British firm from Woking knows a thing or two about churning out slippery supercars, but this Speedtail is something of a caricature; it sits 3 feet longer than the F1, and it's even longer than the F1 GTR Longtail. Helping it cut through the air as precisely as possible is a narrow frontal area, numerous concealed ducts, a complete lack of rear-view mirrors, and front wheels that are almost entirely enclosed.

Of course, a sleek design still needs to be complimented with a mighty power train if impressive performance is the end goal. And with a turbocharged 4.0-liter V8 that combines its efforts with a single electric motor to produce 1,035 horsepower, the Speedtail recipe is certainly a comprehensive one. Combined with a dry weight of just 3,153 pounds, that's enough to see the Speedtail achieve a top speed of 250 mph. Plus, it'll crack 186 mph in just shy of half the time required by its forefather, the mighty F1.

As impressive as the McLaren Speedtail's design is, what's arguably far more impressive is that a $44,245 Corvette from 2005 can match the $2.25 million British hypercar's drag coefficient. With a drag coefficient of just 0.28 Cd, the C6 Chevy is surprisingly slippery. This wasn't a one-hit-wonder for Chevrolet, either, as previous generations had also proven to be remarkably aerodynamic as well. But the C6 manages to outdo them all (albeit only marginally). The C4 boasted a drag coefficient of 0.34 Cd, while the C5 sat at 0.29 Cd. It's worth noting that later generations – including the current C8 — also sit behind the C6's rating.

It's no wonder that people think the C6 is still the best Corvette to date. Regardless of whether you agree or not, it's hard to find real fault with the C6. It combined a brilliantly efficient design with supercar-rivaling performance; the most powerful iteration was the 638-horsepower ZR1, which could top 200 mph. Even the base models were serious performers thanks to an LS1 lurking beneath that low-slung hood. These cars were affordable when new, and they're still great bang-for-buck choices today, with entry-level coupes and convertibles both available for sub-$25,000 – proving that slippery sports cars don't exclusively belong in six-figure price brackets.

Electric cars and hybrids pay a lot of attention to aerodynamics, as being able to slip through the air with minimal resistance is a particularly effective way of increasing a vehicle's range. That's led to buyers of electrified vehicles becoming understandably obsessed with realistic and effective ranges. It should come as no surprise, then, that BMW's bold hybridized i8 sports a drag coefficient of just 0.26 Cd.

That drag coefficient proves that the i8's striking design isn't all about stealing the attention of passers by, in fact, it's really quite clever. The various slices and slashes between those layered body panels help to direct airflow into the car's various radiators, which allows the i8 to disregard active aero. Even the cockpit itself is teardrop-shaped to reduce drag, while the flared rear pillars help keep the visuals sweet without damaging any aerodynamic benefits.

With a turbocharged 1.5-liter inline-three engine and single electric motor tackling the task of motivation, the i8's power train recipe could not be more different from that of the aforementioned Corvette. However, just like the C6, the BMW i8 now finds itself at a very tempting price point, with early examples now changing hands for sub-$40,000.

This one's probably something of a surprise, as unlike so many of the other mentions on this list, the Nissan GT-R doesn't exactly look immensely slippery. It's not particularly low-slung, it doesn't incorporate an obvious longtail design, nothing else about it is clearly geared toward aerodynamics — plus, there's a great big spoiler adorning the trunk. However, that hasn't stopped the GT-R from being a technological marvel with a drag coefficient of only 0.26 Cd. Yep, this chunky model with heaps of boost and gaping grills matches the rating of BMW's slippery little eco-minded coupe.

The secrets of the GT-R low drag coefficient are actually somewhat hidden. It sports an almost completely flat floor, a functional diffuser which helps to channel airflow efficiency, and a slightly tapered rear overhang. Also helping to direct the air in an effective manner are what Nissan describes as "aero-blades" adorning the front fenders, while the narrow fender vents help to provide valuable downforce at the front, too.

Throughout its impressive 18-year tenure, the R35 GT-R saw the power from its twin-turbocharged 3.8-liter V6 rise to genuine supercar levels. And the clever aero tweaks it received over those years helped drivers to make the most of that strength. It came incredibly close to cracking the 7-minute barrier on the Nurburgring, peaking with a time of 7:08.679 in its 2013 Nismo guise — breaking the world record for fastest volume production car in the process.

All of the cars featured upon this list are aerodynamically impressive, and they certainly sit a long way removed from the least aerodynamic cars ever made. However, the Volkswagen XL1 stands in a class of its own. The sole purpose of this striking little hybridized-diesel inline-three-powered sports car was to be as mightily efficient as possible.

The headline number here is a drag coefficient of just 0.19 Cd. To get this number down so far, it combines many of the other engineering techniques used by other cars on this list. It very obviously sports a teardrop profile like the i8, allowing the smoothest possible path for airflow. Like the GT-R, the car's floor is completely flat, preventing the underside from pushing against the wind. And — going above and beyond the Speedtail in this regard – covered rear wheels assist the XL1 in achieving its remarkably low drag coefficient.

It wasn't just aerodynamic efficiency that Volkswagen was chasing with the XL1. It was also designed to be immensely frugal on fuel, to the point where a Honda Insight or Toyota Prius might look like a big-block Chevy from the '70s in comparison. On the European combined test cycle, Volkswagen managed to achieve a frankly mind-boggling 313 mpg, a feat helped by the model's incredibly low curb weight (courtesy of magnesium wheels and a carbon-fiber monocoque chassis).

Man City Signed the ‘English Messi’ - He Made 3 Appearances in 6 Years

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Eleven years after being touted as the 'English Lionel Messi' and subsequently being signed by Manchester City for £12 million, Patrick Roberts is plying his trade in the Championship for Birmingham City.

Born in Kingston upon Thames in south-west London, Roberts joined Fulham's academy at the age of 13. After making his debut for the Cottagers at 17, City opted to sign the wide man for £12 million in the summer of 2015.

He went on to make three appearances for the Citizens' first team, before a succession of underwhelming loan spells saw him sold to Sunderland in 2022. After four years on Wearside, Roberts swapped the Premier League for the second tier again, joining Birmingham permanently in the summer.

He's now an important figure in Chris Davies' team, who are vying for promotion back to the big time. From being tipped to lead City to major honours to being compared to Messi, it all could have been so different for Roberts.

After making waves in Fulham's academy, Felix Magath called Roberts up to a matchday squad for a Premier League fixture in March 2014, when the winger was just 17. He'd just signed his first professional contract, and would go on to make his senior debut a week later against the club he'd eventually sign for, Manchester City.

Introduced as a 55th-minute substitute against the north-west outfit, Magath showered the then teenager with praise after this surprise inclusion, describing the player as an 'extraordinary talent' (via the Mirror).

Naturally, these complimentary words sparked excitement amongst Fulham fans about the level of talent they might have on their hands.

Roberts would go on to make 17 appearances for the West Londoners in the Championship in the following season. Despite failing to score in any of these outings, City bought into the hype around the starlet, forking out £12 million to bring him to the Etihad in the summer of 2015.

It was at this point that Roberts' nickname in the Fulham academy became common knowledge. His peers in the youth ranks at Craven Cottage had called him 'Mini Messi', which led to many branding him the 'English Messi' when he signed for the Citizens (via the Mirror).

As well as being tipped for greatness by Messi, Roberts himself referenced his likeness to the Argentine, citing the legendary playmaker as his idol in February 2016 (via PlanetFootball).

"It’s a conscious thing for me to be like Messi," he said.

"I’ve watched him for years and he’s my idol. You try to copy what he does, and it also comes naturally to me as well. Because I’m left-footed, I tend to move the same way as he does. In games, I try to replicate what he does. I’ve studied him on YouTube and when La Liga is on I watch that all the time. My view is that you watch the best to learn from the best."

It's fair to say that Roberts didn't live up to this tag. After spending six months on the periphery of Manuel Pellegrini's squad, making just three appearances for the Premier League giants, he was shipped out to Celtic on loan.

This sparked a series of temporary stints, which lasted seven years and included stops at Girona, Norwich City, Middlesbrough, Derby County and Troyes, before he finally parted ways with City in 2022.

While Roberts hasn't lived up to the potential he once promised, it would be unfair to dismiss his career as a failure. Most players who are compared to some of the game's greatest players when they are young don't get close to replicating their idol's accomplishments, and the ex-City prodigy has certainly achieved a lot more than most.

Finally settling at Sunderland in 2022, after enjoying an impressive loan spell with the Black Cats in the back half of the 2021/22 campaign, Roberts shone for the north-east club for several years in the Championship.

Lamine Yamal may have won, but some former Ballon d'Or winners didn't even include him in their top three.

He made 147 appearances on Wearside, playing a key role in their promotion charge in the 2024/25 season. The former wonderkid was then granted a solitary Premier League appearance for Regis Le Bris' side before making the switch to Birmingham.

Since joining the Blues, he's scored four goals and established himself as a core member in an improving team that is now looking to return to the top flight for the first time in 15 years.

'Zlatan Ibrahimovic Ended My England Career in 10 Minutes - I Never Played Again'

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

While plenty of attention is devoted to Zlatan Ibrahimovic's character and his larger-than-life personality, his ego wouldn't have been nearly as effective if he hadn't also been one of the best players on the planet during the prime of his career. The Swede was a phenomenon in front of goal, ranking among the greatest strikers in football history, and wherever he played, he rarely struggled to find the back of the net.

Ibrahimovic represented some of the biggest teams in world football throughout his career and ranks among the most decorated footballers of all time when it comes to trophies won. He played for Barcelona, Inter Milan, AC Milan, Paris Saint-Germain and Manchester United, alongside several other notable clubs. His imposing frame was matched by remarkable agility and speed, meaning there were few defenders in the beautiful game capable of keeping him quiet for an entire match. Having retired in 2023, the striker finished with 949 appearances for club and country, scoring 558 goals in the process.

Facing the superstar during his prime could have serious consequences for a player's career too, as one England centre-back discovered when his international career was effectively brought to an end after going up against Ibrahimovic.

Zlatan Ibrahimovic says his former Manchester United teammate deserves credit for Cristiano Ronaldo’s success at Old Trafford.

After several seasons impressing for Stoke City in the Premier League, Ryan Shawcross received his first-ever call-up to the England team in 2012. Supporters had been pushing for the defender to get an opportunity with the national side for some time and he was finally handed his chance, but his debut came against Sweden. He was tasked with trying to deal with Ibrahimovic and, by his own admission, things didn't go according to plan. In fact, the incident turned him into a source of jokes within his family. Via quotes shared by the Telegraph, Shawcross said:

"I’m the butt of the joke in my family, just because the 10 minutes I had in international football was ruined by one man. They are consistently saying that Zlatan ruined my career! I had a chance and I don’t think many other defenders have stopped him. I was given 10 minutes to show what I could do and probably didn’t provide a good enough performance. I was against one of the best strikers in the world who was on a hot streak at the time and these things happen."

The centre-back was able to look back on the episode with good humour at the time, but he never earned another England appearance for the rest of his career. That was unfortunate, considering he enjoyed a strong spell in the Premier League and had arguably done enough to deserve another opportunity.

Zlatan Ibrahimovic previously had tattoos on his body that are no longer there - here's the story behind it.

He never managed to join one of England's biggest clubs, but for much of his career, Shawcross was a top-level defender who held his own against some of the Premier League's finest strikers. After progressing through the Manchester United academy, he spent time on loan with Royal Antwerp and Stoke before eventually making his move to the latter permanent. The Potters were playing in the Championship at the time, but they soon secured promotion to the top flight with Shawcross at the centre of their defence.

From there, he helped Stoke establish themselves as a midtable Premier League side, with the club remaining in the top flight for 10 seasons before eventually being relegated to the Championship. Throughout his Premier League spell, Shawcross was highly impressive and attracted interest from several clubs over the years. No move ultimately materialised, however, and he stayed with Stoke for 13 years.

Across that period, the centre-back made 453 appearances for the Potters, including 317 in the top flight. He was a hugely important servant to the club, but departed in 2021 to join Inter Miami. His stay there lasted just one season before he retired, and he never represented England again. It's fair to say that Ibrahimovic may have played a role in that.

Hamilton says he overrode Ferrari pit call due to 'lack of information'

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Lewis Hamilton finished ninth in the Sao Paulo Grand Prix as Ferrari endured a disappointing weekend in Brazil; Hamilton lost out late in the race after ignoring a call to come into the pits to switch from slick to intermediate tyres as rain intensified

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

Lewis Hamilton says a lack of information led to him overriding Ferrari's call for him to come into the pits as rain fell during the closing stages of the Sao Paulo Grand Prix.

Hamilton was running in eighth at Interlagos when heavy rain began to fall with 10 laps to go. While the leaders had already passed the pit entry by the time the rain intensified, Mercedes brought in George Russell from fifth.

Ferrari instructed Hamilton to come in, but he decided to do the opposite to the car just in front of him at the time, Racing Bulls' Liam Lawson. Hamilton's team-mate Charles Leclerc, running just behind him, came into the pits.

The seven-time world champion spun on the next lap in the treacherous conditions, before pitting at the end of it to follow his rivals onto intermediates.

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

While there was little at stake, as Hamilton ultimately finished ninth rather than claiming what could have been seventh if he had got past Lawson, it was the latest example of imperfect execution from the British driver and his Italia team.

Speaking to Sky Italy after the race, Hamilton said: "The last bit of information I got was that the rain was going to stay the same, and in those conditions my grip was feeling pretty good.

"And I was like, 'ah, I'm catching.' And I was coming out the last corner, just before the pit lane they asked me to box, and I was right behind Lawson.

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

"He went in, and I ended up staying (out). They didn't say it's raining more in the first half of the lap. If I'd have known that I would have come in.

"As soon as I got to Turn 1, I realised it was wet. So, it just lacked a little bit of information there.

"And ultimately, that's on me. Sorry for the team, for that. I gave it everything. We just didn't have the pace today unfortunately."

Hamilton had come into the weekend off the back of a major controversy with his team-mate in Mexico last weekend, as he was angered by a first-lap clash between the pair that he felt cost him victory and the team a one-two.

While Hamilton revealed on Saturday that the pair had "squashed" their issues, the precious nature of their winning opportunity in Mexico City was highlighted by their struggles in Brazil.

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

Both Hamilton and Leclerc were compromised by grid penalties that saw them start from 20th and 14th, respectively, but neither appeared to have the pace to match McLaren's race winner Lando Norris or Red Bull's Max Verstappen in second.

Hamilton told Sky Sports F1: "Really tough day and weekend for the whole team. They have done a really great job.

"All the mechanics did such a great job switching out the engine and making sure the car was as good as it could be."

Hamilton had a great start as he climbed to ninth on the first lap, and successfully pulled off being the first driver to switch from intermediates to slicks as the track dried early in the race, but didn't have the pace to take advantage of either moment.

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

He added: "We struggled in the dry but I had a great start. Unfortunately, the rear tyres just ballooned and overheated and I struggled.

"After that, I was pushing to go onto the slicks, which I eventually got on, I think I was one of the earlier ones [to switch to that tyre].

"I was feeling pretty good. It was very tricky in those conditions. We ended up having an extra stop, which ultimately, I had a lot of ground to cover there."

Grimsby Town vs Nottingham Forest Under-21s - prediction, team news, lineups

Grimsby Town and Nottingham Forest Under-21s meet at Blundell Park on Tuesday night knowing that only a win will do to remain in the hunt for qualification for the EFL Trophy knockout stages.

That is despite neither team posting a victory in their opening two games in Group F of the Northern Section.

After a few hours in front of the television cameras on against Stafford Rangers on Sunday, a game against Under-21 opposition two days later is somewhat of a comedown for Grimsby.

Although they trailed the eighth-tier side 2-0 and 3-2 in their absorbing FA Cup first round tie, recording a 3-3 draw before prevailing on penalties ensured that David Artell's side continued their recent upturn in form, which was needed after a run of five defeats in six matches across all competitions.

In three League Two fixtures against Rochdale, Gillingham and York City, the Mariners have racked up seven points and 11 goals.

The results from that triple-header have catapulted Grimsby into sixth place in the League Two table.

Despite sitting bottom of this group, Grimsby can qualify for the next round of the EFL Trophy with a win, providing that Burton Albion lose to Notts County in another game that is taking place on Tuesday night.

Nottingham Forest Under-21s find themselves in a similar position where a win could lead to progress, despite their 5-1 defeat at Notts County on October 13.

Earning a penalty-shootout win at Burton after a 1-1 draw has kept them in contention to earn a surprise place in the knockout stages.

Forest have found some form in recent weeks, including registering a 4-3 win at Halifax FC in the National League Cup.

In Premier League 2, they sit in sixth place in the standings with 13 points from seven games, their last outing ending in a 2-2 draw with Leicester City.

Nottingham Forest Under-21s form (all competitions):

The absences of Doug Tharme and Edon Pruti due to injury may lead to at least one academy player starting in defence.

David Modupe is expected to miss out for Nottingham Forest Under-21s after suffering an early injury against Leicester on Friday.

Matthew Orr should deputise in central defence. Meanwhile, Fuad Smith has scored five goals in his last five appearances for the Under-21s.

With Grimsby having minimal rest after the FA Cup, it remains to be seen how they may line up. That said, we would still expect Artell's side to come through with three points, even if their young opponents push them hard.

Michael Carrick answers Man Utd quit question as crisis hits alarming new low

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Michael Carrick insisted he would not walk away from Manchester United after Sunday’s wild 4-4 draw with Aston Villa ensured his side would spend the international window languishing in the relegation zone.

Stoppage-time goals from Joshua Zirkzee and Patrick Dorgu saw United rally for an unlikely point on a day when they started both halves by allowing Villa to storm into two-goal leads before coming back.

The point means United sit 18th in the table, doing little to ease the intense scrutiny on Carrick given they are now eight league games without a win – a club record in the Premier League era – with their only victory of the season back in August against newly-promoted Ipswich.

Read more: Haissem Hassan Celtic absence explained as ‘manoeuvring’ costs winger

Asked on Sky Sports if he would quit if he felt it was the right thing to do for the club, Carrick said: “That’s not going to happen.”

Then, speaking in his post-match press conference, Carrick expressed confidence his side could still turn things around this season.

“One hundred per cent,” he said. “I think there’s more than enough. Goals change games and we’ve got to deal with it and it’s something we have to improve on drastically. But there’s more than enough, we’re just making it too difficult for ourselves at the moment in certain times…

“I’m quite clear with what direction the club needs to go in. We’ve just got to win games.”

United were 2-0 down inside five minutes as Alysson and Nicolas Jackson pounced on defensive errors, but Bruno Fernandes got one back from the penalty spot before Youri Tielemans scored against his former club.

Having worked so hard to get level, United conceded twice in the opening seven minutes of the second half to John McGinn’s penalty and Leon Goretzka’s first Villa goal, with many fans having left before their second comeback of the afternoon.

“Football is about moments, football is about what happens in both boxes in the end,” Carrick added.

“Take (away) the moments and the totally avoidable goals we’ve given away, I actually thought we played pretty well, we were the better team for the most part and I’m disappointed we haven’t won the game.”

Unai Emery, among the candidates touted as a potential successor to Carrick, was visibly frustrated at conceding the late equaliser.

“The referee added six minutes, I think the match was done…” Emery said. “I am angry of course. How to accept to lose in the last six minutes with two goals is difficult…

“I’m angry not with the players, angry with the situation. You are winning in the last six minutes, winning in extra time, and we lost the opportunity to have two points more.”

Dorgu headed in from a corner in a crowded penalty box and Emery admitted goalkeeper Zion Suzuki, hugely impressive since joining this summer, still has work to do.

“It is a process, it’s a good example when you concede, it’s (about) adaptation to the Premier League for Zion,” he added.

“They are pushing, they are disturbing the goalkeeper. In some countries, in other leagues, they are not permitting it.

“Here yes, and we have to continue analysing, having experiences and trying to avoid these situations.”

10 NFL trades that need to happen: Derrick Henry to Patriots, Budda Baker to Bills among options

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

The NFL trade deadline is just two days away, and it's already been a dramatic time around the league. The Seattle Seahawks beefed up their offensive line with Quenton Nelson, the San Francisco 49ers brought back DeForest Buckner, the Chicago Bears look like a Super Bowl favorite with the addition of Jeffery Simmons, and the Baltimore Ravens have a new quarterback in Jacoby Brissett. Who is the next domino to fall?

A total of 27 players were traded in-season last year. That's the most in the past 25 years. A total of 12 players were dealt on deadline day last year. Get ready for a wild 48 hours. There's a lot of speculation around Indianapolis Colts running back Jonathan Taylor, but will he actually get dealt? What about the Minnesota Vikings? Could they swing a deal for a new quarterback in the next 48 hours? Below, we will break down 10 trades that need to happen.

Vikings receive: QB Kirk CousinsRaiders receive: Conditional fifth-round pick

ESPN's Adam Schefter got a lot of people excited last week when he said he was going to keep an eye on the Vikings. Are they going to trade for Justin Herbert? Or Mac Jones? Maybe Daniel Jones? What about bringing back Kirk Cousins?

It doesn't appear that Cousins will be dealt at the trade deadline, but it would be a storybook ending if Kevin O'Connell brought Cousins back to town and he contributed to a Super Bowl run. Cousins spent six seasons in Minnesota, threw for 4,000 yards four times and went 50-37-1 as the starter. While he's 38 now, Cousins is still capable of playing good football. His 19 passing touchdowns entering Sunday ranked second in the NFL behind Joe Burrow, and his 265.5 passing yards per game ranked seventh.

Now that we are halfway through the 2026 season, the Raiders can turn their attention to No. 1 overall pick Fernando Mendoza, while Cousins will improve a Vikings passing attack that's sputtering. Kyler Murray's 170.3 passing yards per game entering Week 9 ranked 30th among 31 qualifying passers, and we don't know if Carson Wentz is a solution. Justin Jefferson is sick of not having consistent play under center, so let's get Cousins back to Minnesota and watch him power a Super Bowl run.

Bills receive: S Budda BakerCardinals receive: Third-round pick

How long can the Bills keep winning track meets? Josh Allen can outscore just about any team, but this defense ranks bottom 10 in both yards allowed per game (352.1) and points allowed per game (25.9). Buffalo has four wins in games the defense has allowed 30 points!

Adding Cardinals safety Budda Baker should help. He's an eight-time Pro Bowler playing great football for a not-so-great team. In eight games this season, he's recorded 51 tackles, four passes defensed, one interception and a career-high 3.5 sacks. Not many players have made 1,000 tackles before age 30 — just Bobby Wagner, Roquan Smith, Luke Kuechly, Lavonte David, Ray Lewis, Fred Warner and Budda Baker.

Patriots receive: RB Derrick HenryRavens receive: Third-round pick

Mike Vrabel has a few former Titans on his roster, including A.J. Brown and Kevin Byard. He could swing a deal for another one of his former players in Derrick Henry.

The Ravens running back is averaging 76.6 rushing yards per game and has scored nine total touchdowns in nine games played. Given the injuries at the quarterback position and the fact that the Ravens are the only team in the division with a losing record, it looks like Baltimore won't be making its Super Bowl run this year. Henry is 32 going on 33 and should be helping a contender.

The Patriots rank 12th in rushing yards per game with 123.1, but Rhamondre Stevenson is averaging under four yards per attempt and about 46 yards per game. TreVeyon Henderson's situation remains unclear as well, after he revealed he experienced an "unusual medical emergency." Vrabel knows what Henry is capable of as a bell cow, and he can help the reigning AFC champs get back to the "Big Game."

Raiders receive: WR George PickensCowboys receive: Second-round pick

George Pickens ranked third in the NFL in receiving yards last season with 1,429 and nine touchdowns on 93 receptions, but this season has been a different story. His yards per game has dipped from 84.1 to 66.6, and Pickens played just 56.8% of snaps during the Cowboys' Week 8 loss to Arizona.

Pickens is playing on the franchise tag this year and is due a massive contract. Questions remain about the Cowboys' ability to pay him. Dak Prescott is the No. 2 highest-paid quarterback in the NFL, CeeDee Lamb is the No. 6 highest-paid wide receiver in the NFL and Tyler Smith is the No. 2 highest-paid guard in the league. Then, the recently added Joey Porter Jr. is due a massive contract as well. Is Dallas really going to pay Pickens around $35 million per year? It makes sense to pick up a second-round pick for a player who may not be on your roster next year.

The Raiders, on the other hand, have one of the worst wide receiver rooms in the NFL.Tre Tucker leads the way with 474 yards, then Jalen Nailor is behind him with 113 yards. Pickens would provide Fernando Mendoza with a true perimeter threat for years to come.

Broncos receive: RB Breece HallJets receive: Third-round pick

Breece Hall signed a three-year, $43.5 million deal this offseason after being slapped with the franchise tag. He became the Jets' first 1,000-yard rusher since 2015 and is one of three active players to record 3,000 rushing yards and 1,500 receiving yards in his first four seasons. However, Hall's quad injury opened the door for Braelon Allen, who has really taken advantage of his opportunity. He rushed for a career-high 147 yards against the Raiders last week.

The Broncos' rushing offense entered Sunday ranked seventh-worst in the NFL. While rookie Jonah Coleman has flashed in spots and the injured RJ Harvey is a weapon as a receiver out of the backfield, Hall would immediately upgrade an inconsistent offense that has legitimate Super Bowl aspirations.

Chiefs receive: WR Josh DownsColts receive: Conditional fourth-round pick

The Chiefs would like Brian Thomas Jr., but they don't want to give up a second-round pick for a player with some question marks. They instead trade a conditional fourth-round pick for Josh Downs, who has recorded the second-most receiving yards out of the slot ever since he was selected in the third round of the 2023 NFL Draft out of North Carolina.

Downs caught 30 passes for 330 yards and one touchdown in his first eight games this season and put up a career-high 803 yards and five trips to the end zone in 2024. The Chiefs' issues at wide receiver are well-documented. Rashee Rice entered Sunday averaging 48.6 receiving yards per game, and Xavier Worthy is just a gadget player at this point. Travis Kelce shouldn't be leading this team in receiving.

Steelers receive: WR Ladd McConkey, fifth-round pickChargers receive: WR Roman Wilson, second-round pick

CBS Sports' Bryan DeArdo pitched this trade earlier this week, and it makes some sense. Ladd McConkey could take the Steelers' passing attack to another level, while former Michigan head coach Jim Harbaugh would reunite with Roman Wilson.

McConkey has caught just 17 passes for 286 yards and one touchdown through the first eight weeks of the season. As a rookie, he caught 82 passes for 1,149 yards and seven touchdowns. The Steelers have averaged 26.2 points per game over their last six, but their leading receiver, DK Metcalf, is averaging just 47.3 yards per game. In McConkey, Aaron Rodgers would get a solid slot weapon who could also act as a security blanket.

Giants receive: RB Tony PollardTitans receive: Fourth-round pick

Jonathan Taylor has gotten a lot of oxygen over the past few weeks, but there's another running back in the AFC South that teams should be interested in. Tony Pollard rushed for 129 yards and three touchdowns against the Cleveland Browns in Week 7 and then 117 yards and two touchdowns against the Cincinnati Bengals in Week 8. He's averaging a career-high 74.4 rushing yards per game this season, and he ranks top 10 in rushing attempts, rushing yards and rushing touchdowns. He and Derrick Henry are the only players to have rushed for 1,000 yards in each of the last four seasons.

The Giants do have Cam Skattebo, who rushed for 124 yards and a touchdown in that overtime win against the Houston Texans, but Pollard would upgrade a questionable room as New York looks to win the division. John Harbaugh was upset his team couldn't land Jeffery Simmons. Mike Borgonzi finally answers the phone and sends Pollard to the Giants.

Jaguars receive: DT Moro OjomoEagles receive: Third-round pick

The Jaguars have reportedly been sniffing around for defensive linemen, and Moro Ojomo could be a fit. Jacksonville just saw DaVon Hamilton go down with a torn quad, and GM James Gladstone hopes to make an addition to maintain one of the best run defenses in the NFL. Ojomo adds some juice as a pass rusher from the interior and has recorded the second-most pressures on the Eagles over the last three seasons behind Jalen Carter.

49ers receive: EDGE Khalil Mack, sixth-round pickChargers receive: Third-round pick

Reports indicate that Khalil Mack won't be traded, but this is a player who should be rushing the passer for a contender. The former Defensive Player of the Year turns 36 in a few months, but he can still get after the quarterback. Mack recorded three sacks in his first seven games this season and notched a career-high 17 sacks just three years ago.

The 49ers have the most games missed due to injury this season (142) entering Sunday. The defensive line has 47 games missed by players, the most in the NFL. Mack would help a Raheem Morris defense that can win a Super Bowl if healthy.

Serie A | Fiorentina 2-2 Juventus: Old Lady fight back in Florence thriller

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Juventus fought back twice with Nick Woltemade and Gleison Bremer to grab a point away to Fiorentina, who had been leading through Mateo Pellegrino and Alieu Njie.

Luciano Spalletti hoped the Juve crisis was behind him after back-to-back wins over Napoli and AZ Alkmaar, despite a growing injury crisis. Weston McKennie and Zeki Celik returned to the squad, with Kenan Yildiz, Andrea Cambiaso, Nico Gonzalez, Manuel Locatelli, Khephren Thuram and Kamil Grabara out, but a new trident was launched with Jeremie Boga, Nick Woltemade and Randal Kolo Muani.

The Viola finally got themselves back on track with a 4-2 victory away to Sassuolo on Monday, after scraping two points from five rounds. Marin Pongracic, Luca Ranieri, Fabiano Parisi and Arthur Atta were still on the treatment table, but Pedro Goncalves and Christ Inao Oulai were preferred to Nicolò Fagioli.

It was a lightning start to the game, as Pedro Goncalves floated a corner kick to the back post for the Mateo Pellegrino free header to give Fiorentina the early lead. The Argentine pulled out the old Gabriel Batistuta celebration in front of the home fans.

McKennie tried to replicate it at the other end with a header inches wide from a corner, then David De Gea scrambled across for a one-handed save on the Juan Cabal chest and volley from the edge of the penalty area.

Juventus did equalise when McKennie sent Randal Kolo Muani down the right, he turned Viery inside out to roll across for Woltemade to sweep in first-time from 10 yards with the inside of the right boot. After three goals in the Europa League, this was his first in Serie A.

Kolo Muani was wreaking havoc down the right against Viery, again pulling back for Woltemade, but this time De Gea made a big save and Teun Koopmeiners’ follow-up was deflected wide.

Cabal also stung De Gea’s gloves from distance and a Kolo Muani effort was charged down in the box by a defender whose hands were clamped behind his back, while Cher Ndour drilled wide and Pedro Goncalves failed to make the most of Jhon Lucumi error playing out from defence, as he too slipped at the crucial moment.

Fiorentina restored their advantage when Alex Jimenez saw his low cross-shot from the right palmed into a crowd by Guglielmo Vicario, with Alieu Njie quicker to react than the Juve defenders to bundle over the line. It was the Swede’s third consecutive game on target.

Franco Mastantuono nearly added another moments later, cutting inside to drill just wide of the far post, but Zeki Celik came off the bench and immediately pulled back from the by-line with no teammates ready to finish it off, then Douglas Luiz couldn’t keep the header down on an Edon Zhegrova cross.

Juventus clawed their way back into it again from a Douglas Luiz corner, was De Gea was hesitant and Gleison Bremer anticipated Radu Dragusin to turn in from close range with a combination of head and shoulder.

Penalty appeals were rejected for an awkward bouncing ball that struck Joao Mario on the arm, while Fiorentina were also angered when Dragusin nodded in a Mandragora corner at the 82nd minute, but it was disallowed for his push on Bremer.

Marco Brescianini’s scorcher in stoppages was parried by Vicario, with Bremer clearing before Willie Gnonto could pounce, then the Juve goalkeeper also kept out Mastantuono’s solo effort at the base of the near post.

Brahim Diaz benched for 11th Real Madrid game in a row, Morocco worries grow

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

By Alexis Pereira | 08 Nov 2026 21:37

Brahim Diaz has started on the bench for the 11th consecutive game for Real Madrid in all competitions, and Moroccan supporters are growing concerned by the lack of playing time and by the forward’s apparent passivity about it.

Diaz was a substitute on Saturday against Valencia in La Liga and came on in the 84th minute with the result all but decided, Real Madrid winning 3-0 with the last goal coming in the 75th minute.

Beyond the token minutes, it is the way the 27-year-old is being treated that angers his compatriots. Diaz, who has 34 caps and 15 goals for Morocco, is one of the key figures in the national team, and his apparent acceptance of the situation is what frustrates observers.

The versatile forward has been kept out on the right by Arda Guler or summer signing Yan Diomande, who are six and seven years his junior respectively.

‘Maybe it is time to have a bit of pride and leave this club,’ suggested an insider named Youssef, said to be close to the Royal Moroccan Football Federation (FRMF). Despite Diaz’s undeniable talent, he fears the player could struggle with fatigue during a tournament through a lack of match fitness.

The former Manchester City player gives the impression that he puts his club above all other considerations and that nothing compares with wearing the white shirt, which has sometimes led to accusations that he undervalues his national team in comparison with Real Madrid.

Signed from Manchester City in January 2019 and then loaned to AC Milan for three seasons, Diaz returned to Real Madrid permanently in the summer of 2023 with a firm intention to establish himself.

He was already limited to a substitute role under Carlo Ancelotti and Xabi Alonso before the Jose Mourinho era, but he played an active part in the club’s Champions League triumph two years ago as a luxury super-sub.
