54 min: Arsenal have started this half much like they started the first. They just need to maintain this intensity.

53 min: Little plays a short pass through to Stanway in the box. The midfielder goes for goal and forces a save from Keating.

50 min: Arsenal break down the right side again as Baum looks to send a low cross into the box, but Fisk makes the interception.

48 min: Arsenal move forward now as Fox looks to play a pass into the Liverpool box, but it is intercepted and cleared.

46 min: Liverpool break forward early as Kapocs plays the ball across goal, but nobody can tap it home.

Bill sent this email just before kick-off:

“I can’t help looking at the Arsenal line up and thinking ‘that’s a good team’. So what happened, and how can it be improved?

“Did they lose players who brought spirit over the summer? Are they aging? Is there a gloom or lack of belief, or even lack of ambition in the camp? Is there a position that is lacking (e.g. striker), or even a position or role that is a particular problem (e.g. striker / scoring goals)?

“Is all of this the sort of thing that a new manager needs to address? I kinda hope not. I really like Renée. But maybe that’s the issue - she’s too likeable? Ah well. COYG.”

To me, it feels like Arsenal don’t really have a player who can grab their team-mates and the game by the scruff of the neck when things are not going their way. Katie McCabe had that ability – but she made the move across London to Chelsea this summer. Beth Mead also had the ability to come on and change games, but, of course, she is now at Manchester City.

It also feels like Arsenal have become quite predictable in the way they approach games and play. Teams know what to expect, so they can find a way to beat them.

Of course, that would be down to Slegers, but I also think the players need to take some responsibility for not being clinical enough when they do get chances.

Elsewhere in the WSL right now, the game between Aston Villa and Manchester United is goalless.

And, breathe… That was a brilliant half of football. Although Arsenal lead, it still feels like this could go either way.

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Join us for all the build-up and action as it happens from today’s five Serie A games, as Napoli in crisis host leaders Lazio, Milan visit Genoa, there’s Monza-Atalanta, a massive Inter vs. Como clash, then Juventus away to Fiorentina.

We begin at 11.30 GMT (12.30 CET) at the Stadio Maradona, where Napoli desperately need to turn things around, but are against a Lazio side flying high in top spot.

At 14.00 GMT (15.00 CET), Milan and Atalanta also try to shake off European setbacks with their trips to Genoa and Monza respectively.

All eyes on San Siro at 17.00 GMT (18.00 CET) for a huge Inter vs. Como Scudetto battle to fuel Cesc Fabregas ambition.

Finally, the bitter grudge match between Fiorentina and Juventus at 19.45 GMT (20.45 CET).

Both teams are in crisis mode, as the hosts have lost three on the bounce and are missing Jan Ziolkowski, Michael Folorunsho, Yvan Maye and Matteo Pessina, but Cyril Ngonge shakes off a knock and Gustavo Varela has his eye on the Capocannoniere crown.

La Dea are decimated by injuries and stretched thin by Thursday’s 1-1 Conference League draw against Kairat Almaty. Maurizio Sarri is without a victory since October 15 in all competition, scraping three draws and as many losses. Gianluca Gaetano, Raoul Bellanova, Thomas Kristensen, Isak Hien, Franck Kessie, Giacomo Raspadori and Odilon Kossounou miss out, but Sead Kolasinac returns.

The Grifone are in sparkling form with three consecutive victories, including against Juventus, and had practically a full squad to choose from.

The Rossoneri are deeply unpredictable, lurching from defeat to Bologna to winning the Derby della Madonnina to a 2-0 Europa League loss with Ferencvaros on Thursday.

Strahinja Pavlovic, Fikayo Tomori, Matteo Gabbia, Samuel Chukwueze, Ruben Loftus-Cheek, Pietro Terracciano and Alexis Saelemaekers are still injured, so Yunus Musah becomes a makeshift left-back.

Genoa’s last home win against Milan was 3-0 in October 2016, while they hadn’t secured a victory at all since March 2020 at San Siro.

Honours even at the Maradona as an end-to-end finale fails to see either side break the deadlock.

Four added minutes to be played as both sides search for a late winner.

Lazio break quickly through Isaksen but Beukema does brilliantly to deny the Dane space and time and forces it out for a Napoli throw.

Taylor takes the free-kick but it deflects off a Napoli player and out for a corner kick.

Cancellieri comes on for Frattesi, who is whistled and jeered by the home crowd as he exits the pitch.

Crunching tackle from Pedraza on Politano, who looks seriously hurt on the floor. Pedraza goes into the book for that one.

Mandas does well to smother a shot from Lucca from range. After a dull ten minutes, the game is starting to liven up a bit again. We’re in for an exciting end to this game, that’s for sure.

Napoli playing with renewed urgency since their goal, with a bit of self belief in them now. Lazio look a little shell-shocked and the likes of Leite and Pedraza look far less comfortable on the ball than Sutalo or Tavares.

Oliveira misses a golden chance to give Napoli the lead after the ball lands at his feet in acres of space within the Lazio box, but he balloons it over the bar. Lucky break for Lazio.

Miami’s Mario Cristobal fired up about refs’ controversial call vs. Notre Dame

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Notre Dame raced out to a three-touchdown advantage on Saturday night in a must-win matchup against No. 1 Miami and managed to hold on for a 31-24 victory that will do wonders for their College Football Playoff hopes. The Irish needed to survive a late charge from the visiting Hurricanes that ended when Elijah Lofton could not reel in a potential game-tying catch in the end zone.

MORE: Best 'College GameDay' signs from Week 10: Miami vs. Notre Dame

Here's the near-catch, which has resulted in some controversy.

Now, if you're seeing this clip and not finding an controversy because Lofton clearly did not complete the process of the catch, well, you are in a different headspace than Miami fans who believe they were robbed. Head coach Mario Cristobal had a lot of frustrations to vent about the officials' decision after the loss. And hey, emotions run high so it's perfectly understandable. This type of play, though, has simply not been a catch for well over a decade.

"They negated Elija Lofton's touchdown with two hands on the ball and two feet inbounds," Cristobal said. "Like we told our team before we left Miami, do not leave it in the hands of the officials.

"Congratulations to Notre Dame for winning the game, but very disappointed in the way that was called with the game on the line and clear possession of a football inbounds."

Asked by a reporter if he was given any explanation for the decision, Cristobal asserted multiple times that he received zero.

Nov 7, 2026; South Bend, Indiana, USA; Notre Dame Fighting Irish quarterback CJ Carr (13) and quarterback Noah Grubbs (10) warm up before the game against the Miami Hurricanes at Notre Dame Stadium. Mandatory Credit: Michael Caterina-Imagn Images Credit: IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect (Reuters)

Look, coming so close only to lose by a split-second is really tough. Yet one could make the argument that the call was so obvious it didn't merit any type of explanation. Even Lofton doesn't appear convinced he caught the football. Thinking of it a different way, if this would have somehow been ruled a catch it would be the most controversial call of the college football season.

The good news for Miami is that this setback won't hurt them too much and they remain in firm control of their destiny when it comes to snagging a top-four position and bye in the playoffs.

Allegri: ‘Napoli not out of anything yet, players doing their best’

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Max Allegri insists Napoli ‘are not out’ of anything yet after the 1-1 draw with Serie A leaders Lazio. ‘When we’re in the middle of the storm, we get the boat to shore somehow.’

The Partenopei went into this match in crisis mode after back-to-back defeats to Juventus in Serie A and FC Porto in the Champions League, the latter sparking tension and prompting President Aurelio De Laurentiis to put the whole squad into a training retreat.

Lazio had taken the lead at the Stadio Diego Armando Maradona this afternoon through Davide Frattesi, but Napoli scraped a point thanks to an own goal from Alfonso Pedraza, who was trying to prevent Scott McTominay and Rasmus Hojlund from bundling over the line from six yards.

“We must continue, because we are still trailing in the table, but there is plenty of time. At Milan we had an eight-point advantage and lost 11 in the space of a couple of months,” Allegri told DAZN Italia.

“Let us remember we are not out of Serie A, the Coppa Italia or the Champions League yet. There are difficult moments over the course of a campaign, I believe we need to deal with them in a spirit of calm, try to recover those who are injured, and bring simplicity and organisation to the situation.”

Noa Lang made a gesture towards the fans that they did not appreciate, as he asked them to make some more noise, and they responded with jeers.

There were also angry reactions to Lorenzo Lucca when he was introduced, as the striker had engaged in an on-field row with teammate Kevin De Bruyne during the FC Porto match.

“When the team wins, the fans think we’re great, when we lose they don’t. All I can tell the fans is that this team is giving its all, mentally and physically,” assured Allegri.

“We wasted some big chances today, but these things happen, I have faith in these lads and with more organisation, we can get back on track.”

There are suggestions that Allegri could potentially see his position placed in doubt during the break for international duty, so how is the coach doing?

“I am fine. Fortunately, I haven’t had that many of these periods in my career, but when we are in the middle of the storm, we manage to get the boat to shore somehow. These lads need support and are doing their best.”

Jordan Love, Drake Maye represent new era in Packers-Patriots matchup

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The Green Bay Packers and New England Patriots don't play each other that often.

The most recent time was in 2022, an overtime game at Lambeau Field won by Green Bay with Aaron Rodgers under center during his final season in Green Bay.

In the teams' most recent matchup in New England in 2018, Tom Brady was on the field playing in his penultimate season with the team.

Sunday's game between the Packers (5-3) and Patriots (5-3) won't feature either future Hall of Famer. Rodgers is playing what he's said is his final NFL season in Pittsburgh, and Brady will be calling his first game in Gillette Stadium as an analyst for Fox Sports.

"I think I'm required contractually to catch up with him at some point," Patriots coach Mike Vrabel joked this week about Brady's latest return to New England.

But there will be plenty of focus on the quarterbacks that have come behind Rodgers and Brady for their respective former franchises.

Jordan Love appears to be finding his stride in his fourth season as the full-time starter, leading the league in passing yards for a team looking to make the playoffs for the fourth consecutive season.

Meanwhile, Drake Maye has recovered from a rough start to account for at least three touchdowns in each of his past five games, leading the Patriots to victories in four of them.

While the Packers enter the game last in the NFL in rushing, averaging only 60.6 yards per game, Love said he isn't worried about generating offense.

"The run game isn't where it needs to be, but I think we've still been able to kind of adjust and still make plays," he said. "There's a lot more out there, but we've done a great job just being able to win these games by putting up some points, and that's what it comes down to."

Maye said the matchup is certainly a measuring stick opposite a team that like them is trying to keep pace in their division.

"They're good, they've beaten good teams, and they continue to show up every year," Maye said. "We've definitely got a challenge and a chance to see how we look."

When/where to watch: Sunday, 4:25 p.m. EST, FOX.

Against the spread: Packers 5-3; Patriots 3-4-1.

Last week: Packers beat Panthers 27-19; Patriots beat Dolphins 24-19.

Last meeting: Packers beat Patriots 27-24 on Oct. 2, 2022, in Green Bay.

Packers QB Jordan Love vs. Patriots secondary. The Packers quarterback is leading the NFL with 2,246 passing yards (280.8 per game) to go along with 16 touchdown passes (fourth in the NFL). It will be a challenge for a New England secondary that has only four interceptions this season. The Patriots come in allowing 217.3 passing yards per game.

— The Packers are attempting to beat the Patriots in New England for the first time since a 28-10 victory in 2002. The Packers have faced the Patriots in New England just twice since, losing 31-27 in 2010 and 31-17 in 2018.

— The Packers average 60.6 yards rushing per game and 2.9 yards per carry to rank last in the NFL in both categories.

— Green Bay has had multiple takeaways in five straight games and has gone 4-1 during that stretch. The Packers have 15 takeaways to rank second in the NFL, behind Tampa Bay's 16 entering Week 9.

— The Packers had a season-high six sacks in their 27-19 victory over the Carolina Panthers last week. Six different players each had a sack.

— In that Carolina game, Packers LB Ty'Ron Hopper became the first Packer since Charles Woodson in 2009 to have at least three passes defensed, a sack and an interception in the same game.

— Packers WR Christian Watson has 708 yards receiving to rank fourth in the NFL. Matthew Golden has 619 yards receiving to rank seventh entering Week 9.

— Patriots WR Mack Hollins returned to practice this week after going on injured reserve following a calf injury in their Week 4 win at Buffalo.

— The Patriots can improve to 4-0 at home for the first time since they won their first five home games to open the 2019 season.

— New England is coming off a season-high five sacks in last week's win at Miami. It also hasn't done that since the 2019 season.

— All five of wide receiver Kyle Williams' five career touchdown catches have been for at least 30 yards. He is the third Patriots player to have had each of their first five TD receptions cover that distance.

This is a developing story. Check back for updates as more information becomes available.

They complete this final round of EPL games before heading into the international break

As a Newcastle United fan, have a look at these Premier League fixtures on Sunday and have a think about what you’d like to happen and what is most likely in terms of results.

My conclusions and predictions on these Sunday matches:

Brighton are top of the Premier League form table with five wins and one defeat. However, Brentford have only lost one of their last six, even if winning only two of the last half dozen.

Two teams that are good to watch and I think the fact Brighton had a midweek match might help Brentford get a draw.

I would be very surprised if Chelsea don’t win this by at least a couple.

Man U are in the relegation zone for very good reason. Only a lucky win against Ipswich all season in the Premier League and if Villa turn up today then this will be an away win.

Man U fluked a midweek victory against an average Roma side in midweek but my prediction is that Carrick and his players will enter the international break on the back of another defeat. Whether Carrick is still in charge after the internationals…unlikely.

Amorim: ‘Genoa really strong where Milan suffer, must be competitive’

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Ruben Amorim warns Genoa are ‘really strong’ in areas where Milan have struggled recently, so they need to ‘be competitive’ this afternoon, and he explains why Yunus Musah becomes a left-back.

It kicks off at the Stadio Luigi Ferraris at 14.00 GMT (15.00 CET).

You can follow all the build-up and action as it happens from today’s five Serie A games on the Liveblog.

The Rossoneri are a Jekyll and Hyde team, able to go from a 4-2 home defeat to Bologna, through a 3-1 Derby della Madonnina triumph against Inter, to losing 2-0 at San Siro to Ferencvaros in the Europa League.

Amorim said after Thursday’s setback that this team was “not ready” to be competitive every three days, so in the final match before the break for international duty, it’s anyone’s guess which side will turn up today at Marassi.

“The atmosphere doesn’t matter. We need to respond to the last game, we just need to be competitive, that’s what I want to see today. We showed during the season we can play good football, but we need to be competitive,” Amorim told DAZN Italia.

“The most important thing is to use what we didn’t do in the last game and do it today. We know Genoa are really strong from the first minute, with pressure, with transition, so if you look at the last game, we had issues in those aspects. So we tried to use those mistakes to prepare for this one.”

The injury crisis drags on, as Strahinja Pavlovic, Fikayo Tomori, Matteo Gabbia, Samuel Chukwueze, Ruben Loftus-Cheek, Pietro Terracciano and Alexis Saelemaekers are still out of action.

There is squad rotation with the return of Mike Maignan, who was suspended on Thursday, Mario Gila, Luka Modric, Christian Pulisic and Diego Moreira to the starting XI, but Yunus Musah appears to be a makeshift left-back.

“Sometimes it’s the right foot, the characteristics of the player, the quality we want to have on the ball, so it’s a lot of things,” explained Amorim.

Luis Enrique revels in 11th straight PSG win

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Luis Enrique lauded a "special" victory as Paris Saint-Germain made it 11 straight wins by beating Troyes.

Illia Zabarnyi scored PSG's goal in a 1-0 win at the Parc des Princes.

PSG's 11-game winning run is the third longest in the club's history, after a streak of 12 between April and August 2015, and then one of 16 between December 2015 and February 2016.

It is also the second time Luis Enrique has recorded 11 consecutive wins in his coaching career, after doing so with Barcelona in 2015.

PSG head into the international break sure of their place at the top of Ligue 1, with 23 points from their 10 matches.

“Eleven wins in a row – that's good," Luis Enrique said.

"But what stands out for me is that we've managed to win all our matches between the two international breaks.

"It's the first time we've done that. As is usually the case against teams who are in a tricky position in the table, it was a difficult match. I think we created more chances than usual, we were looking to score more goals, but they defended very well.

"I'm very pleased, especially when we win this sort of match. Winning this match was special, as it always is when we play at the Parc des Princes."

— Paris Saint-Germain (@PSG_English) November 8, 2026

PSG have won their last five home matches in all competitions, their longest run in a single season since August-October 2024 (also five).

They have scored in each of their last 54 home Ligue 1 matches (135 goals), the longest such run by a team since Nice between May 1975 and February 1978 (55).

Zabarnyi has scored two goals in five Ligue 1 matches this season, which is already his highest tally in a single league season and one fewer than in his first six campaigns in total.

"We're in good shape, good form, and we just need to continue," Zabarnyi said.

"Everything in the team is working well. We didn't do anything special, we just keep working, keep doing our thing and now is just our time. I've worked a lot and kept focusing, and now I'm playing games, and playing well, I think, and I deserve it."

God of War Spin-Off Game Seemingly Getting Surprise PS4 Port

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

God of War: Sons of Sparta, the retro-style Metroidvania by Mega Cat Studios, is seemingly coming to the PS4.

The game first launched on the PS5 as a State of Play shadow drop earlier this year.

Word comes courtesy of the social media account PlayStation Game Size, which frequently scrapes Sony’s servers for unannounced information. It most recently accurately leaked the file size of GTA 6, for example.

Writing on X (or Twitter), it simply said: “God of War: Sons of Sparta - PS4.”

Asked for clarification about whether the spin-off side-scroller is coming to Sony’s last-gen console, it simply replied, “Yes.”

Given the style of game, I imagine Mega Cat Studios would have absolutely no problems porting this title to the PS4 – it’s just surprising it’s going to the effort to do so.

While the last-gen console remains popular, Sony has mostly moved on from it now. The last first-party game it released on the console was MLB The Show 23 in 2023, so it’s been over three years.

Should this turn out to be true, PS4 owners will be getting access to a likeable if largely unremarkable Metroidvania.

In our 7/10 God of War: Sons of Sparta review, my colleague Stephen Tailby wrote:

“God of War: Sons of Sparta is a fun spin-off that takes a series we all know and love and places it somewhere entirely different. It's a mostly successful experiment; the Metroidvania structure works well with the franchise's mechanics, and it looks and sounds the part. However, it takes too long to really come into its own, leaving the early parts of the game feeling a bit lacking compared to the richer experience found later on. If you have the patience to see it through, and can overlook some rough edges here and there, this is a worthwhile adventure for fans that delves into Kratos' history in a way we haven't seen before.”

Assuming PlayStation Game Size is accurate, I imagine an announcement will be made before the end of the year.

A few hours before the Clasico between FC Barcelona and Real Madrid, scheduled for this Sunday evening, Kylian Mbappé finds himself under pressure. Despite his individual performances and his efficiency in front of goal, the French striker is still struggling to silence criticism concerning his collective record since his arrival in the Spanish capital in 2024. A situation which fuels debates around his ability to influence major events and lead Real Madrid to new titles. Invited to comment on this poster, Pascal Dupraz did not mince his words, believing that Mbappé embodies, in terms of collective trophies, “the ultimate loser”. A particularly harsh formula for the captain of the Blues, whose individual statistics nevertheless remain impressive.

“Mbappé is far from being the most responsible, but he is the one in the spotlight because he is the star of the team. He scores around forty goals per season, so these are not failed seasons, but he is missing titles. Since he arrived, in terms of collective titles, he has embodied the ultimate loser. The club he left wins everything during that time.more generally detailed the ex-Toulouse coach on RMC. Dupraz is now expecting a great performance from the Frenchman against Barça, in a meeting which could allow him to respond on the pitch and demonstrate his influence in summit meetings.

Serie A | Napoli 1-1 Lazio: Pedraza own goal hands Allegri a lifeline after quick start from Frattesi

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Lazio stayed unbeaten at the top of Serie A but were held to a 1-1 draw by Napoli, as Alfonso Pedraza’s own goal cancelled out Davide Frattesi’s opener and gave under-pressure Max Allegri a point at the Maradona.

Allegri went into the game under heavy pressure after defeats to Juventus and Porto. Aurelio De Laurentiis visited the training ground on Friday, and sporting director Giovanni Manna described the situation before kick-off as “unacceptable”. Gennaro Gattuso, back at his former club, came to Naples having won his last six Serie A games, with no Lazio fans allowed to travel because the fixture was classed as high-risk.

See how it all unfolded on the Liveblog, as well as action from the rest of today’s Serie A matches.

Lazio made the stronger start. Gustav Isaksen fired just wide inside two minutes, and only a flying save from Vanja Milinković-Savić stopped his curling shot in the ninth minute. The Napoli goalkeeper then clawed away Josip Šutalo’s header from a corner.

The visitors went ahead just before the half-hour. Tijjani Noslin led a fast counter and slipped in Frattesi, who arrived late in the box and drilled a low shot past Milinković-Savić for his fourth goal of the season.

Lazio lost Šutalo to a muscular injury soon afterwards, and Napoli grew into the game. They should have equalised in the 42nd minute, when Mathias Olivera’s cutback found Rasmus Højlund two yards out, but Christos Mandas scrambled the Dane’s effort off the line.

Gattuso took off Nuno Tavares at half-time, and his replacement had a costly start. Three minutes into the second half, Noa Lang crossed from the left, and Pedraza, stretching to stop Scott McTominay volleying, turned the ball into his own net.

Napoli pushed for a winner, but Mandas was outstanding. He saved from McTominay at close range and denied Kevin De Bruyne in a one-on-one, and Diogo Leite cleared the loose ball off the line. Olivera also lifted a good chance over the bar.

The game became more open after both coaches went to their benches. Mandas kept out a low shot from substitute Lorenzo Lucca, and Milinković-Savić saved from Matteo Cancellieri at the other end.

The draw takes Lazio to 29 points, two clear of Roma before Inter host Como this evening. Napoli are on 15 points. It’s the first time they’ve taken anything from a Serie A game after going behind this season, but they are still without a clean sheet in eight games.

Mike Vick, Norfolk State lose after his order was ignored

📅 2026-11-08  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Like a growing number of former NFL players, Mike Vick has become a college football coach. Unlike many college football coaches, a direct order from Vick wasn’t followed on Saturday.

With 1:34 to play during Saturday’s game between Vick’s Norfolk State and North Carolina Central, Norfolk State led, 28-23. Norfolk State had the ball, facing second and five from the 50. North Carolina Central had no timeouts remaining.

While a pair of knees wouldn’t have fully exhausted the remaining time, Vick told the offense to take a knee in victory formation. Two straight kneel-downs would have resulted in a punt with less than 15 seconds to play.

Instead, Norfolk State lined up in shotgun formation, the quarterback ran to the left, a defender hit him, and the quarterback fumbled.

North Carolina Central recovered at the Norfolk State 37. Five plays later, North Carolina scored a touchdown, on a 26-yard touchdown pass, making the score 29-28. (The two-point conversion attempt failed.)

Twenty-seven seconds remained. Norfolk State drove in position to attempt a game-winning field goal. The 37-yard try missed wide left.

“I don’t know what happened at the end of the game,” Vick told reporters after a loss that dropped Norfolk State to 4-6 on the season. “I put my headset down. I called victory formation. All we had to do was take a knee, kneel the clock out. That’s just situational football. I don’t know what happened in regards to that. I looked up and we was running the ball, and then I seen the fumble. But my final instructions was victory formation. That’s when you kneel it out. And they had, I think, zero timeouts left and the game was over practically.”

“Well, I put my headset down, and I took my headset off because all we had to do was just run it three times, or two times, or kneel it out. That’s called victory. I don’t know what happened. A run play was called. I looked up, I seen us running the ball. I don’t know what happened. I really don’t. I don’t know what the play call was. I don’t know why we even ran that play.

“People will look at that, and that’ll go on as a teaching tape for a lot of teams across the country for years to come. I hate it had to be us. But I called victory formation. Just letting everybody know that. I called victory.”

While the strategy wouldn’t have completely exhausted the clock, it was the right move. And, even if it wasn’t, the coach gave the order. The order wasn’t followed.

So what happened? Vick said he had taken off his headset and then later realized that a run play had been called. The fact that the offense went straight into shotgun formation suggests that it wasn’t the players going rogue. Apparently, offensive coordinator Brian Sheppard decided to ignore Vick’s directive.

Indeed, a somewhat chaotic postgame video (posted by Vick on Twitter) includes Vick telling his players “it was supposed to have been victory.”

The video of the moments before the play in question shows the offense gathered at the numbers near the Norfolk State bench, speaking to a group of coaches who were still wearing headsets. The coach without a headset (who appears to be Vick) walked to the sideline before turning around and returning to the meeting.

It’s clear that the coaches and players were having a discussion. From Vick’s perspective, there was nothing to discuss.

And while Vick claims his instruction was ignored, he seemed to be a little too nonchalant at a time when he should have realized that something more detailed than telling the quarterback to line up under center, receive the snap, and take a knee was being planned.

If Vick believes Sheppard or other coaches overruled the head coach, Vick should be issuing pink slips immediately. It’s possible, though, that Vick himself bears the bulk of the blame for not noticing that something other than the implementation of victory formation was coming.

The draw for the second round of the FA Cup will take place on Monday 9 November after this weekend’s fixtures

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

The FA Cup is in full swing for the 2026-27 season with 40 first round fixtures to be played over the weekend before the draw for the second round takes place on Monday 9 November.

There were 32 non-league sides left in the competition at the start of the weekend, seeking to stake a claim for a spot in the next round as they come up against the teams from League’s One and Two.

Friday night’s matches saw 2021 champions, and former Premier League winners, Leicester City avoid a potential banana skin at sixth-tier Oxford City while Bristol Rovers overcame Cambridge United.

Saturday saw a few non-league teams get the better of EFL foes as Kidderminster thrashed Doncaster 3-0, Carlisle battled past Salford City 1-0 and Barrow won 1-0 on their long trip to Gillingham, while seventh-tier Guiseley also upset National League Sutton United 1-0. However, League One promotion-chasers Bradford City routed Marine FC 7-0.

Plymouth Parkway and Berkhamsted conclude the first round fixtures on Monday evening and will contest a spot in the next round following the second round draw on TNT Sports.

Here’s everything you need to know about the second-round draw.

The draw for the second round of the 2026-27 FA Cup takes place on Monday 9 November, before the clash between non-league clubs Plymouth Parkway and Berkhamsted.

The television coverage of the game starts at 6:30pm GMT, with the draw itself expected to begin shortly afterwards.

The draw will be available to watch live in the UK on TNT Sports 1, HBO Max, and on the TNT Sports YouTube channel. Coverage begins at 6.30pm, with the draw itself expected to start at approximately 6.45pm, before kick off at 7.30pm.

There will be 40 teams in the draw for the second round with 20 fixtures to be made ahead of the entry of the Premier League and Championship clubs from the third round onwards.

6. Oldham Athletic or Sheffield Wednesday

The 20 FA Cup second-round matches will be held across the weekend of 5-6 of December, with the third-round draw presumed to take place on the evening of Monday 7 December.

Have we been thinking about franchise QBs all wrong? CJ Carr, Darian Mensah and the Brock Purdy predicament

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

CJ Carr running Notre Dame's two-minute offense against Miami to end the first half is all you need to know about who he is.

It's as surgical a series of plays as you'll see this season; he's calm before the snap, unpanicked after, even when he's avoiding the rush, always has his eyes downfield, and throws with the accuracy and precision that will make you a first-rounder, even if you lack the "prototypical physical tools" NFL teams covet in their franchise QBs. When you look like you've been doing this for a decade, no one cares if you can throw the ball 80 yards or run a 4.3 forty.

Heck of a 2 Min Drive from CJ Carr! pic.twitter.com/HxMYEJksnu

That drive ended with an 18-yard laser to Micah Gilbert to give the Fighting Irish a 17-3 halftime lead. And while they didn't cruise to a blowout win – credit to Darian Mensah and the Canes for battling back – it did reinforce a question I've been thinking about as we work our way through another "can't-miss" quarterback class that has, once again, missed:

Have we been evaluating the position backward?

Every fall, NFL teams (and folks in the media too) fall in love with the big, athletic, strong-armed quarterbacks who, if you squint enough, start to look like Justin Herbert or Josh Allen. And there's a reason for that. When it all comes together, it can be special. When it doesn't … well, it can be the 2026 Los Angeles Chargers.

It's why we annually watch evaluators excuse bad decision-making, chronic inaccuracy and poor processing because a QB gets off the bus looking like Will Levis or Drew Allar or if we're talking the '27 class, names like Arch Manning and LaNorris Sellers. Meanwhile, Brock Purdy, Mr. Irrelevant in 2022, and Kirk Cousins, a fourth-rounder a decade earlier, lacked those eye-popping physical traits but proved they could play quarterback at a high level in the NFL.

I'd even throw Mac Jones into the group. He put up huge numbers at Alabama and made the Pro Bowl as a rookie in New England, but his career went sideways after the Patriots decided Matt Patricia and Joe Judge should lead the offense. Jones had a pit stop in Jacksonville before finding his way to Kyle Shanahan and San Francisco, and all he did was play so well in Purdy's absence early last season that there were questions about whether he should keep the job. The point, in part, is what a smart, accurate quarterback with average physical tools can accomplish when the system plays to his strengths.

To be clear, this is less about arm strength or athleticism than how much inconsistency evaluators are willing to put up with for the upside, no matter how unlikely that realized upside might be. Put another way: are we undervaluing quarterbacks who win with anticipation, accuracy, processing and average NFL arms?

Which brings us to CJ Carr and Darian Mensah. Both should come in 6-foot-2 or 6-foot-3, so we're not talking about Bryce Young or Kyler Murray. But neither has a Patrick Mahomes fastball in their locker, either. They see the field, throw on time and with anticipation, and consistently understand where the football needs to go. Saturday gave us two very different looks at those skills.

Carr was clinical from the start. He set protections, got the ball out quickly and accurately, and once he started feeling it, was damn-near unstoppable in the way Purdy can be when he's in his bag. Again, I'd refer you to Carr running that two-minute offense as all the evidence you need.

Mensah spent much of the first half running for his life before settling down in the third quarter and drawing the Hurricanes to within seven points after one of his bread-and-butter back-shoulder throws:

Mensah —> BarkateThat 2025 Duke team… I’ll never forget pic.twitter.com/qa8pn75KKn https://t.co/rG4KgrMZZe

Notre Dame gave Miami's offensive line fits for much of the night, and on the Hurricanes' very next drive, Mensah threw just his second interception of the season.

NOTRE DAME INTERCEPTS DARIAN MENSAH!📺 Notre Dame Football on NBC & Peacock pic.twitter.com/QA0UH9tXao

It's undoubtedly a bad pick, but this is what NFL evaluators will note – and what shows up all over Mensah's tape: he's one of the best anticipation passers in the class. And sometimes, when you throw with anticipation on a speed out and miss inside, you're playing with fire. On this play, Mensah got burned.

It also didn't help that Malachi Toney, the best player on the planet not named Jeremiah Smith, was out with an ankle injury and exactly zero of the remaining Miami receiving options scared the Fighting Irish secondary.

But excuses, as they say, are for losers.

And don't get it confused: this wasn't Caleb Williams going into South Bend in 2023 and throwing three first-half interceptions, repeatedly making bad decisions under pressure as Notre Dame dismantled USC. Mensah was under siege often, and despite that pick, he kept competing, made big-time throws down the stretch and gave Miami a chance to tie the game in the closing minutes.

Mensah finds Lofton but Luke Talich knocks it out of his hands 😱 pic.twitter.com/oA1bN4io4a

He didn't win, and he wasn't perfect, but he bounced back from a slow start and an early second-half interception to give Miami a chance to tie it late, on the road against one of the best defenses he'll face all season. And he did it without abandoning the traits that made him my QB1 in the first place (and Malachi Toney).

It's worth remembering that neither Mensah nor Carr entered the season as sure-fire first-round picks. Back in August, I had both with second-round grades and a lot to prove. And while there was a lot to like, there were also some legitimate concerns.

During his 2025 season at Duke, Mensah appeared to have the bigger arm, but his footwork was all over the place, and his decision-making was at times spotty. Carr threw with anticipation, consistently knew where to go with the football and showed impressive touch on second-level throws. But he could also let pressure affect him, and an average arm raised questions about how his game would translate to the NFL.

The numbers told a different story. In August, we introduced FRED, a model that compares college quarterbacks with previous first-round picks based on their production and experience. Mensah scored a 39.4, putting him within striking distance of 50, a mark every eligible quarterback in our historical sample had reached before eventually becoming a first-round pick. Carr scored just 10.6, although he had considerably less starting experience. I liked Carr more than the model did, but Mensah's numbers suggested he might be closer to first-round territory than his 2025 tape suggested.

Fast-forward to present day, and Mensah is my QB1, Oklahoma State's Drew Mestemaker is QB1a, and Carr is QB3. Both Mensah and Carr have made impressive strides, and both have looked the part of future first-round picks.

Both had dominated stretches of the season, but neither had faced many opponents capable of truly testing them. The Miami-Notre Dame matchup offered a chance to change that.

For Mensah, the biggest improvement this season has been his consistency. Coming into Saturday's game, he had completed 77.4% of his passes, averaged 10.3 yards per attempt and thrown 25 touchdowns and just one interception (which is now two). But here's what really stands out: When he throws in rhythm, he's completing 83.1% of his passes and averaging 11.4 yards per attempt, both among the best marks in college football. It's a reflection of what shows up every week on tape: He sees the field, throws with anticipation and rarely passes up an easy completion to force something downfield.

Carr has made significant strides too, in part because he has addressed one of my biggest preseason concerns. A year ago, he took sacks on nearly 21% of the plays in which he was pressured. This season, that number is now 6.7%, and he's been sacked just twice. His completion percentage under pressure has also improved from 26.1% to 51.9%. Notre Dame's outstanding offensive line deserves some credit, certainly, but Carr looks like a different quarterback when things break down; he's more decisive, better identifies pressure and seldom looks rattled.

The ability to avoid costly mistakes separates both quarterbacks from some of their peers. Mestemaker has put the ball in harm's way seven times in 54 opportunities under pressure, compared with one apiece for Mensah and Carr. Neither has been immune to bad decisions, but both have generally understood when to test a defense and when to take what it's giving them. And as we've seen with many highly drafted quarterbacks, that matters.

How top 2027 quarterback prospects handled pressure entering Week 10

There's also this idea that Carr's average arm means he's Captain Checkdown. Fun fact: Nearly a quarter of his passes this season have traveled at least 20 yards, almost twice Mensah's rate. Heading into Week 10, Carr had eight touchdowns on those attempts and only one turnover-worthy play. Mensah was far more accurate on deep throws, completing 58.1% compared with Carr's 37%, but Carr had 23 more attempts. He wins with timing and touch, even if his accuracy can suffer when he's late on an intermediate route or forced off his spot.

There's also some context to Mensah's remarkable efficiency. A quarter of his attempts have come behind the line of scrimmage, generating 553 yards, compared with just 12.6% of Carr's throws. Miami does a terrific job creating easy completions, and Mensah takes advantage of them. But he's also shown he can make the difficult throws, and his ability to generate offense without regularly risking turnovers puts him in rarefied company, even when compared to previous first-rounders.

None of this means the next Brock Purdy should be auto-drafted ahead of the next Josh Allen. Allen is proof that betting on rare talent can pay off (even if those bets rarely work out, the allure is often too irresistible), and Bryce Young shows NFL teams will invest the No. 1 pick in a quarterback with more modest physical tools if the college tape is exceptional. And Carr and Mensah are making strong cases that they belong in that category.

Processing and anticipation don't replace physical talent. They are talents themselves, and Carr's and Mensah's performances were a reminder of how much they matter.

The question for NFL teams isn't whether either can make NFL throws. They've done that repeatedly. It's whether we've been too quick to forgive the quarterbacks who look the part, and too slow to appreciate the ones who consistently play the part.

“He’s Got The Yips” Mads Hermansen’s West Ham Nightmare Goes Beyond Bad Form

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

There is a phenomenon in sport known as the yips. It’s a condition which seems to affect previously capable sportsmen, psychologically blocking them from performing even the simplest of tasks. In darts, it can become difficult to release the dart from the hand, while in golf it can make putting almost impossible.

In essence, you’re frozen, seemingly unable to perform a task which should have become second nature. And it was during Blackburn Rovers’ second goal against West Ham yesterday, when I watched Mads Hermansen fail to dive, that I found myself wondering whether this goalkeeper has developed the footballing equivalent of the yips.

It wasn’t just that goal, of course. The first was equally concerning, when Ezra Mayers was beaten down the flank and the ball was crossed into the penalty area. The situation required some kind of intervention from Hermansen, but there was none. No punch, no parry, no save. Just indecision and a complete lack of intervention.

Unfortunately, this apparent hesitation has permeated Hermansen’s game recently and it’s hard not to conclude that he’s got the yips. There have been occasions when he has looked like little more than a bystander as goals have flown past him, sometimes appearing rooted to the spot or simply falling over rather than making a convincing attempt to save the ball.

The statistics are equally worrying. Based on the match figures we’ve examined, Hermansen has saved just 20% of the shots on target he has faced over West Ham’s last three Championship matches, conceding eight goals from ten attempts on target.

That’s an extraordinary return for a goalkeeper at any level, let alone one playing for a club with West Ham’s promotion ambitions. And while defenders must accept their share of the responsibility, the goalkeeper ultimately has to provide some resistance when opponents get a shot away.

The warning signs were already there before Cardiff, and his latest performance has only increased my concerns. I don’t know whether Hermansen is suffering from anything resembling the yips, but from watching him, I do wonder whether his confidence has deteriorated to the point where instinctive reactions are being replaced by hesitation.

This has become a huge problem, and one Scott Parker needs to address immediately. As things stand, I would have more confidence in either Alphonse Areola or Finlay Herrick than a goalkeeper who currently appears unable to make the saves West Ham desperately need.

Areola is an experienced international goalkeeper whom Parker already knows from their time together at Fulham. I thought he deserved his opportunity against Blackburn, and nothing I witnessed yesterday has changed my mind.

Hermansen’s performance was among the biggest disappointments in our Blackburn player ratings, and continuing to select him simply because he was previously considered first choice doesn’t make much sense when his performances have deteriorated so badly.

Clearly, there is a bigger issue to address in January, when West Ham will have another opportunity to examine their goalkeeping options. But we’ve only just entered November, and there are still two months and a hell of a lot of fixtures to negotiate before the transfer window opens.

That’s an awful lot of potential points to lose while waiting for a goalkeeper to rediscover his confidence. Parker has an international break in which to make some important decisions, and I would suggest the identity of his first-choice goalkeeper should be very near the top of that list.

Kris Gonzo (commonly known to the West Ham community as Gonzo) is the co-founder of Hammers Chat (established 2014) and the lead video content creator and columnist for Claret and Hugh.

With over a decade of professional sports media experience, he is a frequent West Ham United analyst and contributor for Sky Sports News, BBC Football Focus, and BBC Radio 5 Live. He previously served as an official video and content creator for West Ham United FC during the club's tenure at Upton Park and featured in the official documentary commemorating the historic final game at the Boleyn Ground.

A lifelong Hammer since the legendary 10-0 victory over Bury, when he isn't providing daily match analysis on the Hammers Chat YouTube Channel or broadcasting on X (@GONZObignose), he can be found walking his dog and restoring classic pinball machines.

After a tough run of recent results in the league, the Primavera managed to build on their mid-week cup win with a strong performance against last weekend Monza, winning 5-0 away from home. Max Anastasio, Mattia Zaramella (x2), Riccardo Grassini and Yanis Messaoudi scored in that rout.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

So, returning home to the PUMA House of Football, the challenge was to build on that result. The response was resounding: Giovanni Renna’s side won 5-1 against AlbinoLeffe in the early kick-off.

Milan took the lead after five minutes with a fine goal from Balentien, and the second goal came in the tenth minute through Vincent Ibrahimovic, who capitalized on a defensive error.

The visitors pulled one back in the 30th minute through Gamba, but in the 36th minute the Rossoneri reopened their two-goal advantage as Ibrahimovic got his name on the scoresheet again, curling one around the goalkeeper from inside the box.

In the second half, Anastasio scored the fourth goal on the hour mark following a cross from Angelicchio, and in the 68th minute Zaramella sealed the deal with an easy one-on-one with the goalkeeper.

The win puts Milan in sixth place in the table on 16 points, and they are now just five points off Parma in top spot, and three behind both Roma and Inter who occupy second and third at the moment. Next up is a trip to face Lazio after the break, the team currently in the place above them.

Juventus team news: Woltemade suffers flu as Zhegrova ready to step in

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Juventus could be without Nick Woltemade for tonight’s trip to Fiorentina after the striker came down with flu, opening the door for Edon Zhegrova to make his first Serie A start.

According to TuttoMercatoWeb, the German is a serious doubt for the game at the Artemio Franchi, which kicks off at 20:45 CET [19:45 GMT]. It’s another fitness blow for Luciano Spalletti, who is already without Kenan Yıldız, Khéphren Thuram and Manuel Locatelli.

If Woltemade misses out, Zhegrova is expected to come into the attack. The Kosovo international joined from Lille 18 months ago but has yet to start a league game for the Bianconeri.

TMW expect Spalletti to stick with a 3-4-2-1. Guglielmo Vicario would start in goal behind Federico Gatti, Gleison Bremer and Jhon Lucumí, with Pierre Kalulu and Weston McKennie at wing-back. Douglas Luiz and Pape Sarr would form the midfield pair.

Zhegrova and Kerim Alajbegović would play behind Randal Kolo Muani, who would move back to centre-forward without Woltemade.

There’s some good news for Spalletti. McKennie has shaken off the problem he picked up against Napoli, and Zeki Çelik is also available again. Andrea Cambiaso, however, misses out.

Juventus are seventh and looking for a third straight win.

Follow Daily Mail Sport's live coverage of all FOUR 2pm Premier League games - including Crystal Palace vs Liverpool and Sunderland vs Chelsea, before Man United host Aston Villa at 4.30pm.

So much of Ipswich's survival hopes are likely to depend on the creative talents of Julio Enciso. The Paraguayan playmaker netted in both the draws against Nottingham Forest and Hull.

If he scores again this afternoon, he'll become the first Ipswich player to find the net in three consecutive home Premier League games since Marcus Bent all the way back in 2002.

Ipswich's home form has been decent so far, too. Only Liverpool have come away with all three points in the league. They will need Portman Road to become a fortress if they are to make their stay in the top flight a longer one than last time.

How they do that against a Bournemouth team that's looking for an eighth straight win, in a run including AC Milan, Manchester United and Aston Villa...well, we're about to find out.

I thought Emiliano Martinez might make the bench at least, given how much he was pushing to play, even seeing a specialist to have a face mask fitted in time. But he’s not here.

Sunderland are bottom of the Premier League, but I feel, and a fair few others also feel, that is a false position. They’re just trying to get used to the Thursday-Sunday slog after making it into Europe.

'For us it's a massive game. We want to finish this run of games with a win and improve our position. It's not going to be easy,' he said.

'It's a small pitch. There are going to be demands in both boxes. We need to improve defending set-plays and have a good offensive performance.

Brighton are back home after five away games in a row and more than 5,000 miles on the road.

On Josh Acheampong's absence: 'He's recovering from a minor thing. He's not ready for today but we expect him back after the international break, and Emi (Martinez) as well.

On Jordan Henderson: 'He's so important for the team and I'm sure he will help today.'

On Sunderland: 'All the games in the Premier League are tough. Even if you play good, you don't always get what you deserve. It's the cruelty of football.'

Hello hello hello from the Stadium of Light.

Moises Caicedo starts and captains Chelsea despite only managing partial training in the build-up. I didn’t see that one coming, but then he is influential, and he is the type to have told Xabi Alonso that he wants to play.

It’s an iffy back line for the Blues, with Marco Palestra and Pep Chavarria as full backs signed in the summer. Wesley Fofana is next to Levi Colwill in central defence and was welcomed to Sunderland by one Chelsea fan who shouted at him: ‘Fofana, please be on the bench.’

A recent study conducted by kwiff analysed a variety of data to work out which fanbase in the country has the most passionate fans.

Factors included how often teams sell out their stadium, matchday traditions, and even how many streets are named after club legends.

And believe it or not, Manchester United have come out on top for having the most fanatical fans!

Liverpool travel without Ryan Gravenberch today. He missed the Fenerbahce loss in midweek, and the Arsenal defeat before, after a heel injury.

'The Ryan Gravenberch thing is a day-to-day thing, it’s a matter of pain,' Andoni Iraola said in the build-up to today.

The rumour mill has it that Crystal Palace manager Pierre Sage is clinging on to his job by a thread. Quite a few managers played Liverpool in a similar state last term and ended up winning so there is solace for him. I’ll start workshopping some puns for when he does get sacked. Sage stuffed six weeks before Christmas, etc.

Another important factor for Liverpool today - they need to win to guarantee that they are above Everton going into the international break.

A draw, depending on how many goals are scored, might be enought o lift them above their city rivals.

It's not often that you get a quarter of the way into the season with the Toffees above the Reds!

Liverpool can climb as high as fourth depending on results.

Manchester United are emerging as the Premier League club showing the strongest interest in Como centre-back Jacobo Ramón as they begin planning the next stage of their defensive rebuild.

The 21-year-old has enjoyed an outstanding rise under Cesc Fàbregas in Italy, establishing himself as one of Serie A’s most promising young defenders.

His performances have now earned him a first senior Spain call-up, while United, Chelsea and Liverpool are all monitoring his progress.

According to AS, Man United have been the most persistent English club in their pursuit of Ramón, although Chelsea and Liverpool are also keeping the defender on their radar. Read the AS report on Jacobo Ramón

Ramón is currently focused on Como and is not expected to leave during January, meaning any serious transfer battle is more likely to develop next summer.

The biggest complication for United is Real Madrid. Los Blancos retained 50 per cent of Ramón’s rights when selling him to Como and can reportedly bring him back for around €10million.

Madrid remain impressed by his progress and could eventually exercise that option before deciding whether he has a place in their first-team squad.

Fàbregas has previously made his admiration clear, describing Ramón as “a young player with a great future” when discussing the defender’s potential.

From a Man United perspective, the interest makes plenty of sense.

Ramón is young, physically imposing at 1.96m and comfortable building attacks from defence. He has also developed a goalscoring threat, contributing three goals and two assists for Como this season.

United have already seen his quality first-hand. Ramón scored during Como’s 4-1 Champions League victory over them in October, a performance that reportedly strengthened their interest.

United have increased their monitoring of the Spaniard as they assess potential defensive additions for 2027.

The difficulty is that United do not control the situation. Madrid’s inexpensive buy-back clause gives them a huge advantage, while Ramón is attracting interest from several major clubs.

Still, United should remain in the conversation. If they want to build a younger, more technically secure defence, Ramón fits that strategy extremely well.

November 15 Is Officially A Dark Day For Xbox Game Pass

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

November 15 is already set to be a somber day for Xbox Game Pass, so members should brace themselves for a massive hit to the subscription service’s catalog. The addition and removal of games from Microsoft’s service is part of its very structure, but removals tend to happen all together on a single day, while additions arrive spread out throughout the month. For example, five free games have been confirmed for Xbox Game Pass in November, though their additions will happen on different days, as part of the Wave 1 updates to the service this month.

This, however, is not the case with the games being removed from the service in November. According to a new report by CBR, several games are being removed from the service on November 15, including the classic GoldenEye 007. In total, members can expect 13 games to be removed at once from Xbox Game Pass, fully severing access to them for free via the service. If players intend to continue playing or start any of the removed games, they’d better start soon or consider purchasing these games – especially now, as they are at a discount.

The previous reports hinted at Xbox Game Pass losing 14 games this November, but one of the titles, Football Manager 26, is not being removed specifically on November 15. Instead, the game is leaving the service on November 10, the same day its sequel is being released. Even so, the majority of the loss is coming in bulk, and if you want to enjoy any of the games here, you’ll need to make choices.

The list below highlights all 13 games being removed from Xbox Game Pass on November 15, 2026, according to the official announcement on Xbox Wire:

The service is going to lose some very relevant titles with this batch. Apart from the aforementioned and classic GoldenEye 007, members will also lose access to incredible games like Moonlighter and Winter Burrow on Xbox Game Pass. Moonlighter’s been available for years, and Winter Burrow was added about a year ago, so most of the titles have been offered for a decent amount of time, but it is time to say goodbye.

There is one game, however, that you should make an effort to beat before it is removed from the catalog, especially if you’re a fan of good stories. The addition of 1000xResist to Xbox Game Pass happened in November last year, allowing members to play one of the best games of 2025. This is an extremely underrated game that arguably didn’t get the recognition that it deserves, but it is more than worth your time. On top of that, you can potentially beat the game before it leaves the service.

November 2026 is a huge month. Despite the removal of several titles from the subscription service, Nintendo Switch 2 owners can now officially play The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time Remake. In a few days, on November 19, Xbox and PlayStation players will be able to start playing the highly anticipated GTA 6. For those who aren’t purchasing any new games and are relying on their memberships, there’s good news too, as Xbox Game Pass is getting new additions throughout the month.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

FC Barcelona – Real Madrid: probable line-ups

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

This is the meeting that all of Spain is waiting for. FC Barcelona welcomes Real Madrid this Sunday at 9 p.m. at Camp Nou for a Clasico which could already weigh heavily in the title race. The Blaugranas will want to take advantage of home advantage to gain the upper hand over their historic rival, while the Merengues will look to leave Catalonia with a positive result to move up the standings. For this poster of the 10th day of La Liga, Barça should rely on Joan García in goals. In front of him, Xavi Espart, Pau Cubarsí, Gerard Martín and João Cancelo are expected to compose the defense. A rearguard who will have to be vigilant against Madrid’s offensive power, even in the absence of Vinicius Jr, suspended for this meeting.

In the midfield, FC Barcelona should rely on a trio composed of Rodri, Pedri and Fermín López. The technical mastery and ability of these three players to circulate the ball will be essential to impose the rhythm of the Catalans. Up front, Lamine Yamal should occupy the right side, with Raphinha on the left flank and Anthony Gordon up front. The young Spanish international will be particularly expected on the eve of the big Ballon d’Or ceremony in London where he is one of the credible candidates. Faced with a Madrid team capable of projecting quickly forward, Hansi Flick’s men will also have to be careful not to discover themselves and to maintain sufficient collective balance.

For Real Madrid, Thibaut Courtois was announced as the starter but a last minute injury changed Madrid’s plans. It will therefore be Andriy Lunin who will guard the cages, with a defense made up of Federico Valverde, Ibrahima Konaté, Antonio Rüdiger and Marc Cucurella. The combination of Konaté and Rüdiger in the center would bring physical power and aggression in duels, while Valverde could be used in his unusual new role on the right side of the defense. In midfield, Eduardo Camavinga, Aurélien Tchouaméni and Jude Bellingham are announced as starters. The trio will have to respond to the intensity of the Barcelona environment and prevent Pedri from dictating the game.

On the attacking front, Arda Güler should be aligned alongside Kylian Mbappé and Diomandé. The French international will be one of Madrid’s main offensive threats and will be responsible for weighing on the Catalan defense in a match that is always special for both clubs. The absence of Vinicius Jr, suspended, should modify the offensive habits of Real Madrid, who will have to find other solutions to make the difference in the final meters. With Mbappé at the forefront and Güler in creation, José Mourinho’s men nevertheless have several options to put Barça in difficulty. The Portuguese technician, back on the Madrid bench, will return to the Camp Nou for a particularly anticipated Clasico.

Liverpool are reportedly keeping a close watch on Aston Villa goalkeeper Zion Suzuki as they begin planning for life beyond Alisson Becker.

The Japan international has impressed since moving to Villa Park in the summer and is already attracting attention from several of Europe’s biggest clubs.

Liverpool’s interest is particularly significant because Alisson is in the final year of his contract, leaving the Reds with an important decision to make over one of the most influential players of their recent era.

According to TEAMtalk, Liverpool have checked on Suzuki while assessing their long-term goalkeeping options.

The 24-year-old joined Aston Villa for around £30million in the summer and has quickly impressed with his shot-stopping, distribution and ability to play as a modern sweeper-keeper. Real Madrid, Paris Saint-Germain, Arsenal, Manchester City and Tottenham are also monitoring him.

Villa, however, are under no pressure to sell. Suzuki signed a five-year contract and is considered a major part of their future, with TEAMtalk suggesting he could eventually command a world-record fee for a goalkeeper.

A CaughtOffside report also confirms Liverpool’s interest and notes that Suzuki is being viewed as a potential long-term successor to Alisson.

Liverpool would be wise to explore their options now rather than wait until Alisson actually leaves.

Manager Andoni Iraola admitted this week that he does not know whether the Brazilian will sign another contract, although discussions are taking place behind the scenes.

Alisson’s current deal expires next summer after Liverpool activated a one-year extension earlier in 2026.

Liverpool already have Giorgi Mamardashvili and highly-rated youngster Lucca Brughmans in their plans, but TEAMtalk reports that Mamardashvili is not currently viewed as Alisson’s definitive long-term replacement.

That makes Suzuki an understandable target. He is young, already Premier League-tested and possesses the distribution required in Iraola’s system.

The difficulty will be price. Villa have little reason to sell, and Liverpool could face competition from several elite clubs.

Still, if Alisson does depart in 2027, Suzuki looks like exactly the type of goalkeeper Liverpool should be considering.

Liverpool face Jeremie Frimpong transfer question as Saudi Pro League move emerges

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Alfredo is one of the most highly respected football journalists in Italy, breaking many of the biggest transfer news... read more

Jeremie Frimpong’s Liverpool future has suddenly become uncertain, with the Netherlands international reportedly open to a move to Saudi Arabia just 18 months after arriving at Anfield.

The 25-year-old joined Liverpool from Bayer Leverkusen for around £29.5million in 2025, but injuries and competition at right-back have prevented him from becoming an undisputed starter.

That has now led to surprising speculation that his stay on Merseyside could be cut short.

According to WinWin, as reported by GOAL, intermediaries have offered Frimpong to Saudi Pro League giants Al-Hilal ahead of the January transfer window.

The report claims Frimpong would be open to discussing a move to Saudi Arabia, potentially giving him the opportunity to start again after an inconsistent spell in England.

However, there is currently no suggestion that Liverpool and Al-Hilal have entered formal negotiations.

Frimpong is receptive to a January departure, with intermediaries approaching Al-Hilal on his behalf. Liverpool could look for another right-back if he leaves, particularly with Frimpong struggling to consistently establish himself.

The biggest twist is that Al-Hilal may not actually want Frimpong.

A source close to the Saudi club told Kooora that Frimpong is not currently among their transfer priorities, with manager Simone Inzaghi instead focused on strengthening at centre-back and in midfield.

That makes this situation worth treating cautiously. Being offered to a club is very different from that club actively pursuing a transfer.

Liverpool also have little reason to force an exit. Frimpong remains contracted until 2030 and still offers elite pace and attacking quality when fit. His recent injury problems have disrupted his rhythm, but selling after only 18 months could prove premature.

There is clearly a debate to be had over whether Frimpong fits Liverpool’s long-term defensive plans.

However, unless Al-Hilal or another club make a significant offer, giving him more time to prove himself appears the more sensible approach.

Liverpool consider defensive changes as summer signing struggles to find his best form

Stan Collymore is a football pundit and former player, signing for Liverpool FC in 1995 for an English transfer record... read more

Chelsea and Liverpool are both keeping a close eye on Brentford left-back El Hadji Malick Diouf, with the 21-year-old already attracting major interest despite only arriving at the club during the summer.

The Senegal international has built a strong reputation in English football thanks to his pace, athleticism and attacking threat from the left side.

Those qualities naturally appeal to two clubs looking at long-term defensive options, although Brentford are in no rush to cash in.

According to 4-4-2, both Chelsea and Liverpool have contacted Diouf’s representatives as they explore a possible future move.

The report suggests that a fee in the region of €50m to €60m could eventually be required.

Diouf only joined Brentford from West Ham in August, with the Bees paying up to £40m and handing him a five-year contract with an option for another season. Brentford head coach Keith Andrews praised the defender at the time, highlighting his development potential and the impact he had already made in the Premier League.

A related CaughtOffside report also states that both clubs have approached his camp, with Diouf viewed as a player capable of developing into a top-level left-back.

From Brentford’s perspective, there is no need to panic.

TEAMtalk reported that the club have no intention of entertaining a January sale and still see Diouf as an important long-term asset.

That stance makes sense. Selling him only months after paying a major fee would achieve little unless Chelsea or Liverpool make an offer Brentford simply cannot ignore.

For Chelsea, Diouf would fit their preference for young, athletic players with significant resale value. Liverpool’s interest is equally logical given their need to plan for the long term at left-back.

The bigger question is timing. Brentford have contractual control until 2031, so any January deal looks unlikely. But if Diouf continues to improve, interest from elite clubs will probably only grow.

For now, the Bees are in a strong position and that means Chelsea and Liverpool may have to be patient or pay a significant premium.

'I Played Under Pep Guardiola at Man City - It Was Horrendous For My Mental Health'

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Most players of the modern era probably dream of playing under Pep Guardiola. The iconic coach has won trophy after trophy across spells in charge of Barcelona, Bayern Munich and Manchester City.

However, you don't make it to the top of the sport without having to make some ruthless calls along the way. As such, it's fair to say Guardiola hasn't always seen eye-to-eye with every member of his different squads over the years.

Perhaps most infamously, he fell out with Zlatan Ibrahimovic when the pair were at Barcelona as the Swedish icon struggled to get on board with the Spaniard's man-management style. It's now emerged that one former Man City player also didn't enjoy his time working under Guardiola.

When James Trafford signed for the Citizens in the summer of 2025, he looked set to become the club's number one, replacing departing goalkeeper Ederson. And the Englishman was given the job initially, but on deadline day of the transfer window, Guardiola signed Gianluigi Donnarumma – who was one of the best goalkeepers in the world – from Paris Saint-Germain.

Having started the first three games of the 2025/26 Premier League season, Trafford suddenly found himself benched. Speaking on Football Daily's The Football Interview, Trafford reflected on just how difficult that was:

"What a horrendous, horrendous feeling. Not playing. You are ringing a lot of people and just venting. Like most people, you have a [bad] day at work and you ring someone up.

"I ring my dad, I ring my mum, I ring my missus, I ring my mates. I like ringing my mates and we can just have a good talk about it all. It's quite funny.

"I had to play regularly this season for my own mental wellbeing. You just lose yourself not playing. "Maybe I'm different, I don't know, but the feeling of not playing every week, it felt horrendous.

"I just had to play every week this season for that mental feel-good and to keep progressing with my career and edging me closer to achieving the dreams I want to achieve."

Trafford would not play for Man City again in the Premier League until the final game of the season, which just so happened to also be Guardiola's last match in charge. By that stage, though, the title race was over, with Arsenal confirmed champions the week before.

Trafford had seemingly been promised the number one shirt, but instead spent the campaign playing second fiddle to Donnarumma. Ironically, he still played a vital role in the two trophies City lifted in that campaign.

Indeed, the English goalkeeper played every minute in the club's FA Cup and League Cup runs, as his team beat Chelsea and Arsenal in the respective finals for each competition. Reflecting on that experience, Trafford remarked:

"Going into any season, I always have the highest of hopes and always the belief that it will go the best way it could.

"Unfortunately, we didn't win all four trophies. It didn't go how I expected it to go, but it happens.

"In the moment, I just tried to make the best of it as I could. Looking back, I don't think it was a disappointing season in the end because I learned so much, I improved so much and I won two trophies in one year.

"There were moments when I was very down, regretting things and feeling disappointed, but looking back, I'm very thankful for the experience. What an incredible year."

It easy enough to see him earn a spot in England's squad for the 2026 World Cup. Once again, Trafford had to settle for a bit-part role, but he was more accepting of that, noting: "I didn't play, but it is what it is. I didn't expect to play when I got called up. I knew what my role was, but you just take as much experience as possible, learn from it and keep trying to win the day, because the more days you win, the better position you're in."

He then left Man City that same summer, joining Leeds United for a fee in the region of £40m. At Elland Road, the 24-year-old has quickly cemented his status as the number one choice between the sticks, and now has his eyes on Jordan Pickford's job for Euro 2028.

"It's my aim and it's something I've always dreamed of," Trafford said of his England hopes. "I always believe that one day I could be. Every day I try to keep improving to put myself in the best position to be England's number one one day.

"I think everyone in the goalkeeping department with the national team wants to be number one because it's an amazing position to be in."

With Pickford now 32, Thomas Tuchel may well start to look for alternatives in the near future. It will be interesting to see if Trafford can make a genuine push for the role.

Brock Taylor sets Vanderbilt football record with five field goals vs Mississippi State

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

STARKVILLE, MS − Brock Taylor set a Vanderbilt football single-game record by hitting five field goals in one game against Mississippi State.

The fifth field goal put the Commodores (3-6, 0-5 SEC) up, 22-14, in the fourth quarter against the Bulldogs (4-4, 2-4) in the game Nov. 7 at Davis-Wade Stadium.

Taylor's kicks were from 22, 34, 36, 38 and 54.

The previous record for most field goals in a single game was four, held by both Hawkins Golden in 1973 against Georgia and John Markham against Duke in 2000.

Taylor is up to 12 field goals this season and 43 in his career, which is third all-time in Commodores history. The school record for career made field goals is held by Markham, who made 47 between 1997-00. Taylor made 18 field goals in 2024 and 13 in 2025.

Aria Gerson covers Vanderbilt athletics for The Tennessean. Contact her at agerson@gannett.com or on X @aria_gerson.

Sony Indefinitely Delays Another PS5 Peripheral Days Before Release

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Sony has indefinitely delayed the PS5’s official Pulse Elevate desktop speakers, which were originally due to deploy days from now, on 12th November.

In a brief update on the PS Blog, the manufacturer said that it had “identified a production issue with the product and have made the decision to delay the launch”.

This delay feels eerily similar to the one that affected its FlexStrike arcade stick earlier in the year, which was originally due out on 6th August but ultimately indefinitely postponed.

At the time, the manufacturer said it had encountered “unexpected production delays”, but that was several months ago now and it hasn’t provided an update since.

Sony’s Edwin Foo said of the Pulse Elevate delay:

“The additional time will allow us to ensure the product will deliver the experience we intended for our players. We apologise for the delay and appreciate your patience and understanding. We look forward to bringing Pulse Elevate to players soon.”

The speakers – which are designed for both desktop and portable use – promise “lifelike, ultra-low latency” audio.

What’s particularly bizarre about this delay is that many outlets and influencers received review units earlier this week.

I think I’m allowed to say Push Square received a pair, and SpawnPoiint posted about them on Thursday:

I wonder what went wrong during production, and whether it’s going to have to scrap its entire inventory and start over from scratch on these.

Surely this close to its originally intended release, the $220 product must have made it into warehouses around the world already.
