Calciomercato no stop - Indiscrezioni, trattative e retroscena del 7 novembre

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

SASSUOLO, VALUTAZIONI IN CORSO SU AQUILANI DOPO IL KO CON LA ROMA. E SPUNTA D'AVERSA. DALL'ARGENTINA RILANCIANO: "ICARDI VUOLE TORNARE IN SERIE A, JUVE IN POLE DOPO IL NO DELL'INTER". INTER, RINNOVO CALHANOGLU IN SALITA: DISTANZA SULL'INGAGGIO, JUVENTUS E COMO OSSERVANO. NAPOLI, ANGUISSA NEL MIRINO DEL BESIKTAS DI ITALIANO.

La pesante sconfitta per 3-0 contro la Roma potrebbe avere conseguenze importanti per il futuro di Alberto Aquilani sulla panchina del Sassuolo. Il tecnico neroverde è sempre più in bilico dopo il quinto ko consecutivo, il sesto nelle ultime sette partite. Una crisi di risultati che ha fatto precipitare la situazione e che potrebbe spingere la società a prendere provvedimenti durante la sosta per le nazionali. Come riferito da Sky Sport, per la prima volta dall'inizio della stagione il club valuterà concretamente la possibilità di intervenire sulla guida tecnica, avviando una riflessione approfondita sulla posizione dell'allenatore. Al momento non è stata ancora presa una decisione definitiva, ma la posizione di Aquilani è considerata a rischio. La sosta rappresenterà un'occasione per analizzare il rendimento della squadra e valutare se proseguire con l'attuale allenatore oppure dare una svolta alla stagione. In caso di esonero, uno dei nomi che potrebbe entrare in corsa è quello di Roberto D'Aversa, profilo già accostato alla panchina neroverde. Non si tratta però di una scelta definita: la società avrà tempo per esaminare anche eventuali alternative prima di decidere come affrontare la ripresa del campionato.

Il mercato invernale potrebbe riportare in Italia, stavolta per davvero, un attaccante che in Serie A ha lasciato un segno indelebile. Mauro Icardi vuole rimettersi in gioco nel campionato italiano e la Juventus starebbe valutando concretamente il suo profilo. A rilanciare l'indiscrezione è il portale argentino TyC Sports, secondo cui il club bianconero rappresenterebbe oggi la destinazione più accessibile per il centravanti classe 1993, rimasto senza contratto dopo la conclusione della sua avventura al Galatasaray lo scorso giugno. Prima di guardare altrove, Icardi avrebbe provato a riaprire una porta che per lui ha sempre un significato particolare: quella dell'Inter. L'argentino si sarebbe persino dichiarato disponibile a un taglio importante dello stipendio e ad accettare un ruolo marginale nelle gerarchie offensive di Cristian Chivu. Da Milano, però, è arrivato un rifiuto senza possibilità di interpretazioni. Giuseppe Marotta, prima della sfida contro il Feyenoord, ha infatti escluso categoricamente un possibile ritorno, chiarendo che Icardi non rientra nei programmi della Beneamata. Ecco allora che prende corpo l'ipotesi Vecchia Signora. A rendere particolarmente suggestiva la candidatura bianconera è la presenza di Luciano Spalletti, allenatore che conosce bene Icardi e con il quale l'argentino ha vissuto alcune delle migliori stagioni della propria carriera. Insoddisfatta di Woltemade e Kolo Muani, la Juventus cerca maggiore peso offensivo e proprio un centravanti con esperienza internazionale come Maurito potrebbe rappresentare la soluzione giusta.

Il futuro di Hakan Calhanoglu resta uno dei principali nodi da risolvere in casa Inter. Il centrocampista turco, sotto contratto fino al 30 giugno 2027, vorrebbe continuare la propria esperienza in nerazzurro, ma la trattativa per il prolungamento è ancora in una fase interlocutoria. Come riferisce Tuttosport, ci sono già stati i primi contatti tra il direttore sportivo Dario Baccin e l'agente Gordon Stipic, senza però passi avanti decisivi. La distanza riguarda soprattutto l'aspetto economico. Calhanoglu percepisce circa 6,5 milioni di euro a stagione e vorrebbe un rinnovo biennale a cifre simili. L'Inter, invece, valuta una proposta più contenuta, considerando l'età del giocatore, che compirà 33 anni a febbraio, e i problemi fisici delle ultime stagioni: undici infortuni muscolari e oltre 200 giorni di indisponibilità. La società preferirebbe un accordo annuale con opzione, caratterizzato da una parte fissa più bassa e bonus legati al rendimento. Nei giorni scorsi Giuseppe Marotta aveva comunque rassicurato sull'andamento dei dialoghi: "Non siamo in una situazione di ansia per quanto concerne i rinnovi, c'è solo un giocatore che è in scadenza di contratto, ed è Calhanoglu, con cui abbiamo un ottimo rapporto". Intanto, attorno al centrocampista si muovono diversi club. Oltre agli interessamenti dalla Turchia, senza offerte finora ritenute adeguate, il suo nome è stato accostato alla Juventus. Secondo Tuttosport, anche il Como starebbe valutando il profilo del regista, considerato un possibile innesto d'esperienza per la squadra di Cesc Fabregas.

Il Besiktas di Vincenzo Italiano è in difficoltà: una sola vittoria nelle ultime cinque, contro il Basaksehir il 26 ottobre scorso, poi due sconfitte contro Celtic in Europa e Trabzonspor in Super Lig, poi il pareggio faticoso 2-2 contro il Kasimpasa. Oggi la sfida contro il Genclerbirligi, ma la vetta si allontana e serve vincere per provare a non perdere il treno europeo. Così il tecnico italiano aspetta rinforzi e il primo grande obiettivo è un centrocampista di spessore per il suo reparto mediano. Secondo quanto raccolto da TMW, il primo obiettivo di Italiano sarebbe André Zambo Frank Anguissa del Napoli. Il trentenne di Yaoundé è in scadenza nell'estate che verrà e le trattative per il rinnovo sono più che in stallo. Non solo: il momento del Napoli certo non aiuta e non dà forza alla dirigenza nelle trattative sui nuovi contratti e c'è da immaginare che Anguissa adesso voglia valutare anche un progetto importante a livello sia sportivo che economico per il prosieguo di carriera. Non gli mancano certo le pretendenti, tra paesi arabi, Serie A e grandi leghe europee. Il centrocampista che abbini qualità, quantità, esperienza e struttura manca però in questo momento, quanto meno un altro giocatore di personalità che riesca a dare tanto a un Besiktas che nei momenti di difficoltà fatica a reagire. La differenza tra la qualità del Besiktas e la rosa per esempio del Galatasaray è lampante ed evidente. L'ambizione di Italiano è forte e concreta, i rinforzi per gennaio sono necessari. E quello di Anguissa può essere un nome caldo.

DALL'INGHILTERRA: NO DI DIDIER DESCHAMPS ALLA PANCHINA DEL MANCHESTER UNITED

A poche ore dalla seconda lista dei convocati di Zinedine Zidane sulla panchina della Francia, il nome del suo predecessore Didier Deschamps è tornato a fare capolino. Secondo il noto tabloid inglese Daily Mirror, la dirigenza del Manchester United avrebbe infatti effettuato un tentativo per ingaggiare il tecnico cinquantottenne e sostituire Michael Carrick, sulla graticola per via di un ruolino disastroso in Premier League che vede i Red Devils al 17esimo posto dopo una sola vittoria in 9 giornate. L'ex commissario tecnico dei Bleus, libero da vincoli contrattuali dopo l'addio alla nazionale seguito al quarto posto nei Mondiali del 2026, era considerato il profilo ideale per carisma, palmarès e disponibilità immediata a costo zero. Tuttavia, l'assalto dei dirigenti dei Red Devils si è scontrato con un netto rifiuto: Deschamps ha declinato l'offerta, spiegando di non essere interessato all'incarico e preferendo valutare con calma il proprio futuro.

Texas football: QB Arch Manning has one of season's best games in win vs Missouri

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

COLUMBIA, Mo. - In one of the season's most critical games, Texas football quarterback Arch Manning delivered arguably his most efficient performance.

Manning threw for 298 yards and three touchdowns on 28-of-38 passing in front of a rabid, sold-out crowd Saturday at Memorial Stadium during the Longhorns' 34-20 win. More importantly, he didn't commit a turnover for the first time since Week 3 against UTSA.

With receiver Cam Coleman missing the game with a shoulder injury and Ryan Wingo playing through his own nagging injuries, Manning turned to Emmett Mosley V and his running backs. Mosley led Texas with 85 yards receiving, but Manning's ability to find running backs Raleek Brown and Derrek Cooper helped key the win.

RELATED: How hoops helps Rasheem Biles on Texas' defense

Brown hauled in six catches for 48 yards and a touchdown while Cooper tallied had 24 yards and a touchdown on his one reception.

According to Texas coach Steve Sarkisian, Manning's composure reflected how the team played in every facet of the game. And it's the primary reason why Manning is 8-2 as a starting quarterback against ranked teams, including wins in each of his six past such starts.

"I thought Arch played a heck of a football game for us today," he said. "There are moments where it wasn't perfect. Let's start with that. This was not perfect, but I think the resiliency and the mental toughness of the team showed up today. I thought we were at our best when our best was needed today."

Texas Longhorns tight end Emaree Winston (85) jumps over Missouri Tigers safety Kensley Louidor-Faustin (7) as he carries the ball in the second quarter during the game against Missouri at Faurot Field at Memorial Stadium in Columbia on Saturday, Nov. 7, 2026. But Manning may need to be even better next week, when the New Orleans native makes his first appearance as the starting quarterback in his home state of Louisiana against LSU.

Not that Manning plans on worrying about it too much, at least until the team gets back to Austin.

"I'm going to enjoy this one and enjoy the flight home with the guys," he said. "We all know we got a big one next week."

Murray kicks 40-yard FG, Howard has game-sealing interception as UL Monroe edges Arkansas State

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

JONESBORO, Ark. (AP) — Abram Murray kicked a 40-yard field goal with 1:49 remaining and Gavin Howard had a game-sealing interception with a minute to go as UL Monroe took down Arkansas State 19-17 on Saturday.

The Warhawks (2-7, 2-3 Sun Belt Conference) won their second straight against Arkansas State (4-5, 2-3), which had won the previous 15 before last season's 28-16 ULM win in Monroe.

Austin Carlisle led the Warhawks ground attack with 111 rushing yards on 19 carries, and Don Chaney added 68 on 12. Carlisle also completed all four of his passing attempts for 31 yards.

Taking over with just under six minutes remaining after an Arkansas State field goal made it 17-16, the Warhawks marched down the field in seven plays and ran off almost four minutes of clock to set up Murray's 40-yarder.

Arkansas State took over and got to their own 44-yard line before Howard intercepted a Trey Owens pass.

ULM picked up the one first down they needed to secure the win.

Owens was 16-of-29 passing for 193 yards and a touchdown, connecting with Jaylen Bonelli on three passes for 66 yards. He threw two interceptions and fumbled once.

UL Monroe: vs. Louisiana-Lafayette on Thursday.

Arkansas State: at Coastal Carolina next Saturday.

UCLA stays unbeaten by speeding past Minnesota with a two-play, 150-yard burst by Aidan Mizell

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

MINNEAPOLIS (AP) — Once Ty Dieffenbach saw the Minnesota secondary align and react the way he expected, it was time for the UCLA quarterback to let it fly.

Aidan Mizell was going to run it down. Dieffenbach had no doubt about that. And when the Bruins got the ball back about five minutes later, well, it was time to try it again.

With two 75-yard touchdown passes from Dieffenbach to Mizell on consecutive possessions in the fourth quarter, the Bruins sped away from the Gophers and showed the Big Ten — and the College Football Playoff selection committee — yet another strength of their unbeaten breakout team in the 34-20 victory on Saturday.

"We knew it was going to be a big finding-the-gaps-in-the-defense game, and we just worked all week on the timing," said Dieffenbach, who had a career-high 303 yards and three touchdowns on just eight completions.

Said Mizell: "We knew the whole game we were able to get behind the DB. We were just waiting for the right time to call it."

Mizell, a transfer from Florida who entered the week with only seven receptions all season developed a strong connection during training camp with Dieffenbach while they worked frequently with the backups. Dieffenbach, who took over last month for the injured Nico Iamaleava, quickly realized he could hardly overthrow the 6-foot-1 senior.

"He can really run, so you've just got to put it out there for him," Dieffenbach said.

Bruins coach Bob Chesney said offensive coordinator Dean Kennedy was trying to set up those plays to Mizell earlier in the game, and the strategy clearly worked on the Gophers — undoing more than 47 minutes of solid defense that had the game tied at 17 early in the fourth quarter.

"How we practice is how we play, and we knew by the way we practiced this week it was over with," Mizell said.

On the first one, the Gophers were in a two-high formation with quarters zone coverage underneath. But Mizell caught safety Kerry Brown flat-footed and blew by him on a post route, and safety Zack Harden — who was supposed to be playing deeper — bit on a crossing route in front of him.

On the second one, both Harden and safety Aidan Gousby lurched forward on the run fake by Dieffenbach. That left no safety help for cornerback Aydan West, who was badly beaten by Mizell on a post route from the other side of the field.

"When you have a blown coverage, you're going to get beat," Gophers coach P.J. Fleck said. "We've got to have depth to the defense, and we didn't have it."

Papua New Guinea’s Most Expensive Film, ‘Mussau: The First Song,’ Wraps Shoot, Unveils First Looks (EXCLUSIVE)

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Indian filmmaker Bijukumar Damodaran directs the $4.5 million period drama, shot on remote Mussau Island and in Goroka, which is eyeing a 2027 festival premiere.

Papua New Guinea’s most expensive film to date, the $4.5 million historical drama “Mussau: The First Song,” has wrapped principal photography after a 40-day shoot across two provinces.

Filming took place on Mussau Island in New Ireland Province and in Goroka, in Eastern Highlands Province. PNG-based producer NAFA Productions celebrated the wrap at a Thanksgiving event in Port Moresby on Saturday. Government figures at the event included Belden Norman Namah, Minister for Arts, Culture and Tourism; Chief Secretary Ivan Pomaleu; and Chief Censor Jim Abani. Related Stories Taylor Swift's 'I Knew It, I Knew You' and 'La Bola Negra's 'La Nieve' Among Nominees for Hollywood Music In Media Awards

Indian director Bijukumar Damodaran, a three-time Indian National Film Award winner, is at the helm. Parul Agrawal produces, and Noelene Taula Wunum is executive producer.

Inspired by a true story and set in the 1930s, the film centers on Taula Livovoa, a young islander who walks away from tribal violence and takes up a faith that is new to him. His choice sets off a sweeping social change that the producers rank among the South Pacific’s most remarkable.

The dialogue is in six languages: Mussau, Emirau, Agarabi, Gadsup, Tok Pisin and English. Some of them are indigenous languages with small numbers of speakers that are at risk of dying out. The producers present the film as a way to protect the country’s linguistic and cultural heritage and bring it to a global audience.

Much of the cast was drawn from the local community, and many are acting on screen for the first time. Some had never watched a film before. Several roles are played by great-grandchildren of the real people whose lives inspired the story. Two British actors round out the ensemble.

Mussau Island has little infrastructure and patchy communications, which made the shoot hard to pull off. The 120-person crew, 20 of them technicians from India, spent 30 hours at sea crossing the Pacific to reach the island. Temporary lodging and facilities were set up there. Islanders helped throughout, including on sequences shot in the sea at high tide. The unit then moved on to Goroka to finish filming.

“This has been one of the most challenging and rewarding journeys of my filmmaking career,” Damodaran said. “The people of Mussau welcomed us into their lives, their culture and their history, and their contribution has been invaluable. Having descendants of the original historical figures portray some of these characters, alongside community members who had never seen a film before, made this journey even more profound.”

Hilda Tanimia wrote the story. The screenplay is by Jennifer Litau, who also served as translator, along with Joses Imona and Damodaran. The cinematographer is Yedhu Radhakrishnan and the editor is Davis Palakal Manuel. Arnold Laena handled production design and Gladice John designed the costumes. The music comes from Music Masters of PNG and the Murat Choir. The cast includes Freddy Sutchiff, Candy Ali, Daniel Lee Hird, Kevin Jackson, Jamie Napo, Calvin, Alan Balalua, Frank Eke and Dolvin Aite.

NAFA Productions wants the film to bow at a top-tier international festival in 2027. It also hopes the film will be Papua New Guinea’s official entry for best international feature at the 100th Academy Awards.

Damodaran has made that run once before. His earlier PNG–India co-production “Papa Buka” was the first film Papua New Guinea ever submitted to the Oscars’ international feature race.

Can Verstappen, Hamilton come from the back in Sao Paulo 'reverse grid' race?

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Lando Norris starts on pole in Brazil from Kimi Antonelli but there will be plenty of attention further down the field with Max Verstappen and Lewis Hamilton starting at the back; watch Sunday's Sao Paulo Grand Prix live at 5pm on Sky Sports F1, build-up from 3.30pm

Sports Journalist @NigelCJourno

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

A mixed up grid and rain on the forecast has set up the prospect of a thrilling Sao Paulo Grand Prix on Sunday.

For the third year in a row, Lando Norris will start on pole position in Brazil with new F1 world champion Kimi Antonelli alongside him on the front row.

Unusually though, there will be plenty of attention further down the field as power unit changes for Red Bull's Max Verstappen and Ferrari's Lewis Hamilton mean they start at the back, while Ferrari's Charles Leclerc starts 14th for a 10-grid place penalty.

And Mercedes' George Russell, who was left frustrated to not qualify first after a strategic error from the team, starts seventh following a five-place grid penalty for a new internal combustion engine, so several of F1's biggest names will begin the Sao Paulo Grand Prix - lights out at 5pm on Sunday with build-up from 3.30pm, live on Sky Sports F1 - out of position.

"It's almost a reverse grid race, no?" Mercedes team principal Toto Wolff told Sky Sports F1.

"Yeah, exciting. I think we want to lock in the points for the Constructors' Championship. But it's all floodgates open, the better. Let's race."

1) Lando Norris, McLaren2) Kimi Antonelli,, Mercedes3) Liam Lawson, Racing Bulls4) Nico Hulkenberg, Audi5) Isack Hadjar, Red Bull6) Oliver Bearman, Haas7) George Russell, Mercedes8) Fernando Alonso, Aston Martin9) Alex Albon, Williams10) Oscar Piastri, McLaren

Verstappen is no stranger to making his way through the pack at Interlagos after his remarkable victory from 17th in 2024 in the rain.

The Red Bull driver, who has won three times in Brazil, gained 11 places in the opening 11 laps before making the right strategy call to take a crucial victory.

Last year, Verstappen started from the pit lane and impressively charged his way to the podium in dry conditions, but he thinks it will be more difficult to get towards the front this time around.

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

"It's going to be a bit harder to overtake. The Overtake Mode doesn't really do a lot around here. Everyone is draining their battery from the last corner to Turn 1. Everyone has their wings open, so the effect is less than normal," he told Sky Sports F1.

"That will make it a bit harder to overtake once you get to the cars that are similar on lap times. The first few cars are not a problem. The others will be a little harder."

Overtake Mode has been a replacement for DRS since the start of this year.It’s a mode the car goes into when you are within one second of the car in front.It allows the following car a higher car speed while still deploying maximum energy from the electric motor.On a normal lap, when the car reaches 180mph, it has to, by regulation, ramp down its electric motor power. With Overtake Mode active, that speed tolerance raises to 209mph, so the following car is allowed to go faster.

Hamilton memorably went from 20th to fifth in the 2021 Sprint in Brazil, after he was disqualified from Qualifying, then won the Grand Prix after starting 10th.

However, Hamilton will not have the same car advantage from five years ago with Ferrari struggling this weekend compared to their recent form.

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

But, the high chance of rain means Hamilton, and Verstappen, will have the chance to showcase their extraordinary skill and could still find themselves towards the pointy end of the field with a special performance.

"It will be tricky conditions but I love racing in the rain," said Hamilton.

"I was basically using the qualifying as a test to make sure the engine is working, and making sure the car felt good. I feel good for tomorrow."

Leclerc has less cars to overtake from 14th and feels he learned a lot from the wet qualifying by experimenting with set-up tweaks which will benefit him in the race.

"It's going to be very tricky. My biggest strength in the junior categories has been wet driving. Since my nine years with Ferrari, we have never found a way around it," he told Sky Sports F1.

"I still believe if we manage to find the right window of this car, we can fight for much better than top six."

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

Perhaps quite fittingly, last year's F1 world champion Norris will start on pole from the new world champion Antonelli in the first race since the latter secured this year's title a week ago in Mexico.

McLaren have brought a new front wing, rear wing, beam wing and floor elements to their car this weekend and it appears to have made an immediate performance impact.

Norris and Antonelli traded lap times throughout Friday's practice sessions in the dry but the McLaren driver feels he needs rain to beat Antonelli on Sunday.

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

"I expect a battle no matter what. If it's dry, I probably expect a bigger battle because I don't think we quite have the speed compared to them," he told Sky Sports F1.

"That's why I feel like we had a very good day today. We made the most of conditions where it's not so much about who has the best car, it's more who can push things to the limit and make the most of that opportunity.

"I'm hoping when I wake up it's a dark, gloomy day because we will have a better chance then."

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

Norris pointed to Antonelli's impressive long run pace in the dry on Friday as the reason he's hoping for rain, whereas the Mercedes driver says he's open to any conditions.

"Hopefully, we can get off the line well, and let's see if it's going to be wet or dry," said Antonelli.

"In the dry, I felt very good over a single lap and on the long run, especially in FP2. So let's see. I'm looking forward to it.

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

Mercedes have a big opportunity to win the Constructors' Championship with Antonelli starting in second and Russell in seventh, while the two Ferraris are outside of the top 13.

If Mercedes outscore Ferrari by three points, or more, they will seal their ninth title since 2014 but the result of the race itself appears to be more important to the drivers, and Russell is hoping for more rain following his impressive qualifying.

"I felt really confident in the rain. I think my pace, along with Lando's, was by far the best out there," he said

"In the rain here, it's always a challenge. I hope it is wet. I feel I've got a greater chance if it's wet."

The NFL splits the funds it collects from player fines between the NFLPA's initiatives to support retired players and the NFL Foundation and other organizations that support youth football programs, player safety initiatives, and community health efforts.

MLB free agency: Six international players to know for the 2026-27 offseason

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

Major League Baseball's free agency period opened earlier this week and teams are now clear to pursue any roster upgrades they deem necessary. That includes players coming over from Japan's Nippon Professional Baseball league, South Korea's Korea Baseball Organization, and Taiwan's Chinese Professional Baseball League. Those are high-level leagues that have sent many impact players MLB's way over the years.

Look no farther than the Dodgers to see how important international talent can be to a club. The Dodgers employed four different former NPB and KBO players this past season, including two-way star Shohei Ohtani, ace Yoshinobu Yamamoto, and starter Roki Sasaki. Ohtani was signed as an MLB free agent, but the others went to the Dodgers through the posting system.

Per the posting agreement with MLB, posted NPB and CPBL players get a 45-day window to negotiate and sign with an MLB team. KBO players get a 30-day window. Players who do not sign during that posting window return to their previous team in NPB, CPBL, or KBO. The MLB team that signs a posted player pays a fee to the player's former NPB, CPBL, or KBO team.

$5M plus 17.5% of contract value over $25M

$9.375M plus 15% of contract value over $50M

For example, the White Sox paid Munetaka Murakami's former team, the Yakult Swallows, a $6.575 million posting fee on his $34 million contract last offseason. That's $5 million on the first $25 million plus 17.5% of the remaining $9 million, which is $1.575 million. The $5 million plus $1.575 million equals the $6.575 million posting fee paid to the Swallows.

Unlike last lockout, MLB will not be pausing the signing window during the expected lockout, according to The Athletic. In other words, if owners lock players out on Dec. 1 as expected, that date will essentially serve as the same deadline for already posted NPB players unless the lockout ends before their 45-day window expires.

There are no superstar-caliber players like Ohtani or Yamamoto available in this winter's international free agent crop. There are, however, several interesting players who can improve an MLB roster in 2027. Here are the six best NPB, CPBL, and KBO players to know this offseason.

Japan's Triple Crown winner hit .306/.383/.609 with 39 home runs in 2026. He has a similar skill set to Murakami but is not exactly Murakami 2.0. He doesn't have quite as much power (but has plenty of it) and won't walk nearly as often, but he does swing and miss a bunch and is unlikely to remain at the hot corner long-term. Sato is a very aggressive hitter who ran a 66% contact rate this past season. That is below the 77% MLB average. The consensus seems to be that he will land at first base or in a corner outfield spot if not next year, then soon. Murakami successfully overcame his contact issues to slug 35 homers in only 127 games in 2026 around a hamstring injury. At minimum, that will have MLB teams reconsidering how they evaluate the Japan-to-MLB transition. Like Murakami, Sato carries risk, and like Murakami, his power makes that risk worth the dice roll. The Hanshin Tigers officially posted Sato on Nov. 1.

Listed at 5-foot-8, Taira gets outlier extension down the mound (close to seven feet despite his height), which creates a low release window. Hitters rarely see the ball come from where Taira releases it. He's not a gimmick pitcher either. Taira sits mid-90s with his four-seam fastball and backs it up with a cutter, sinker, sweeper, slider, changeup, and splitter. The sweeper and slider are distinct pitches, as are the changeup and splitter. The Seibu Lions used Taira as a reliever in 2024 and 2025, then started him in 2026, and he handled the assignment well. The breaking balls are just OK, and the fact of the matter is that pitchers this height don't often hold up under a starter's workload. I would assume the team that signs Taira will give him a chance to start. In the long run, though, he might be a reliever, perhaps even a multi-inning setup man. Taira was officially posted on Nov. 1.

A down 2026 season took some of the shine off Itoh, who won the 2025 Eiji Sawamura Award (Japan's Cy Young equivalent) after throwing 196 ⅔ innings with a 2.52 ERA. To be sure, Itoh pitched well in 2026 (3.18 ERA in 172 ⅔ innings), but he was not as consistently dominant as in the past. The 5-foot-9 right-hander is a seven-pitch pitcher with a low-90s fastball and the usual array of secondary pitches (sinker, cutter, curveball, slider, sweeper, splitter), none of which stands out as a plus offering. Itoh has thrown at least 150 innings in each of the last six seasons. He's seen as a crafty back-end starter in MLB, or perhaps even a reliever (Itoh sat 96 mph out of the bullpen in the World Baseball Classic). The Hokkaido Nippon-Ham Fighters posted Itoh on Nov. 1.

Roh has an unusual agreement with the Hanwha Eagles. He signed the richest deal in KBO history in February (11 years and roughly $23 million), and as part of the deal, the Eagles agreed to post him for MLB teams this offseason, and Roh agreed to return to the Eagles to finish his career when his big-league stint is over. This past season, Roh, 26 in November, authored a .283/.360/.505 batting line with 29 home runs. He has real right-handed power and a good approach at the plate. There are concerns about his propensity to swing and miss as well as his arm at the hot corner. The Eagles formally posted Roh on Nov. 1.

The Seibu Lions posted Takahashi last offseason, but he was unable to find a contract to his liking, so he returned to Japan for another season and threw 148 innings with a 2.64 ERA. Takahashi, 30 in February, is an international free agent now, meaning he does not have to be posted and can instead negotiate at his own pace. More of a ground ball pitcher than a bat-misser, Takahashi sits in the 91-95 mph range with his fastball and has two good secondary pitches in his slider and splitter. He also throws a cutter and curveball. Takahashi is considered a potential innings guy for the back of an MLB rotation.

Tseng pitched for Chinese Taipei in the World Baseball Classic in 2023 and 2026 and he has been one of the CPBL's top relievers the last few years. In 2026, he saved 25 games with a 1.80 ERA and 65 strikeouts in 52 ⅓ innings. Tseng's fastball typically sits in the mid-90s and has run as high as 100 mph in the past, though it has been a few years. His go-to secondary pitch is a breaking ball that needs improvement for Tseng to carve out an MLB career as more than a middle reliever.

Future Hall of Famer Max Scherzer will pitch in "lockout games" this offseason to determine whether he will continue his career, reports the New York Post. U2’s The Edge Wrote an ‘Elf’ Sequel, But It ‘Petered Out and Never Happened,’ Says Andy Richter

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

According to Andy Richter, U2’s the Edge penned a script for a sequel to “Elf” — but fans will likely never get to see (or read) it.

On a recent episode of “Obsessed: The Podcast,” the actor explained that Shauna Robertson, who’d produced the original film, once received a message saying that the band member, also known as David Howell Evans, had written a script for “Elf 2” Related Stories Taylor Swift's 'I Knew It, I Knew You' and 'La Bola Negra's 'La Nieve' Among Nominees for Hollywood Music In Media Awards

“Somebody reached out to her and said, ‘The Edge from U2 has written a sequel to ‘Elf,’ and he wants to talk to you about it,’” Richter said. “It’s the weirdest story, but apparently it was fully written. Somewhere in the world there is an Edge-penned ‘Elf 2’ out there… I would love for that to make the light of day. It never happened. It’s one of those things where she’s like, ‘All right, sure, I’ll meet him,’ and then it just petered out and never happened.”

In addition to the Edge, “Old School” and “The Hangover Part II” writer Scot Armstrong penned a separate sequel script about “the evil South Pole elves kidnapping Santa or something like that,” said Richter. (He also noted that Armstrong had helped write “Elf,” but wasn’t credited for guild and procedural reasons.)

And yet despite how beloved the holiday classic remains 23 years later, Richter is steadfast in the belief that a sequel isn’t necessary at this point. “I’m on the side of ‘Let it stand,’” he said. ”Just let ‘Elf’ be ‘Elf.’ You don’t have to use every part of the animal.”

He continued: “Now that time has passed, the movie has become so special that I think that people, rightfully so, are loath to mess with it because we all can think of movies that have been tarnished by their sequels. And this is so special and so perfect and so beloved… Why risk it?”

“Elf,” directed by Jon Favreau, originally hit theaters in 2003, starring Will Ferrell, James Caan, Zooey Deschanel, Mary Steenburgen and others. The Christmas classic followed Buddy the elf (Ferrell), who travels from the North Pole to New York City to meet his biological father, Walter Hobbs (Caan).

Ritcher appeared briefly in the film as one of Hobbs’ colleagues and co-wrote the book “Elf Unwrapped: Behind the Scenes of a Modern Christmas Classic,” released Tuesday.

The top goalscorers for club & country in 2026 so far: Kane, Yamal, Mbappe, Raphinha…

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Big names from Real Madrid, Barcelona, Arsenal and Bayern Munich feature alongside some surprise names in the most prolific goalscorers of the calendar year so far.

Some big names are surprisingly absent from the most prolific goalscorers of 2026 to date.

Drawn from Europe’s five major leagues, here are the top 10 goalscorers in 2026 across club and international competitions.

Schick has had reason to celebrate in 2026. Earlier this year, he reached the milestone of 100 goals for Bayer Leverkusen.

He’s been on fire this season, with 11 goals from 13 games for the German side.

There won’t be any more international goals from Schick, though. He retired from Czech Republic duty after the World Cup.

The jury is still out on Gyokeres, who remains a perplexing forward.

This calendar year has seen him look borderline anonymous and increasingly sidelined at Arsenal.

And yet Gyokeres also fired Sweden to the World Cup with a hat-trick against Ukraine and a late winner against Poland. He’s basically the Ikea Gerd Muller at this point.

Dembele has scored 20 goals in 36 PSG appearances this year, including several crucial strikes in Europe – none bigger than the equalising spot kick he coolly dispatched against Arsenal in the Champions League final.

His goal in France’s World Cup win over Iraq was, incredibly, his first in major tournament football. That was swiftly followed up with a rapid hat-trick against Norway, before another in the quarter-final against Morocco.

He is largely taking it easy right now, once again frequently rested during PSG’s domestic campaign. But he popped up with a goal against Manchester City.

What a year it’s been for Malen. Started it as a benchwarmer at Aston Villa. Set to finish it as one of Europe’s most lethal strikers at Roma.

Malen made an immediate impact after his January move to the Italian capital and his scoring rate has barely slowed down since.

For some reason, his form hasn’t quite translated into international football; he hasn’t scored for the Netherlands at all this year.

But high-flying Roma are laughing. Malen almost won the Serie A golden boot last season despite arriving midway through and he’s a leading contender for the gong this season.

A fairly recent entrant into the top 10 after heading into the international break on the back of a hat trick against Union Berlin.

Olise was the Bundesliga’s best assister last season but has been more than capable of finding the net himself a fair few times too.

He didn’t score any goals at the World Cup but netted the winner in Zinedine Zidane’s second game in charge of France. The best Fantasy Footballer around right now.

Yamal has long been talked up as a Ballon d’Or contender, and slowly but surely he’s getting the numbers to back it up.

The teenager’s goalscoring output has grown season on season, and 2025-26 was no exception. February’s hat-trick against Villarreal was evidence of a player about to go truly stratospheric.

Unfortunately, the final furlong of the campaign was hit by a hamstring injury, or else we suspect he’d be a few places higher up in this list.

He needed just 10 minutes in his first World Cup start to find the back of the net, although that would be his only goal in Spain’s triumph out in North America.

Now he’s looking fit and hungry – at least based on the evidence of his exceptional form of late.

The Brazilian has an exceptional tally of 33 goals for Barcelona so far this calendar year (plus one for Brazil), which is especially impressive when you consider he’s missed quite a lot of action due to injury.

He averages a goal every 49 minutes for Barca this season – to put that in context, Kylian Mbappe averages a goal every 84 minutes for Real Madrid.

Raphinha has scored in all but three of the Barca games he’s played in in 2026-27, scoring hat tricks in two, and currently leads the Europe-wide goalscoring charts with 15 in the league.

The top three are the last three winners of the European Golden Shoe. This list has taken a while to take shape, but the frontrunners are the usual suspects.

Haaland’s four goals against Iraq and Senegal in the group stages finally saw him climb up into the top 10, from which he was surprisingly absent in the latter half of the 2025-26 club season, before he added to his World Cup tally against the Ivory Coast. Then there were two goals against Brazil.

He finally drew a blank as Norway exited the World Cup at the hands of England at the quarter-final stage. He earned a well-deserved rest before spearheading Man City’s new era under Enzo Maresca.

Same as it ever was with nine goals in nine Premier League appearances for City this season, plus braces against Porto and PSG and another Champions League goal against his favourite opponents, RB Leipzig.

Like his rival Haaland, it somehow took Mbappe until mid-June to enter the top 10.

Mbappe kicked off the year in superb style, scoring in six successive starts for Real Madrid (nine goals in total) before dipping considerably with just one goal in his next seven appearances.

By the nature of his 10 goals at the World Cup, we don’t expect to see the Real Madrid superstar dropping back out. His Champions League record is pretty sensational.

Arguably the finest player in world football right now. How often have you been able to say that about an Englishman?

The only player in Europe’s five major leagues to average a better-than-goal-a-game output so far this calendar year, the England captain has 41 goals in 40 appearances for Bayern Munich in all competitions in 2026 – including home and away against Real Madrid and PSG.

He capped off his superb season with a hat-trick in the DFB Pokal final. Two goals against Croatia showed Kane carried his form into the World Cup, where he eventually scored six times.

Unbelievably, the Norway game was Kane’s first knockout appearance for club and country in 2025-26 in which he didn’t score. Which, of course, he followed with a blank against Argentina.

Surprisingly enough, Kane failed to add to his tally by drawing a blank in his first three Bayern appearances of the 2026-27 campaign.

But it’s back to normal service with his Bayern tally standing at 10 goals in 14 games. The Czechs and Croatia have been on the receiving end in the Nations League too.

Still some way to go before catching Lionel Messi’s calendar year record, mind.

I Tried Hailey Bieber’s Favorite Creatine Gummies for Two Months, and I Have Thoughts

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Every product on this page was chosen by a Harper's BAZAAR editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Why these candylike gumdrops have converted me into a creatine-lover—plus, the other formulas I love

The supplement landscape has never been murkier, more confusing, or more overwhelming. While I have a pretty extensive wellness regimen, it feels like there are a million and one powders, pills, and gummies I “should” be adding to my routine. One supplement that keeps coming into the spotlight is creatine.

I first heard about creatine from my 62-year-old mother, who waxed poetic about the supplement. As someone who isn’t one to experiment with supplements, my ears perked up. Then, the influencer of all influencers, Hailey Bieber, posted on Instagram a few weeks later touting the same supplement—this time, a gummy version from Arrae. I thought: That’s it, I’m taking the creatine plunge.

“Creatine works like an energy recycler,” says Roxanne Pero, a Dallas-based obstetrician and gynecologist and part of O Positiv’s Medical Advisory Board. “Every cell in the body needs energy, and that energy comes from a molecule called ATP, or adenosine triphosphate. Creatine helps the body regenerate ATP more quickly, especially when energy runs low, and especially in tissues that use a lot of it, like muscle and the brain.”

As someone who works out five times a week, often in heated classes that push my body to depletion, I figured I was the perfect candidate for creatine. However, creatine is one of those supplements anyone can benefit from; it kicks in when our cells need more energy, like during strenuous exercise, but also during sleep deprivation and stress—and even perimenopause, menopause, and postpartum for women—which everyone experiences at some point.

“Active women in their 20s and 30s may benefit from improved training performance, while women approaching menopause or experiencing it may be interested in supporting muscle strength as part of a broader strategy that includes resistance training and adequate protein,” says Rachel Brief, a registered dietitian and wellness advisor at the Lanby. However, the supplement has gained traction among women in their 40s and above. “As a woman enters menopause and postmenopause, the decrease in estrogen contributes to age-related muscle and strength loss,” says Shilpa Raut, the vice president of R&D at Cymbiotika, who holds a master’s degree and a PhD in pharmaceutical sciences. Creatine can help women maintain muscle mass, support steady metabolic health, and improve cognitive resilience, which often declines with age.

If you’re wondering why everyone isn’t already taking this “magic” supplement, well, I was too. I quickly discovered that this “gym bro” supplement had a bad reputation for causing bloating, which is not a risk I’m willing to take as a girl with a sensitive gut. However, Bieber’s favorite creatine, Arrae’s Tone gummies, are known for the benefits without the bloat. Not to mention, the brand swaps the typical chalky, white powder that creatine conjures for candylike gummies. “Creatine had always been marketed to men, because no one had ever presented it in a way that felt like it was made for women,” Arrae’s cofounder Siffat Haider tells me. “Our customers adopted it immediately, and that demand is why we kept building on the flavors.”

The one caveat with creatine is that you need to drink water to get the full benefits. “Creatine draws water into muscle cells, and that is part of how it works,” says Michael Fredericson, a sports-medicine physiatrist, professor at Stanford University, and Elysium Scientific Advisory Board member. If you’re dehydrated, creatine will pull your body’s existing water supply into your muscles, causing dehydration and hindering normal bodily functions.

After I incorporated Arrae Tone into my routine for two months and subsequently told people I started taking creatine, everyone asked me the same question: “Does it actually work?” My verdict: I don’t plan to stop taking creatine. The Arrae gummies have no chalky taste and are easy to swallow. I take a lot of different powders and pills, so having a delicious gummy reminiscent of my childhood is a nice change of pace. Plus, the dosage is only 3.75 to 5 grams (depending on whether you take three or four gummies), which is on the lower end of the dosage spectrum and has been proven to be effective without causing bloating. Plus, it’s creatine monohydrate, which is the gold standard of creatine, Brief explains.

Experts say creatine is especially beneficial as a supplement to strength-training workouts, so I was excited to see how it amplified my workouts. Even a few weeks into taking creatine, I noticed more definition in my abdominals and arms, areas where I’ve historically struggled to see muscle. When I want to add a powder to a smoothie or my water to boost hydration, I love the Elysium Health powders; they’re only 5 grams, so they’re easy on my stomach, and they come in delicious fruity flavors that taste like nostalgic lemonade.

Florida State reportedly doesn’t want Deion Sanders for one reason, but it doesn’t make sense

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Florida State reportedly doesn't want Deion Sanders for one reason, but it doesn't make sense originally appeared on The Sporting News. Add The Sporting News as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

With Florida State now having a job opening after moving on from head coach Mike Norvell, one of the first names brought up is Colorado head coach Deion Sanders.

Sanders, arguably the greatest player in Florida State history, seems like a strong candidate from the outside. However, it's also fair to say Sanders isn't loved by everyone, and his below-average tenure at Colorado has only made that worse.

Colorado has been one of the worst teams in college football over the past couple of years, finishing 3-9 a season ago and now sitting at 3-5.

The Buffaloes also went 1-8 in the Big 12 last season and are currently 1-4.

Simply put, there's a lot that Colorado has to figure out, and if Florida State wants to turn this thing around right away, Sanders hasn't proven that he can accomplish that.

According to the latest report, there are some other factors at play here, though I question whether those are fair.

"While there is an appetite for someone who is an 'FSU guy,' it has been relayed from the top down that some of the baggage that comes with Deion is not desired as they commence this rebuild," Noles247 insider Kolby Crawford told CBS Sports on Monday.

I have to question this because Coach Prime has always been like this. This isn't somebody who has just changed his ways over the past couple of years. All Florida State fans loved him when he was with the program because of what he was able to do and the attitude he had on and off the field.

Sanders has never been a bad guy by any means, but he's always believed he's the best on the field, and when he was a player, that was accurate 99.9% of the time.

I feel like he made a name for himself at Florida State because of a different kind of attitude that others don't have, so saying fans wouldn't like that doesn't feel fair to me.

Whether there are other reasons they don't want him is one thing, but this one seems off.

The Chicks Recall ‘the Incident’ and Compare Bush to Trump in ‘Taking the Long Way’ 20th Anniversary Shows: Concert Review

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Senior Music Writer and Chief Music Critic

Natalie Maines does not normally talk a great deal in concert. This may sound counterintuitive to say, since objectively there is no person in music history more famous for talking in concert than Natalie Maines. But she has always been more of a quality than quantity gal when it comes to that, so her legendary 2003 comment about being ashamed to share a home state with then-president George W. Bush was more of an anomaly than an indicator that she or her bandmates are going to be prone to lengthy speeches at any given concert by the Chicks. Related Stories 'The Mosquito Bowl' Review: Football Heroes Take to the Battlefield in Peter Berg's Emptily Macho WWII Epic 'A Few Good Men' Review: Broadway Revival Breathes New Life Into Aaron Sorkin's Courtroom Classic

Fortunately, the short current tour the group is wrapping up was custom-built to feature a steady stream of personal asides, which are welcome, if you happen to be a fan of the trio as personalities and not solely as musical talents. The main reason for that is that the outing is officially a celebration of the 20th anniversary of “Taking the Long Way,” the 2006 record that won five Grammys in total, including a rare Grammys sweep of the top three categories, album, record and song of the year. Not much was revealed in advance about the exact format of the tour. As it turned out, the Chicks (nee: Dixie Chicks) were not only down for spending the first two-thirds of the concert performing “Taking the Long Way” in its 14-song entirety, but for providing between-song commentary on most of the tracks, along with background on the preceding events that ultimately led to this left turn of an album — including “the incident,” as the Bush flap has been euphemistically known in band circles for 23 years.

This made for a rare treat, as Maines, Martie Maguire and Emily Strayer sat on stools on a riser for an hour and a half and went deep into “Storytellers” mode, engaging in a mixture of somewhat pre-scripted and fairly spontaneous dialogue before nearly all of the songs, also sometimes putting up documentary film clips on the big screen to further illustrate what was going down in the studio in the mid-2000s. (Some of the clips were from their “Shut Up and Sing” documentary, but many were unreleased.) The show fell into that rare sub-genre many of us love from a veteran performer: the docu-concert.

But the other reason for some added chatter during the tour was the timing, which was coincidental (seemingly?), leading up to — and, in Los Angeles, overlapping — the 2026 midterm elections. Their three-night stand at the Dolby Theatre in Hollywood began Sunday, two nights before the nation went to the polls, followed by a show on election night Tuesday, with a wrap-up of the stint and the tour scheduled for Wednesday. (Wednesday’s show will be a make-up for the one that was supposed to happen Monday, but got canceled in the hours beforehand due to an unspecified band illness.)

Naturally, the topical events of this week were bound to inspire a few thoughts comparing the current climate with the heated temperature that resulted in a lot of attempted Chick-broiling in 2003-4.

“It was 20 years ago, and a lot has changed since then,” Maines told the Dolby crowd near the start of Sunday’s show. “Now I would give George Bush a big kiss. Now we’re ashamed the President of the United States from the United States.” (Maines had made similar remarks earlier in the tour, and noted that these had been publicized enough that the White House issued an official statement “calling me a peanut head… or peanut brain, I can’t remember.” For the record, White House spokesperson Davis Ingle said on Oct. 15, “Natalie Maines is a despicable nobody who clearly suffers from a severe case of Trump Derangement Syndrome that has completely rotted her peanut-sized brain.”)

The crowd of 3,400, skewed disproportionately toward progressive cowgirls, went nuts. As they did later in the first act, when, before singing “I Hope,” Maines shared that they had recently visited the Obama museum, which gave them hope of again seeing a country that is not “run by a deranged nutjob.”

The only other overt reference to current events came during the second act — the greatest-hits set — when Maines sang “White Trash Wedding” and followed the time-honored opening line “You can’t afford no ring” with the contemporary addendum: “Because gas prices are too high! And electricity. And medical!” They expounded upon this a little more during Tuesday night’s show, when all three members fanned themselves with large “Fuck Trump” fans.

There’s one word to describe the trio’s nearly quarter-century history of not backing down: Nice.

Not every artist who does a full-album throwback show plays the original record in order; some rearrange the sequence to put a big single at the end. But the Chicks adhered to original running order, which meant that “Not Ready to Make Nice” provided a natural climax to the show only three songs in. And anyway, sometimes gratification that isn’t delayed is the best kind. So when Maines broke from the visage of chagrin and anger that characterizes most of “Not Ready…” to bust into a defiant smile after admitting “I kinda like it,” we’d gotten the most cathartic moment of the show out of the way less than 15 minutes into a two-and-a-half-hour gig. And we kinda liked it, because after all, act-2 favorites like “Sin Wagon” and “Goodbye Earl” don’t exactly count as anticlimactic.

Any serious fan of the group might have heard those latter barnburners in concert enough for one lifetime… not that retributive murder and taunting fornication ever get too old. But the main draw of this show for a more hardcore Chick-aholic was as it usually is for veteran acts’ full-album gigs: the promise of long-ignored deep tracks as inherently part of the concept. And “Taking the Long Way” certainly has its recently unplumbed depths. Four of the songs from the 2006 album have remained staples of the group’s sets: the quasi-title track “The Long Way Around,” the balladic “Easy Silence,” the rocking “Lubbock or Leave it” and, of course, “Not Ready.” That left 10 the group has not gone back to in a show for at least 10 years and, in several cases, 20.

Or never. “Baby Hold On” is apparently the one track the group never performed live before this tour (at least according to setlists.fm). Four had not been played since the arena tour of 2016: “Everybody Knows,” “Silent House,” “Favorite Year” and “I Like It.” Another, “Lullaby,” had not mollified an audience since 2014. And four hadn’t been performed since the year the album came out, two decades ago: “So Hard,” “I Hope,” “Bitter End” and “Voice Inside My Head.” In fact, the last two of those had apparently only ever been performed live once. All to say: the Chicks were taking the long way back around to a huge part of this setlist.

How’s it holding up, anyway? Probably everyone can think of Grammy album of the year winners that seemed like a good idea at the time, to somebody, but haven’t dated well. And it would be wrong to imagine that “Taking the Long Way” made it into the winners’ circle solely on musical merits, when there was clearly an appetite to give the Chicks a medal of valor for all that they’d put up with on top of a slew of Grammys. Even I, not having listened to the full album in quite a while, approached this concert playback with a touch of trepidation: What if it didn’t live up to memories and it felt like the Recording Academy had given out all that gold as a consolation prize for their crucifixion?

So there was good news Sunday — not quite as good as Tuesday night’s national news, but still pretty good — in just how solidly “Taking the Long Way” stands up as not just some sort of political milestone but a superior collection of music. (I’d still give the nod to “Home” as their best, but this remains a strong second.) The dance the trio did in collaboration with producer Rick Rubin and their songwriting collaborators was a slightly tricky one, with enough themes of personal iconoclasm running through several key songs that it almost came to feel like a concept album, even if they were only being allusive to the controversy that continued to follow them… with the exception of “Not Ready,” which was pretty darned specific, given how only a minority of us can relate to receiving death threats.

That anthem was preceded by a film reel of right-wing commentators having their way with the Chicks in the immediate wake of the Bush controversy, including Fox host Bill O’Reilly famously opining that “these are callow, foolish women who deserve to be slapped around.” Maines recounted their first writing session with Dan Wilson, who co-wrote six of the album’s songs, where “he said, ‘So you probably wanna write about the incident… You wanna write like an apology song?’ We said, ‘Fuck that.’ And he was like, ‘OK, fine. How about “Not Ready to Make Nice”?'” The result gave songwriting blind dates a good name. “Even though we went through all the stages of grief, we landed on anger for most of it,” said Strayer, the trio’s banjo player, “and we thought it was so specific to our story that it wouldn’t necessarily relate to everybody else, but little did we know or anticipate that it would become such an anthem for everybody else’s story as well.” Now, maybe even more than then, there’s something about their righteous Troll Derangement Syndrome that rings a bell.

The members were able to point out instances in other songs that referred in some way to the controversy. While each verse in “The Bitter End” was about a different kind of parting, and in some cases a sweeter one, they confirmed that the final verse did indeed describe their immediate estrangement in 2003 from the country music establishment they’d spent years kissing up to, be it label execs or radio programmers or fellow country stars. The last verse rang afresh with a mixture of resignation and the triumphalism that is inherent in any of their three-part harmonies: “You had a good time / Drinking all of our wine / After the show / We all rode the wave / Of that crazy parade / Oh, where’d you go / What happened to / The ones we knew / As long as I’m the shiniest star / Oh, there you are… / We’ll still be here when you come round again.” That’s a family reunion that never quite came true, as the group came to be more accepted in rock and Americana circles than in mainstream country… though the number of hats and boots in attendance at the Dolby made it clear there was a huge part of the former audience that never got lost back when sides were taken.

There were some more amusing memories during this first section, as “Lubbock or Leave It” led to a discussion of whose parents were most supportive at the time. Maines’ father, famed steel player Lloyd Maines, “a father figure to us musically,” was seen in a film clip describing how he’d tossed off an angry letter to the hometown Lubbock country station that banned the Chicks — whereas Strayer and Maguire, who are sisters, had “our mom was saying things like, ‘I’m gonna put her (Maines) over my lap and spank her’ — stuff like that. So we had a little different dynamic going on at home.”

Some other family fighting came up. “I bet that’s the only time you’ve ever heard sisters fight about in vitro,” said Maines, following a film clip in which Maguire and Strayer got into it about the difficulties of scheduling dates on their European tour around her IVF procedures. “I had to go see a different gynecologist in a different country every other day,” explained Maguire, while her sister had already had success using the procedure. The resulting song, being performed on this tour for the first time in two decades, was “So Hard,” surely one of the first songs on a bestselling album devoted to in vitro fertilization. (Ironically, Sara Bareilles just performed a contemporary one on the same subject at the Dolby just a couple of weeks ago.)

And speaking of songs that very much capture a moment in time, Strayer pointed out that “Baby Hold On” referred to the three children under the age of 3 1/2 she and then-husband Charlie Robison had at the time, and the need to find marital intimacy amid that, when they sang, “Let’s kick the babies out of bed / How ’bout you and me instead.” Strayer and Robison would be divorced within a couple of years, which may account for why the song was never played live until this year. “I think I thought it was about hope, but it was probably more about desperation at that point,” Strayer admitted to the Dolby audience. “We’ve all been there,” responded Maines.

The album also had its share of less overtly autobiographical but still deeply personal-sounding songs. “Favorite Year,” co-written with Sheryl Crow, was one of these, a spirited but still haunting recollection of a past love affair that left a lot of “what if” questions behind, even though the singer has a husband and child. If this one had a personal meaning for one or more of the members, we didn’t learn, because there were a few numbers from “Taking the Long Way” when they did just shut up and sing. But “when we were together, I was at my best” is both a warm thought and a chilling one. And the song provided yet another example of how, on this album, the trio sometimes sounded a lot closer to the Bangles than anything out of Nashville or even Texas.

Come the end of the album playback, Maines said they would be “taking a brief intermission, and come back and play this whole album backwards.” Instead, the second act brought nine songs that are a part of any of their contemporary sets — including the mashup of “Long Time Gone” and “Daddy Issues” they’ve been including ever since they premiered it in collaboration with Beyonce on the 2016 CMA Awards. (Is it really the 10th anniversary of that controversy?) The only original song performed from the last 20 years came from their only album released in the last 20 years, the title track of 2020’s “Gaslighter” … a divorce song, not a Trump song, though it being played during the week that America effectively voted to divorce the president gave it a little extra resonance.

There was more rock and less talk in this final greatest-hits segment. Of course fans would hope that another album might be in the hopper from the music world’s least studio-prolific superstar act shy of Lauryn Hill. Perhaps collecting memories and overseeing the documentary footage for the “Long Way” retrospective has reminded them they like the recording studio? Or perhaps they will continue to lean on repertoire. They’ve got one of the greatest catalogs in contemporary music, so it’s hard to complain if they find a way to milk it every few years. They took the short way around to becoming instantaneous pariahs 23 years ago, but when it came to going from that panic-button moment to being transformed into heroines for the ages, it’s been a hell of a long tail.

Kylie Kelce Reveals the One Thing That Makes Someone the Perfect Houseguest

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Kylie Kelce has revealed what a visitor needs to do to be the perfect guest in their home—and it has to do with impressing her and her NFL-star husband Jason Kelce's daughters.

On a recent episode of her podcast, "Not Gonna Lie," the mom of four revealed that their daughters— Finnley, 18 months, Bennett, 3, Elliotte, 5, and Wyatt, 7—love having guests read to them.

“I know one way to my girls’ hearts, and really kids, I always tell people you don’t need to bring gifts when you come to our house. If you come in, you sit down, you offer to read our girls a book, they will be like forever," she revealed.

Kelce added, “Like genuinely, we have obviously family [that] will come and they’ll bring this and they’ll bring that, but if when you come and you sit down, you read a book, I think it’s one of the best ways to connect with kids.”

The glimpse of her houseguest request came just days after she defended her and her spouse's decision to snap up multiple Philadelphia properties right next to each other.

In an episode of her podcast, which featured guest Nikki Glaser, Kelce, 34, and the comedian chatted about hate comments.

"Do you ever get in the comments? Do you ever get feisty?” Glaser asked.

In her reply, Kelce detailed an Instagram account that frequently posts information about their real estate.

“One of the ones that I did it—which was not to a person so it doesn’t really feel as aggressive—was like ‘Celeb Homes.’ They put a bird’s-eye view of where we live with our children, and they were trying to give details of our property," she said.

The account then revealed that the Kelce family had also purchased the home "next door."

“[They were like], ‘Why would you need this many houses?’ And I’m like, ‘for f---ing security and privacy, you pieces of s--t.' They left the post up, so it’s still there. You can still find it," Kelce explained.

“‘Why would anyone need three neighboring properties?’ For safety, privacy, and security. Get the f outta here," reads the comment Kelce left.

She then told the comedian, "I think I typed ‘get f---ed.’ And then when I tried to post it, it wouldn’t let me. Why? I want to tell people to get f---ed."

Her admission came after she offered a glimpse of the family's childcare arrangements during an appearance on an episode of "IMO with Michelle Obama and Craig Robinson." She explained that she and the former Philadelphia Eagles star prefer to keep their relatives closely involved in raising their children.

The couple have built a support system that allows them to balance their professional commitments with family life.

A major part of that arrangement involves Kelce's parents, Lisa and Ed McDevitt, who live just 15 minutes from the family's Philadelphia-area home.

Kelce explained that her parents regularly help care for the girls, making it possible for her and Jason to navigate their busy schedules without relying on round-the-clock childcare.

Although the couple do employ a nanny, Kelce revealed that the arrangement is limited to traditional working hours rather than having someone live in their home.

“My husband and I have very busy schedules. We don’t necessarily want a live-in nanny or someone who’s going to be raising our kids void of us. That feels weird. So we do a lot with my parents every chance we get. We do things with Jason’s parents. We find that those are the values that we were raised with that we want our kids to have, not just because we tell them that, but because we live it," she explained.

The decision, she explained, reflects their desire to remain actively involved in their daughters' everyday lives.

Rather than bringing in someone to provide full-time care, the Kelces turn to their extended family for additional support.

Kelce said maintaining those close family relationships is particularly important because she wants her daughters to grow up with the same values that shaped her and Jason's childhoods.

She also emphasized how grateful she is for the nanny who helps the family during the workday, acknowledging that the additional support is essential to managing their responsibilities.

However, the couple's approach to childcare goes beyond convenience.

For Kelce, living close to her parents has been a priority, and she admitted that the thought of settling somewhere far from her family has never appealed to her.

“We couldn’t do it without my parents. My parents live 15 minutes from us, and they have helped us in every way imaginable," she said.

The Pennsylvania native explained that remaining in the area where she grew up has allowed her to preserve those relationships while raising her own family.

That connection to her hometown has also influenced the kind of upbringing she hopes to provide her daughters.

Kelce wants the girls to become familiar with the places that shaped her childhood, from local businesses to the community organizations that continue to play a role in her life.

She believes that growing up with those personal connections can help children develop a stronger sense of responsibility toward the communities around them.

Her own involvement with the Eagles Autism Foundation is one example of how she has maintained those local ties.

For Kelce, the opportunity to contribute to the community where she was raised is another benefit of remaining close to home.

“Now, granted, we do have a nanny. I always give a shoutout because we couldn’t do it without her, but we have her during regular work hours and really nothing outside of that," she added.

Kelsi Karruli is a senior celebrity reporter who joined Realtor.com in 2025. She writes about trends encompassing real estate and celebrity properties. She previously wrote for Daily Mail, True Urban Culture, and JMedia Corporation, where she specialized in covering celebrities, relationships, psychology, and gender issues. She has a bachelor's degree in journalism from Pace University, where she received the James Rose Award for excellence in journalism. When she isn’t updating readers on A-listers' moves, she is spending time with her cat, Mary, and cheering on the Albanian soccer team.

Viktor Gyökeres the difference as Arsenal claim nervy win against Slavia Prague

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Another away day, another victory for Arsenal. This time, the much-maligned Viktor Gyökeres stole the show as poor Slavia Prague matched the longest winless streak in European Cup and Champions League history despite a spirited late comeback. That now stands at 23, equalling the mark set by AEK Athens and FCSB – formerly known as Steaua Bucharest – while Arsenal are now unbeaten in their last 10 away European trips. After last week’s wobble against Fleetwood, there was no prospect of Arsenal making the same mistake again as soon as Noni Madueke fired them ahead early on after being superbly set up by Gyökeres.

That was his third assist in his last five appearances, although Mikel Arteta will have been even more encouraged by the emphatic finish that had seemed to put the game beyond Slavia’s reach just before the break. While Kai Havertz remains his undoubted first choice, having another striker in form will only boost Arsenal’s challenge and it was noticeable that there was an extra spring in the 28-year-old’s step after his goal.

Slavia’s solitary victory in this competition came in their first-ever match in 2007 and, after losing 3-0 to Arsenal here exactly a year ago, the Czech champions knew their chances of avoiding the unwanted milestone were extremely slim. While Fleetwood’s giant-killing against the Premier League champions in the EFL Cup last week may have given them some hope of an upset, Arteta’s side has developed a killer instinct on their travels in Europe. Not since May 2025, in the second leg of their semi-final against Paris Saint-Germain, have Arsenal lost an away game. The 2-2 draw at Bayern Munich two weeks ago underlined their credentials as genuine contenders once again.

After their exploits at Anfield on Sunday that took them three points clear at the top of the Premier League, Arteta heavily rotated his side again with six changes. Martín Zubimendi was handed only his third start of a frustrating campaign for the Spain World Cup winner, while Gyökeres led the line for the third time in Arsenal’s past four matches.

The Sweden striker finished as top scorer last season with 21 goals and finally got off the mark against Fleetwood with a scrappy finish, although he has clearly been struggling for form and confidence. Yet there was no doubting the quality of his assist for Madueke’s goal after just eight minutes. David Raya’s long ball should have been dealt with by the Slavia defence but they made the criminal error of allowing it to bounce. That was the cue for Gyökeres to come alive as he beat his marker to the ball, chested it down and played the perfect pass for the England forward to run on to and score. Arteta’s reaction on the touchline suggested he enjoyed it too.

Mikel Merino was Arsenal’s hero here last year with two goals and he could have extended their lead on three occasions before the half-hour mark. The lively Madueke created his best opportunity after cutting the ball back to Merino but he could not beat Nazar Domchak in Slavia’s goal.

With Martin Ødegaard watching from the bench next to William Saliba, on his return from a long-term back injury, Arsenal lacked some of their usual fluency in attack. But Slavia were still no match for them and their task became almost impossible seven minutes before the break. Gyökeres exchanged a smart one-two with Merino inside the area before curling a brilliant shot into the far corner. It needed a goalline clearance from Michal Sadilek to prevent a rampant Arsenal inflicting more damage before half-time.

To their credit, Slavia started the second half with much more aggression after introducing their record signing Ange N’Guessan from the bench. The hosts came within a few centimetres of being awarded a penalty when Emmanuel Ayaosi was brought down by Ezri Konsa on the edge of the area. The England defender and Rice both found themselves in the book as Arsenal struggled to recapture their rhythm from the first half without ever looking in danger of conceding.

Kick off your evenings with the Guardian's take on the world of football

But that all changed in the last 20 minutes after David Jurasek let fly from long range and his effort was deflected just wide of Raya’s goal. From the resulting corner, Gabriel Magalhães somehow managed to clear two goalbound headers off the line in a matter of seconds. The Brazilian celebrated with a massive fist pump.

Mikulas Konecny should have done much better with another headed chance as Arsenal’s formidable defence was put to the test in the final 15 minutes. A spectacular save denied Oskar Kubiak as Raya managed to push the ball on to the post at full stretch. Slavia eventually scored the goal all their endeavour deserved in the final minute of normal time when Wiktor Nowak fired in at the far post. There were a few nervous moments in stoppage time and Arteta breathed a sigh of relief at the full-time whistle as Slavia were condemned to their fate.

West Ham make startling comeback to earn draw in Scott Parker’s first game in charge

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Cardiff 3-3 West Ham: The Hammers came back from three goals down to secure a point in Parker’s first match in charge

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Scott Parker saw his West Ham side recover from three goals down to draw 3-3 at Cardiff in a thrilling first game in charge.

Jack Moylan’s first hat-trick in English football had Cardiff in cruise control at half-time and Hammers fans venting their anger.

But the Hammers – inspired by captain Jarrod Bowen – fought back in incredible fashion as Divine Mukasa, Arne Engels and Max Kilman netted to secure a point.

Parker was confirmed as Hammers boss on Wednesday morning – just 24 hours after Nuno Espirito Santo’s departure – but it was a tough watch for the former England midfielder initially as Moylan had Cardiff 3-0 ahead inside 39 minutes.

Moylan scored a hat-trick on his Republic of Ireland debut against Grenada in May, but this was his first club treble since scoring three for Shelbourne in May 2023.

The draw extends Cardiff’s unbeaten home league run to 10 games over the last two seasons, while West Ham occupy the final play-off place in eighth.

Nuno’s 13-month tenure had ended after the Hammers had won just twice in six matches and lost successive home games to Southampton and West Brom.

Taty Castellanos had hinted at better times ahead, forcing Nathan Trott in to early action after Bowen’s effort had deflected to him.

But the Hammers were behind after Alex Robertson found Moylan in the 11th minute. Moylan advanced to find the top corner of Mads Hermansen’s net with a ferocious strike from 20 yards.

Hermansen tipped aside Calum Scanlon’s cross and Will Fish headed over as Cardiff probed. There was no reprieve for West Ham when Perry Ng curled a ball over a static defence after 36 minutes.

Moylan cleverly slowed down for Morato to bundle him over, a clear penalty. Cardiff had missed their last two spot-kicks, but this time Moylan was entrusted with the responsibility and sent Hermansen the wrong way.

Angry Hammers fans responded with cries of ‘you’re not fit to wear the shirt’, and many of them appeared to be making a swift departure three minutes later.

Joel Bagan’s corner cleared Fish at the near post, but Moylan came in behind him to head home his sixth goal in five games, and his 10th of the season.

Parker sent on Mohamadou Kante and Joel Piroe for Soungoutou Magassa and Castellanos at half-time and there was instantly more conviction from the Hammers.

Bowen floated a delicious pass through after 58 minutes, and Mukasa lifted the ball over Trott and in off a post.

The deficit was reduced further seven minutes later when Piroe flicked on Bowen’s pass and Engels slid home his first Hammers goal.

Kante nearly toe poked the ball past Trott before Kilman touched home Bowen’s brilliant cross, and West Ham almost won it through substitutes Ezra Mayers and Tomas Soucek.
