Arsenal survive second-half scare to beat Slavia Prague

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Slavia Prague vs Arsenal. UEFA Champions League.

Report as Arsenal move up to third in Champions League league phase table with 2-1 win over Slavia Prague; Noni Madueke and Viktor Gyokeres scored as the Gunners withstood second-half pressure to continue their unbeaten start in the competition

Football Features Writer @nicholaspwright

Arsenal survived a second-half onslaught from Slavia Prague to win 2-1 at the Fortuna Arena and continue their unbeaten start to the season in the Champions League.

The Gunners, runners-up last term, appeared on course for an emphatic victory after scoring twice through Noni Madueke and Viktor Gyokeres in a one-sided first half.

But Slavia roared back into the contest in the second period and gave Arsenal a scare as Wiktor Nowak's late goal from close range set up a nervous finale for Mikel Arteta's side.

Arsenal hung on to claim their third win in four Champions League games but needed a goal-line clearance from Gabriel Magalhaes and several saves from David Raya, who brilliantly pushed an Oskar Kubiak header against the post shortly before Slavia's goal.

Slavia had 17 shots in the second half, having only had one before the break as Arsenal were made to work for a result which looked a foregone conclusion after their dominant start.

Arteta freshened up his side with six changes from Sunday's 1-0 win over Liverpool and scored their early opener when Madueke finished coolly having been put through on goal by Gyokeres.

The striker added the second with a sumptuous, curling finish after combining with Mikel Merino but Arsenal missed chances to extend their lead before the break and Slavia responded.

A switch from a back four to a back three allowed the hosts to commit more bodies forward and they created a succession of chances as Arsenal were made to sweat for their victory, which sees them move up to third in the league phase table.

UCL | FC Porto 4-1 Napoli: Error prone Partenopei collapse in Porto

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Napoli had got back on track with Scott McTominay, but crumbled in a 4-1 defeat to Francesco Farioli’s Porto with some terrible defending on set plays and late Rafa Marin red card.

The Partenopei finally secured their first Champions League points beating Bodo/Glimt 4-3, but were fresh off a 2-0 Serie A loss at Juventus over the weekend.

Giovanni Di Lorenzo, Mathias Olivera, Stanislav Lobotka, Leonardo Spinazzola, Alisson Santos, Benoit Badiashile, Costantino Favasuli, David Neres and Alessandro Buongiorno were injured, while Luca Marianucci was not on the UEFA list. Rasmus Hojlund returned after missing the Juve trip with flu symptoms and Kevin De Bruyne shook off an ankle knock to play a more advanced role in this 3-5-2 formation.

FC Porto were top of the table domestically with a perfect record under Italian coach Francesco Farioli, yet lost all three European games this season to Manchester City, Real Betis and PSV Eindhoven. Vasco Sousa, William Gomes and former Roma defender Chris Smalling are out injured, while Oskar Pietuszewski needed a late fitness test after missing the last three matches. Ex-Milan strikers Andre Silva and Santiago Gimenez were in the squad.

Antonio Vergara returned to the starting XI after a long lay-off and was perhaps too eager, booked in the opening minute for crashing into Jan Bednarek.

Pepê had the ball in the net at the fourth minute, but was offside on the long ball over the top when smashing in off the underside of the woodwork.

Borja Sainz also skimmed the crossbar with a curler, so it was no surprise when Porto took the lead as Gabriel Veiga pumped in a corner for the totally free Bednarek header from nine yards. It was more sluggish defending from Napoli.

Man United must move heaven and earth to bring Cesc Fabregas to Old Trafford as their next head coach

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

By Matt Law | 04 Nov 2026 21:56, Last updated: 04 Nov 2026 22:03

Como put in one of the most extraordinary performances in Champions League history on Wednesday, with the Italian side recording a 9-1 win over Lens.

Anastasios Douvikas struck a hat-trick, while Nico Paz, Lucas Da Cunha, Marc Oliver Kempf, Mattia Liberali, Martin Baturina and Matteo Papaccioli were also on the scoresheet in France.

Como beat Manchester United 4-1 in their previous game in the Champions League, and Wednesday's success has left them fourth in the overall table, picking up nine points from four matches.

The Blue and Whites have netted 19 times in the league stage of the 2026-27 Champions League, only the holders Paris Saint-Germain (20) have more.

Only two teams have scored eight or more goals in a single Champions League fixture over the last 10 seasons, with Como now on an elite list with Bayern Munich - 8-2 vs. Barcelona in 2020 and then 9-2 vs. Dinamo Zagreb in 2024.

Meanwhile, Como are the first team to score nine in an away European Cup match since Feyenoord did so against Olympiakos Nicosia in September 1971.

The team's fourth-placed finish in the 2025-26 Serie A campaign saw them qualify for the Champions League, and they have already shown that competing at this level will not be an issue.

I Biancoblu have some incredible players - notably the excellent Paz - but so much praise has to go to head coach Cesc Fabregas, who is already making serious waves in the management game.

Fabregas, who will turn 40 next May, has won 46, drawn 26 and lost 25 of his 97 matches in charge of Como since being appointed permanent head coach in July 2024.

Como are fourth in the Serie A table this season on 23 points, only five points behind the leaders Lazio, and a challenge for the title cannot be ruled out.

Fabregas has a contract at Como until the summer of 2028, but Man United should make an attempt to appoint the Spaniard as their new head coach next year.

Whether that means keeping Michael Carrick and enduring some more pain is a different question entirely; there is also the option of bringing in an interim head coach for the rest of the season, should the club ultimately decide to move away from Carrick.

Fabregas' Arsenal and Chelsea connections would make a move to Man United a fascinating one, but he knows the Premier League and also knows what it takes to win it, doing so twice with Chelsea.

The Spaniard was a wonderfully gifted midfielder and has shown during his early management career that he can put together a team capable of playing incredibly exciting football.

Como were head and shoulders above the Red Devils in their recent European clash.

Man United must look to the future with their next appointment, and Fabregas has the potential to become one of the leading coaches in world football in the coming years.

Liverpool calamity hands Iraola his most chastening night yet

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Andoni Iraola looked up to the ceiling of Fenerbahce’s media auditorium as he tried to find the words to describe the madness he had just witnessed.

“Cheap” was his first conclusion, swiftly followed by “messy”; technically, Liverpool’s head coach was right but this was a moment when nobody would have found issue had he gone further. “Absurd” and “preposterous” would have been more accurate portrayals of this calamity.

Liverpool have won two European trophies in Istanbul, but they do not tend to enjoy visits to Turkey. Roy Hodgson, in August 2010, was the last Anfield manager to oversee a win here and there was an irony that the hero of that night in Trabzon – Dirk Kuyt – masterminded this downfall.

Kuyt, interim head coach, got a remarkable response out of a Fenerbahce team who had amassed 1,086 Premier League appearances between them and he was as giddy as the locals after Mason Greenwood, Archie Brown and substitute Vedat Muriqi scored to give the club a famous night.

Another quickfire equaliser for Fenerbahçe 💨 Archie Brown brings the Turkish side level ⚽️ 📺 Stream now on TNT Sports & HBO Max https://t.co/fRCRh6tXYB https://t.co/8kZUXPEC3C

But even Kuyt would have been the first to recognise the helping hand Fenerbahce were afforded. Liverpool were meek, naïve, and while the Turkish side danced in the rain on the Asian side of the city, Iraola was left to dissect the most chastening 90 minutes of his short Anfield career.

“It’s frustration because it’s a difficult place to come here and to play,” said Iraola. “During most of the game, we were thinking: ‘OK we can win this one.’ But obviously it hasn’t happened. We conceded, I think, messy goals, especially the first and the third one. The second one is a rebound.”

Yet it all started so smoothly. For all that Fenerbahce tried to put Liverpool out of their stride with a soundtrack of high-pitched whistling, Iraola’s side kept the ball well and they might have been ahead in the fifth minute but Victor Muñoz and Bradley Barcola, playing as a central striker, erred.

Another chance went begging when Joe Gomez fizzed a ball across the six-yard box that was screaming to be converted, but nobody could apply the finishing touch. When Trey Nyoni put Liverpool ahead, it seemed like all those local predictions of a resounding away win would come true.

What a moment for Trey Nyoni 🥹 The young Liverpool midfielder scores his first professional goal, and he does it on the Champions League stage ✨ 📺 Stream now on TNT Sports & HBO Max https://t.co/fRCRh6tXYB https://t.co/Vtj4Yd3tnn

Nyoni is a terrific player-in-the-making and the joy of seeing him wheel away, having become Liverpool’s second youngest Champions League scorer behind Trent Alexander-Arnold, was everything you want to see in football.

So for the landscape to change so dramatically – and so quickly – after Liverpool had taken the lead was staggering, and it all came about after Irfan Can Kahveci slid in and turned a cross from Mattéo Guendouzi on to the bar.

Those who remember Kuyt from his Anfield days 15 years ago will recall a player who never considered any cause to be lost and the Dutchman implored his team to attack the flanks with pace, even earning himself a yellow card at one point. Eventually, the pressure paid off.

Greenwood had felt aggrieved not to have been given a 23rd-minute penalty, after Kostas Tsimikas placed his hands on his back, but he did not have to wait long to make an impact, his opportunity coming after Dominik Szoboszlai conceded a cheap free-kick.

Szoboszlai is rapidly becoming an enigma. How can a player with the gift of being able to score goals from the heavens make the mistakes of a weekend amateur? There was no need for him to foul N’Golo Kanté but the error was compounded, Greenwood’s strike deflecting off Jérémy Jacquet.

Since Manchester United felt duty-bound to sell Greenwood in 2024 the former England forward has continued his career away from the Premier League. After leaving Marseille he has found a home in Istanbul where the fans have taken to him and they bellowed his name following the equaliser.

Alisson Becker, wrong-footed by Jacquet’s decision to break off from the wall and turn his body, was livid and rightly so. Liverpool had gone from sublime (in patches) to ridiculous and the passage of play up to the interval was hugely uncomfortable.

Would things settle down after the break? Not a bit of it. Liverpool, worryingly for Iraola, could not stamp any authority and though there was a brief respite when Cody Gakpo and Alexander Isak combined after being introduced as substitutes, the lead felt as sustainable as a snowball in a sauna.

That didn't take long ⏱️ Alexander Isak comes off the bench and gets himself on the scoresheet 📋 📺 Stream now on TNT Sports & HBO Max https://t.co/fRCRh6tXYB https://t.co/sGxu7R5et8

Fenerbahce’s followers had made this a hot house and they levelled as quickly as they had done in the first period when Brown, who started his career at Derby before having spells in Switzerland and Belgium, stole in at the back post to turn in a flicked clearance that Gakpo got wrong.

A draw would have been fine but Liverpool contrived to throw it away completely. Alisson stayed stuck on his line as a high ball came in, Nyoni, Jacquet and Alexis Mac Allister were weak in the challenge and Muriqi nodded in to spark pandemonium.

Vedat Muriqi sends the Fenerbahçe fans into pandemonium with a stunning header 🔊 📺 Stream now on TNT Sports & HBO Max https://t.co/fRCRh6tXYB https://t.co/YhJxPLOcbl

Having conceded three against LASK Linz and then another two in a dreadful 45 minutes against Villarreal before shipping another three here, Liverpool have shown that, as things stand, they cannot be considered as serious players in this competition. How can they be with defending so bad?

“We put ourselves in a good position,” Isak told LFC TV afterwards. “To lose this game is not a chaos situation, we just have to get back to winning games. If we win our next game then we are back to a really good position.”

Isak is right but the “if” is suddenly a big one – it will remain that way unless Liverpool learn quickly.

“Congratulations to Fenerbahce, they deserve it. We did not play how we had to play. We did not defend after the goal we conceded, after three or four minutes, automatically, we conceded another goal. I think we have to mark better if we want to win games. We did not play very good and that is why we lost.”

A remarkable little stat for you to digest before kick-off. The answer is here: Liverpool’s starting line-up has 1241 Premier League appearances. Fenerbahce have 1086.

“I think we have been [rotating] basically all the season. You play every three, four days you have to refresh the legs. The defensive positions we do not have as many options. We have injuries but offensively I think we can rotate a little bit and today we can see other players.”

On Bradley Barcola playing as a central striker:

“I think Bradley, even Victor [Munoz], are players that are wingers but have played sometimes up front. I think we should be mobile. I think we should attack the spaces that are there. They are there to have good combinations outside. We do not expect them to stay there as the nine. I think Bradley can do this job very well.”

The Hungarian has been struggling of late with a hamstring injury but, having returned to the matchday squad on Sunday against Arsenal, he is now back into the starting XI tonight.

I think he showed brilliance at Paris Saint-Germain and he showed it as well this year. We have to get to know each other very well. I think from that moment on it can only be better than it is already now. There are only good things and positive things we can look forward to.”

Andoni Iraola says he will not waste any more time thinking about the penalty Liverpool were denied against Arsenal and won’t seek an explanation from Pro Ref. There was a significant fallout following the 1-0 loss at Anfield on Sunday with many judges believing Stuart Attwell, who was VAR, should have consulted match official Andy Madley to highlight an obvious pull by Jurrien Timber that prevented Alexander Isak heading a Bradley Barcola cross.

Liverpool’s head coach explained in the heat of the moment that he would tell his players to go down if they felt contact in the area and Cody Gakpo, the Netherlands international, explained last night in Istanbul it was a message the squad had taken on board.

“As a team, we play pretty fair – maybe we should dive some more like the gaffer said,” Gakpo, who also had claims to be awarded a penalty against Arsenal, explained. “On both occasions you could have given a penalty or a foul, but they decided not. We have to live with the decision. That’s it.”

But as angry as he was with the situation, Iraola has drawn a line on the matter. He is not going to seek out Pro Ref for a discussion – even though he feels a number of decisions have gone against Liverpool this season – and is channelling his energy into other situations.

Iraola said: “I didn’t ask for an explanation and I haven’t received an explanation, no. I told you what you I think about the plays but, honestly, I don’t have the time to [speak to Pro Ref]. I have been preparing for Fenerbahce. It is just two days between games so I have just been focusing on this game and the opposition and how to prepare the team.”

Liverpool have made the long trip to Turkey to take on Fenerbahce on matchday four of the Champions League.

Liverpool enter this fixture having won all three Champions League matches thus far, beating Atletico Madrid, LASK and Villarreal to make it nine points from nine for Andoni Iraola’s side. Liverpool’s five-game unbeaten streak was ended by Arsenal in the league on Sunday. They return to the city where the club enjoyed one of their most memorable nights, beating AC Milan in the Champions League final 21 years ago.

“Istanbul is always going to be very special for Liverpool,” Iraola said. “It is difficult to find a more important night. Tomorrow [tonight] it won’t be easy, you talk about statistics, especially English teams playing here. They have very good players and a very good squad, they know how to play in this competition. We have to start strong in the game. It is very early in the season to establish comparisons. We want to continue how we have done in the Champions League, three games and nine points, it is a good chance to get to 12 points and then finish in the top eight. Champions League away is very difficult.”

In addition to the long-term absentees Hugo Ekitike, Conor Bradley and Giovanni, both Ryan Gravenberch and Jeremie Frimpong have missed the trip to Turkey.

Fenerbahce have made a slow start to their Champions League campaign, taking just two points from their opening three fixtures. They have drawn twice against Roma and Slavia Prague at home whilst they suffered a heavy 6-0 loss at Aston Villa. Former Liverpool forward Dirk Kuyt has been appointed their interim coach following the resignation of Ismail Kartal following their recent defeat to arch rivals Galatasaray just over a week ago.

“It is a great opportunity for us to show what we can do,” Kuyt said. “It is also a great opportunity for me, a game between two teams that are very important to me. We have a very good plan, and we have to believe in this plan. No-one who loves Fenerbahce is happy with the current situation, but this is a huge opportunity. We have players who have won the Champions League and the World Cup.”

They come into tonight’s match having lost back-to-back league games since drawing against Slavia Prague on matchday three of the Champions League. They have plenty of former Premier League players in their ranks, including Ederson, N’Golo Kante and Romelu Lukaku.

Player Ratings West Ham v Cardiff: Bowen Inspires Incredible Comeback

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

West Ham produced an extraordinary second-half comeback to rescue a 3-3 draw against Cardiff City, having looked completely shell-shocked as they trudged off at half-time three goals behind. Scott Parker’s first game in charge certainly wasn’t short of incident, with the Hammers seemingly heading for another humiliating defeat.

Joel Moylan’s first-half hat-trick had left West Ham in all sorts of trouble, but three substitutions at the interval helped transform the performance. Goals from Divine Mukasa, Arne Engels and Max Kilman completed a remarkable recovery, with the Hammers almost snatching a winner during a frantic finish.

Jarrod Bowen was the inspiration behind the comeback, although the difference between West Ham’s first and second-half performances was astonishing. Having looked completely devoid of confidence during the opening 45 minutes, the players suddenly discovered some urgency, purpose and attacking quality.

It was certainly a dramatic response to the dreadful performance against West Bromwich Albion, although Parker will have seen plenty of problems that still need addressing.

Made a couple of decent saves and produced one absolutely brilliant long throw that almost released Castellanos. Had little chance with Cardiff’s opening goal, but his decision to charge out of his penalty area, miss the ball completely and collide with Kilman was unnecessarily rash. Another unconvincing performance.

Steady and assured defensively in the first half, but his understanding with Bowen was almost nonexistent. The pair looked like they’d never met each other before. Completely different after the break, when Veltman played with real intensity, provided attacking width and combined excellently with the captain. One of West Ham’s most improved performers.

Some excellent defending, including a particularly impressive one-on-one challenge late in the game. His distribution was also a major positive, regularly pinging accurate balls towards the left flank and producing a lovely through ball to create Kanté’s one-on-one opportunity. One of West Ham’s better players across the 90 minutes.

Made an excellent early tackle but was subsequently booked after Solomon’s dreadful loss of possession exposed the defence. May have got a slight deflection on Cardiff’s opening goal, although there was little he could realistically have done. More than made amends in the 81st minute, showing West Ham’s strikers how to finish with a superb half-volley to equalise.

Actually started very well, winning numerous tackles and recovering possession repeatedly. Unfortunately, his lack of pace was badly exposed by a simple ball over the top, leading to a clumsy and avoidable penalty. He’s a centre-back being asked to play left-back, and it showed. Replaced at half-time.

Made his fair share of tackles and demonstrated some of the value of having a specialist defensive midfielder. Produced a crucial last-ditch challenge to prevent Cardiff from potentially scoring again. However, West Ham still left far too much space in central areas and he was withdrawn at half-time.

Worked hard in a box-to-box role but offered surprisingly little of note for much of the match. Played a decent pass in the build-up to one of Kanté’s attacking runs before making a beautifully timed run beyond Cardiff’s defence to score his first West Ham goal. Took the finish very well, and it was a significant contribution.

West Ham’s most technically accomplished player in the first half, looking comfortable in possession and enjoying the freedom of playing as a number ten. Picked out some lovely passes, including an excellent through ball that Castellanos couldn’t capitalise upon. Took his goal brilliantly, controlling Bowen’s chipped pass with his right foot before finishing delicately with his left. A very encouraging performance.

His performance was a microcosm of West Ham’s entire evening. Anonymous and nervous in the first half, but absolutely commanding in the second. His clever chipped pass created Mukasa’s goal, his long ball started the move for Engels’ strike, and his inswinging delivery provided the equaliser for Kilman.

By the closing stages he was practically running the game, producing a wonderful piece of jiggery-pokery to create a late chance for Mayers before delivering another dangerous inswinging ball that Souček headed on target. Outstanding after the break.

Showed some early purpose with a good run down the left, but his decision-making was dreadful. Repeatedly hesitated when presented with opportunities to shoot or pass, and one particularly poor three-yard ball straight to an opponent led to Kilman’s booking. His final deliveries were ineffective and he struggled to make the most of promising positions. Replaced after 67 minutes, and not before time.

Pretty much anonymous throughout the first half. Repeatedly outpaced when through balls offered him opportunities to run beyond Cardiff’s defence, including one occasion when he was beaten for speed by centre-back Will Fish. Managed a tame shot just before the interval but offered very little. Admittedly, West Ham’s disjointed attacking play hardly helped him. Replaced at half-time.

At least attempted to drop deep and get involved, although his first couple of contributions involved giving the ball away. Headed a decent Veltman cross straight at Trott and failed to connect properly with another Bowen delivery. Improved considerably, producing a good shot from distance before supplying an excellent flick-on for Engels’ goal.

Brought tremendous energy and directness after coming on at half-time. Produced an excellent driving run almost half the length of the pitch, followed by some lovely footwork to twist and turn through Cardiff’s defence. Gave the ball away cheaply on occasion but also forced Trott into a very good save after a wonderful first touch created a one-on-one opportunity. Probably did enough to force his way into Parker’s thoughts for the next game.

Introduced at half-time to replace Morato and provide some much-needed pace at full-back. Won an early tackle and looked more comfortable dealing with the demands of the position. Almost scored a dramatic late winner after making an excellent run to meet Bowen’s chipped delivery at the far post, attempting a left-footed instep volley that went tantalisingly close.

Made more of an attacking impression in his first 30 seconds than Solomon had managed for much of the evening. Immediately dropped his shoulder, cut inside and tried his luck with a shot that went wide. In the 89th minute, produced a stinging effort that Trott could only parry back into his penalty area. Confident, positive and willing to take responsibility.

Introduced in the 88th minute for Veltman. Almost scored a dramatic stoppage-time winner when he met another dangerous inswinging Bowen delivery with a well-directed header, forcing Nathan Trott into an excellent save. Not on the pitch long enough to warrant a numerical rating.

Kuyt revels in Fener’s special night as Iraola bemoans set-piece sloppoiness

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Dirk Kuyt celebrated a special night for Fenerbahce, as he believes the Turkish giants demonstrated what a "wonderful club" they are in their win over his old team Liverpool.

Kuyt spent six years at Liverpool, reaching the Champions League final with them in 2007.

Yet he lined up against the Reds on Wednesday, as he is in interim charge of Fener.

And the Dutchman led Fener to their first Champions League victory in 18 years as Vedat Muriqi's 79th-minute header clinched a 3-2 win.

Liverpool twice went ahead in Istanbul, through Trey Nyoni's first senior strike and Alexander Isak's maiden Champions League goal for the Reds.

Yet Fener twice fought back, with Mason Greenwood and Archie Brown on target before Muriqi's first Champions League goal settled the contest.

"It was a very special night for me and everyone who loves Fenerbahce," said Kuyt.

"We had some difficult times but I have to give a very big compliment to the players of Fenerbahce and also the staff members who keep pushing the players.

"We told each other to stick together, be united and keep believing in ourselves. That's something I learned from another club that means a lot to me and that is Liverpool, who we played tonight.

"I'm very happy with all the players, all the people involved, that we made a miracle tonight because not many people expected us to win. But we said before the game that we have a chance if we show passion, energy; if we stick to our game plan then the crowd will come behind us.

"We showed Europe what a wonderful club and what wonderful supporters that Fenerbahce has."

Fenerbahçe 3-2 Liverpool A fifth consecutive away defeat in the UCL for Liverpool against a Turkish club. Fenerbahçe were 2-1 down with just 17 minutes to go, but goals from Archie Brown & Vedat Muriqi turn the game around in their favour.

— Opta Analyst (@OptaAnalyst) November 4, 2026

Isak gave Liverpool a 2-1 lead in the 69th minute, meaning it is the latest they have ever gone ahead in a major European game and then lost.

Liverpool conceded three goals from set pieces in a Champions League game for just the second time, along with their 3-3 draw with Sevilla in November 2017, though this was the first time ever they have let in three non-penalty set-piece goals in one game.

"The main reason that I can find for why we lost this game is we conceded from three set pieces. It's something that we were doing very well in," he said.

"We knew it was one of the strengths of Fenerbahce. We concede from a free-kick, a corner and even from the long throw at the end.

"They're goals that have hurt us because we've gone twice in front after having good moments, controlling the game. We've found the goals but we have conceded in situations we should definitely defend better.

"It's frustrating because it's a difficult place to come here and play. In most parts of the game we were thinking we could win this one but obviously it hasn't happened.

"Especially the first goal and the third goal – the second one is a rebound – but the first one, I don't think we set the wall properly and the last one, Muriqi wins the ball against three defenders; it's definitely something we need to defend better."

Spalletti ‘happy to give a bit more responsibility’ to Juve youngster Alajbegovic

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Luciano Spalletti believes youngster Kerim Alajbegovic has earned "a bit more responsibility" ahead of Juventus' Europa League trip to AZ.

Alajbegovic scored his first Serie A goal for the Bianconeri in their 2-0 win over Napoli on Sunday.

At 19 years and 41 days old, he became the youngest non-Italian goalscorer in the history of the Serie A fixture between Juve and Napoli.

Alajbegovic is also the youngest forward to have both scored at least one goal and provided at least one assist in Europe's top-five leagues in the 2026-27 season.

In total, five forwards born in 2007 or later have both scored and assisted in these leagues this term: alongside the Bosnian, they are Lamine Yamal, Charalampos Kostoulas, Jay Robinson, and Paul Mendy.

Spalletti has been surprised by Alajbegovic's early impact, but has tempered expectations around the teenager.

"Alajbegovic took on the responsibility; a young player like him usually slides back in difficult moments, but instead he gave an important contribution to turn those difficulties around," said Spalletti.

Heading to Alkmaar  #AZJuve #UEL https://t.co/Fzq9AeliGy

— JuventusFC  (@juventusfcen) November 4, 2026

"He was signed originally as a player with promising prospects, but seeing his form and response, we are happy to give him a bit more responsibility.

"It is nice to see this response, but it is difficult to play regularly at this level, so let's not get ahead of ourselves.

"I can say he will feature for some of the game [against AZ], either sooner or later."

AZ and Juventus are meeting for the first time in European football, with the hosts unbeaten across their last five European matches against Italian sides (W4 D1).

Juventus are unbeaten across their three Europa League games against Dutch sides (W2 D1), scoring seven goals and conceding just once across those matches.

"Lost his spark..." - Vinicius Jr handed brutal verdict by Spanish media despite Real Madrid win

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

By Lewis Blain | 04 Nov 2026 21:32

Real Madrid maintained their perfect start in the 2026-27 Champions League league phase with a 3-2 victory over AEK Athens on Wednesday evening, but Vinicius Junior failed to take advantage of an opportunity to impress Jose Mourinho.

The Brazilian was handed a chance to strengthen his claims for a regular starting position ahead of his return from domestic suspension, but delivered another underwhelming performance on a night where Yan Diomande continued to enhance his reputation.

Vinicius's display attracted widespread criticism in Spain, with one publication awarding him just 2/10, while several prominent journalists questioned whether he deserves to remain in Madrid's strongest starting XI.

ElDesmarque were particularly scathing, awarding Vinicius just 2/10 and criticising his lack of attacking influence and defensive contribution. They highlighted his failure to track back, the space he left behind him and the fact that he completed none of his attempted dribbles.

AS were similarly unimpressed, describing his first half as ineffective and his second-half efforts as "un quiero y no puedo" - 'trying but unable to deliver'.

Former Real Madrid player and coach Jorge Valdano felt that "La palabra aceptable no es para un jugador como Vinicius" - 'the word acceptable does not apply to a player like Vinicius' - adding that he had produced just one good attacking move.

AS deputy editor Tomas Roncero was even more direct, declaring "Vini tenía una opción para lucirse, pero nada" - Vini had an opportunity to shine, but 'delivered nothing'. He subsequently questioned whether the Brazilian deserved to start, claiming that he had "perdido la luz" - 'lost his spark'.

Cadena SER's Dani Garrido agreed that Vinicius had wasted his opportunity, while Real Madrid correspondent Javier Herraez offered a pointed comparison with his rival for a starting position: "Diomande es un ejemplo, aprende de él Vini" - 'Diomande is an example, learn from him, Vini.'

The growing statistical gap between Vinicius and Diomande makes the criticism particularly difficult to dismiss.

According to figures highlighted by El Pais, Vinicius has attempted 37 dribbles this season, successfully completing just 15 at a rate of 40%. Diomande, meanwhile, has attempted 56 and completed 36, boasting a considerably better success rate of 64%.

Even more concerning is that Vinicius has gone two consecutive Champions League matches without attempting a single dribble, an extraordinary statistic for someone whose explosive running and ability to beat defenders have traditionally been his biggest strengths.

Diomande is therefore offering Mourinho an increasingly convincing alternative, with AS suggesting that the summer signing is already several lengths ahead in the battle for a starting position.

One disappointing performance would not normally threaten the status of a player with Vinicius's achievements, but the mounting criticism suggests that his problems are becoming a recurring issue rather than an isolated loss of form.

The Brazilian still possesses the quality to turn things around, but he needs to rediscover his confidence, directness and defensive commitment sooner rather than later.

With Diomande making a compelling case to remain in the team, Vinicius can no longer rely solely on his reputation to guarantee a starting position at the Bernabeu.

Harriet Phillips Says Raising Her Daughter Solo Was “Painful.” But Now She Can Ask Kate Middleton for Advice

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Harriet Phillips, the wife of Peter Phillips, son of Princess Anne, sat down with Hello! for an exclusive interview five months after her royal wedding, which took place at All Saints’ Church in Kemble, Gloucestershire, the village where she grew up. The ceremony was attended by King Charles III and Queen Camilla, as well as many other members of the royal family.

For Harriet, who until then led a life far removed from high society, her official entry into one of the world’s most famous royal families inevitably brought about some big changes.

Harriet and Peter Phillips at Royal Ascot

She and her daughter, Georgina, now live at Gatcombe, Princess Anne’s estate. Peter’s sister, Zara Tindall, also lives there with her husband, Mike, and their three children. After 24 years with the British National Health Service (NHS), where she specialized in child mental health and caring for the most vulnerable children, Harriet left the NHS to embark on a new professional venture at a private foundation, continuing to focus on early-childhood development.

She has also taken on her first official role as patron of Home-Start Cotswolds, an organization that supports families with young children. “Becoming a parent can be one of the most wonderful experiences in life, but it can also feel overwhelming and isolating,” Phillips told Hello! “We often assume that parenting comes naturally, but the reality is that everyone needs support at different points. Home-Start offers vital, practical, and nonjudgmental support. Just having someone to listen can make an enormous difference.”

For Harriet, the decision to leave the health service does not represent a break with the past: “It’s been an enormous privilege to spend 24 years working in the NHS, and I’ve loved my career. It has taught me so much, professionally and personally. Over time, I’ve become increasingly passionate about the importance of promoting healthy brain development. If we can support parents during this critical early period, we have an opportunity to make a lasting difference for children.”

The Phillips couple at the 2026 DMMI Royal Charity Polo Cup

Harriet then looked back on her love story with Peter, which began in an entirely unexpected way. The two first met properly in early 2022, when their three daughters were on the same hockey team. At first there was just some casual small talk between them, then walks with their dogs, followed by dinners at pubs away from prying eyes and, finally, evenings spent cooking together. A low-key courtship, away from the spotlight, blossomed into a love story.

When she met Peter, there wasn’t a “thunderbolt moment”; Harriet said she simply felt comfortable with him, enjoying an unspoken sense of calm. “I just felt incredibly at ease with him and experienced a lovely, peaceful, safe feeling when talking to him—like being with a really good friend,” she told Hello!

He was the one who made the first move: “Peter was the brave one in recognizing that there was something between us and acting on it. This time, I thought, If I’m lucky enough to meet someone again, I want things to move slowly and ensure that everything is entirely right.” Then she added, unable to hide her happiness: “He is very romantic, loving, kind, loyal, amazing, and my best friend.” Their early dates were simple and low-key. “He’s a great cook and I love cooking. He’s great at barbecues. It was wholesome and suited to who we both are as people,” she said.

On July 25, 2025, during a weekend in the south of England, Peter proposed on a beach. “We were on a weekend getaway in the south of England on a hot day during the heat wave. He asked me to marry him on the beach; I was completely overcome,” she said. “He had the ring made at Pragnell beforehand—it’s absolutely beautiful.” Neither of their families had been told about the surprise: “It was the best-kept secret ever.”

During the interview, Harriet also reflected on her childhood in Gloucestershire and fondly remembered her parents and their long marriage. “I was brought up in a village in the middle of fields. My parents celebrated an incredible 51 years of marriage, and sadly, my father died just before Peter and I got together.” It was an experience that left a deep mark on her life, and today, as she said in reflecting on her own love story, Harriet also sees it in the context of meeting the Princess Royal’s son: “I often think maybe that’s why Peter came along.”

The loss of her father, and the difficulties she faced in the years that followed, helped shape her outlook on life. Harriet explained: “There is a beauty that comes out of difficult situations. A beautiful jewel is formed under pressure. Difficulties in life enable you to relate to people and build empathy.”

Harriet and Peter with Princess Royal Anne

Harriet then talked about her first meeting with Princess Anne, her eventual mother-in-law, which was much more informal than she had imagined it would be. They met in the garden at Gatcombe, sitting on benches and drinking cups of tea. “There was always going to be initial nerves about meeting anyone’s family members, but I am quite a calm person,” she said. “It was really relaxed…It couldn’t have been nicer.”

Harriet has come to appreciate, above all, the princess’s dedication to her work and local charities: “She sets an amazing example with her work ethic. What I’ve seen is her tireless attention to local charities and her genuine interests. It’s important to do things that match who you are, where you live, and what your passions are.”

And their relationship already seems very strong. “She’s brilliant, really good fun, and straight-talking,” Harriet said. “She’s incredibly generous—we had our wedding reception at Gatcombe, and it was amazing and special for Peter and I to have our wedding where he grew up.”

Charles and Camilla also welcomed her warmly right from the start, as seen in the various photos taken at public events. Her first meeting with the king and the queen consort took place at Royal Ascot: “It was so lovely and relaxed and there was lots of laughter. You feel as though you’re meeting family, but at the same time, it feels otherworldly. Both the king and the queen have been so welcoming to me and Georgina. I can’t thank them both enough.”

The couple with the king and queen at Royal Ascot

After their wedding, Harriet and Peter attended several public events, including Wimbledon. In September, they were together at Somerset House in London for the opening of the exhibition “Reviving Craft: Reflection of the Mind,” dedicated to traditional Chinese craftsmanship and featuring over 70 artists.

Harriet also spoke with Hello! about Kate Middleton, including the admiration she feels for her and the bond they’ve formed. “I can look to Catherine for advice and guidance,” she said. She recalled a special moment during a vacation on the Isles of Scilly: The children (Georgina, George, Charlotte, and Louis) found themselves playing in the water together, in a spontaneous and informal atmosphere. “I’ve had an incredible experience being welcomed in. We happened to be on holiday in the same place. It was great because the children were all jumping into the water at the same time,” she shared. “It was very casual, but there is a clear level of respect there.” And speaking of the Princess of Wales, she added: “She’s very openhearted, and I would always feel comfortable asking her if I needed advice about something.”

Her first stay at Balmoral with her daughter was also a special experience. Scotland is one of Harriet’s favorite destinations, and vacations with the royal family have remained surprisingly simple: “It’s the loveliest family holiday with an extraordinary layer…. We go walking and running, play football with the children, cycle in the hills, and go wild swimming, which is fantastic.”

Then, inevitably, her memories turned to the wedding, an event marked by emotional moments. Harriet got ready with her mother and the three girls in the family: Georgina, Savannah, and Isla. Then her brother, Nicholas, arrived to drive her to the church. “It was quite wet and it had rained that morning, but the girls kept my train completely white and immaculate,” said said. “They did a really good job.”

In the church, decorated with floral arrangements that seemed to bring nature indoors, Harriet carried a bouquet with myrtle and lily of the valley: “All my family members have had myrtle in their wedding flowers, so I continued that tradition.”

Before going in, the couple prayed together with the vicar. “Then [we] walked into the fragrance of the flowers in the church. It was incredible,” she said. “It’s such a beautiful church, and I grew up going to it. It’s tiny—we were all squished in!” The presence of the king, the queen, and other members of the royal family didn’t distract her: “I was so focused on being in the moment. Of course, you’re aware that people are around you, but I really wanted to enjoy it and be present. I was so happy. That’s very natural for me. I was peaceful. I prayed. Maybe the peace of God came over me.”

The reception took place in the garden at Gatcombe. The sun came out at one point; there was live music, Champagne, and a casual menu featuring lamb raised on the estate. Instead of the traditional wedding cake, there was a huge tiramisu. “There was lots of laughter and it was an amazing family day. It finished at midnight, but everyone wanted it to carry on,” she said.

Before meeting Peter, Harriet was a single mother raising her preteen daughter, Georgina. It wasn’t always an easy journey, which made her particularly sensitive to the challenges that parents face and the importance of having a support network.

“Although at times it was a painful experience, I did have the support of people who loved us. Any time in life when you’re under pressure or facing adversity, something good always comes from it,” she said. “Lean in; don’t be scared.” Her Christian faith played an important role—“It’s definitely the foundation of how I live; it’s like a golden thread that has woven through my life. I was brought up in a Christian family, but this is a little bit different because it’s like making a decision for yourself: It is a personal relationship and a conscious choice, rather than just being religious.”

Harriet is very proud of her daughter and the relationship they’ve built. “Becoming a mother gives you an inner strength to protect your loved one and face whatever comes,” she said. “Everyone has challenges in life that are dressed up differently. Maybe this has been my challenge—the experience of bringing up a child on my own. I’ve been able to see the good things that have come out of it.”

Harriet and Peter with their three daughters on their wedding day

Today her family has grown, and Harriet looks to the future with enthusiasm, but without rushing: “Peter and I are so fortunate to have such wonderful girls between us. I’m excited about what’s to come with our family and always mindful that going gently is so important.”

Her new life with Peter doesn’t mean setting aside her own identity. “I will continue as I am. I’m a professional and will continue working in the area I’m passionate about,” she said. “I’ve got a wealth of knowledge that I don’t want to waste, so I will continue to use that.” When asked what she’d like to accomplish in the next 12 months, she gave an answer that once again came back to children and families: raising greater awareness of the work of Home-Start Cotswolds. As she explained, “We don’t just support babies by caring for them directly—we support babies by supporting the people who care for them.”

It’s a philosophy that also seems to capture her new sense of balance in her life. She has joined the royal family without ceasing to be Harriet, the woman who for 24 years worked alongside children and their parents, and who today has found in Peter a life partner and, above all, a best friend.

Her mission at Home-Start brings her even closer to the Princess of Wales, who has also been committed for years to supporting the well-being of children in their early years, a crucial period that shapes their personalities and determines the adults they will become. Who knows if the two women, now close friends, might one day form a professional partnership as well.

Harriet with Peter, Zara, and Mike Tindall.

Matthew Stafford's wife calls out NFL over apparent scheduling disadvantage given to Rams

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

For the first time ever, the NFL will play a game the night before Thanksgiving, with the Los Angeles Rams hosting the Green Bay Packers in a rare Wednesday matchup on Nov. 25 at 8 p.m. ET on Netflix.

To make the game work on the schedule, both teams were essentially forced to sacrifice their bye week, and Kelly Stafford definitely isn't happy about that fact. The wife of Matthew Stafford took to social media this week to call out the NFL for putting the Rams at a scheduling disadvantage compared to the league's other teams.

"Just curious to hear the thought process behind making the Rams the only team in the NFL without a true bye week," Kelly wrote on Instagram. "Because damn, after 18 years around this league, even I need a week off during the season...and I have never played a single snap."

Normally, a team goes 14 days between games during a bye week, but the Rams will only be getting 10 days off. Here's what their schedule looks like in the lead-up to their Thanksgiving Eve showdown with the Packers:

Week 10 (Nov. 15): at Cardinals (Sunday)Week 11 (Nov. 22): BYEWeek 12 (Nov. 25): Packers (Wednesday)Week 13 (Dec. 3): Chiefs (Thursday)

The Packers will be in a similar situation, but they get a slightly more favorable schedule since they don't have to play on Thursday night in Week 13. Instead, the Packers will play on Sunday, Dec. 6, so Green Bay will play only one game over a 19-day period. They still won't have a full bye, but it's better than what the Rams have.

Here's what Green Bay's schedule looks like in that same window:

Week 10 (Nov. 15): Vikings (Sunday)Week 11 (Nov. 22): BYEWeek 12 (Nov. 25): at Rams (Wednesday)Week 13 (Dec. 6): at Saints (Sunday)

NFL teams look forward to the bye each year so they can rest and heal, but Rams and Packers players won't be getting the full week off in 2026.

"I'm tired just watching from the stands," Kelly wrote. "Can't imagine how the guys feel."

Kelly then ended things by asking the NFL to explain why the Rams got hosed over.

"Anyway, feel free to leave that explanation right here," Stafford said.

The Rams were given one of the wildest schedules in the NFL this year. Not only are they playing a Wednesday game, but they also have a Friday game against the Seahawks on Christmas Day. If the Rams end up playing a Saturday game, which could happen if they get flexed in Week 17 or Week 18, they'd become just the fourth team in 99 years to play a game on six different days of the week during a single season.

The Rams have been the NFL's guinea pigs this year. They also played in the inaugural Australia game back in Week 1. Thanks to that trip, the Rams are scheduled to travel more than 34,000 miles this season, which is the second-most in the NFL.

Although Kelly Stafford isn't happy about the Rams losing their bye week, the Rams will be home for Thanksgiving. That means they'll get to spend the holiday weekend with their families, so the team's odd schedule isn't a total loss.

Square Enix will ‘most likely’ focus on new games after Final Fantasy 7 Revelation, producer says

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

What's next? Will the fantasy finally be final?

Mainline Final Fantasy entries have taken a bit of a backseat in lieu of the FF7 remake trilogy, but that should be changing soon.

The trilogy’s producer Yoshinori Kitase teased the franchise’s future in a new interview, but I also wouldn’t hold my breath for Final Fantasy 17 launching any time soon after FF7 Revelation drops this April.

“I think that mainly we are more likely to invest in new titles and new games, but at the same time, for example, this time with the [FF7] remake, there was me and other creators who worked on the original game, which we had really strong feelings towards,” Kitase said when asked about new IP and remakes in the interview with IGN. “And we really wanted to create something new related to the old game to allow new users to play and to know the appeal of this game as much as the old users who play the old game. So there might be different cases like this one where the company is willing to invest in remaking old projects and other kinds of games like it happened this time.”

That’s…not really a definitive answer either way regarding remakes vs. new entries vs. new IP at Square, which is notoriously busy with multiple RPG releases year in and out across its numerous dev teams. For example, this year alone, the company has released Final Fantasy Resonance, The Adventures of Elliot: The Millennium Tales, Bravely Default Flying Fairy HD Remaster, and a few others.

Kitase’s words are iffy no matter how you look at it, but mainline FF entries have generally slowed down quite a bit. FF16 launched in 2023, but FF15 released way back in 2016. Meanwhile, FF7 trilogy games have come out in 2020, 2024, and next year again in 2027.

I’m generally a very big fan of the remake trilogy, and FF7 Revelation is one of my most anticipated titles of 2027, but I have a hard time imagining the company going on to remake another game… although I would not be against it, for several classic titles (6, 8, 9, or 10, please). Additionally, this year, FF Resonance has shown a true thirst and appreciation for classic turn-based games on top of the action-combat focus of recent titles, so I’ll be heavily tuned in to see what direction the series goes next.

Check out the full IGN interview for more from Kitase, including his thoughts on Clair Obscur: Expedition 33 being heavily inspired by Final Fantasy classics.

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The halfway point of the 2026 NFL season arrives this weekend. Next week, Dianna Russini apparently will return to sports media.

Per a source with knowledge of the situation, Russini has begun getting the word out to people in the league that she’ll be returning to work “next week.”

It’s unclear where she’ll be working, or what she’ll be doing.

She resigned from The Athletic in April, one week after photos were published by the New York Post of Russini and Patriots coach Mike Vrabel at a resort in Arizona. In both her comments to the Post and her resignation letter to The Athletic, she denied that there was any type of impropriety.

The Athletic conducted a review of her work. Its findings were released in early September.

“Russini’s relationship with Vrabel was in clear violation of The Athletic’s standards,” wrote Mike Semel of The Athletic, who conducted the investigation. “Regardless of whether the relationship was romantic, it was a breach and should have been disclosed.”

Beyond the relationship was the manner in which she handled the emergence of the controversy.

"[W]hen first confronted with the photos by The New York Post,” Semel wrote, “Russini waited two days before telling her editors and managers. She then told a story to explain the photos that did not align with facts, raising issues of trust.”

Along the way, a strange story emerged regarding Russini being pulled over for texting while driving — made stranger by her decision to tell the story publicly and embellish the notion that she FaceTimed a head coach for help in getting out of the ticket.

So if she’ll be reporting again, she’ll need to restore her own credibility. Especially given the unique set of facts that resulted in her departure from The Athletic.

The British pop star performed a mini-set for the almost-100,000 sports fans gathered at this race that stops a nation.

Dirk Kuyt, Liverpool’s big-game hero and embodiment of the Benitez era

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

English fans can be excused for never been quite sure what to expect when a striker swaps the Eredivisie for the Premier League.

Will they be the next Robin van Persie or Ruud van Nistelrooy? Or are they being sold false goods? Think Ricky van Wolfswinkel. A star in Holland with Utrecht and then in Portugal with Sporting but the scorer of precisely one goal in England during a full season with Norwich.

Dirk Kuyt had plundered 71 goals in 101 games for Feyenoord before Liverpool pressed ‘buy’ in the summer of 2006. In theory, the Reds were getting a bargain at just £10million. A proven goalscorer and, those who had seen him in action, the very antithesis of a goal hanger.

Yes, Kuyt ran around a lot. He worked for the team. He put in a shift. Ironically, that was part of the reason why his career at Liverpool will be remembered as that of honest pro rather than superstar.

Kuyt, with those solid legs and neat mop of blonde hair, made his debut in a 2-1 home win over West Ham on August 26, 2006, coming on as a substitute to replace Peter Crouch.

That 2006-07 Liverpool team could be classed as being ‘between strikers’.

Crouch brought something different to the mix and Craig Bellamy was a classy pest with a goal or two in him, while Robbie Fowler had come back for a second stint but was more nostalgia act than genuine first-teamer so there was a goalscoring void to be filled. Was Kuyt the man?

This, of course, was the Rafa Benitez era at Anfield. His tactical wizardry had guided a genuinely mixed bag of a Liverpool team to Champions League glory in 2005, but now there was the Holy Grail of trying to win the Premier League.

Anyone and everyone can chip in on cup runs, but a title assault needs a genuine and regular goalscorer. A 20-goal haul from Kuyt and solid contributions from elsewhere could have been the winning formula.

The Dutchman took a little while to settle but scored twice in four days at the end of September, helping Liverpool to home wins against Newcastle and Spurs.

Reds fans were hopeful but cautious too. On the one hand, there was a feeling that could this be the start of something. On the other, there was a nagging feeling that he was good but not great. A seven out of 10 player.

Fans are greedy. They want superstars. They want No.9s and No.10s. Kuyt looked like what he was. A £10million signing from the Dutch league.

He scored 14 goals in that first campaign, a very respectable haul but not a game-changing one.

In the next, he managed 11 while a late flurry in Liverpool’s near-miss 2008-09 season yielded 15.

It was back to 11 in the following campaign but back up to 15 in 2009-10, a tally boosted by one very special afternoon against Manchester United.

One thing was very clear. Rafa loved him. Or rather, given that the Spaniard doesn’t really do love, Rafa saw how valuable he was to the cause. From being an out-and-out striker, Benitez turned Kuyt into the ultimate workhorse, ploughing a furrow up and down one side of a front three.

READ: When Dirk Kuyt shamelessly stole Luis Suarez’s wondergoal v Man Utd

And Liverpool fans adjusted with him. The fanciful expectations of Kuyt being a world-beater had been downgraded to a different type of admiration. He wasn’t Pele, but good old Dirk would play his heart out for the club and die for the cause. And that goes a long, long way on Merseyside.

While other Reds flickered and flirted with greatness, Kuyt offered a rubber-stamped guarantee. Whatever the occasion, whatever the opponent, whatever the location, he would put in a blue-collar shift week in, week out.

That can sound patronising, but Kuyt had another trick up his sleeve which moved him into a category beyond honest trier. The man from the little coastal town of Katwijk aan See (Dutch for Clacton-on-Sea… well, not really) could score big goals in big games.

When he finally left the Big Anfield House in 2012, his ‘best bits’ were quite something:

– Scorer in the 2006-07 Champions League final against AC Milan

– Scorer of the winning penalty in the 2007 semi-final second leg against Chelsea.

– First player in 21 years to score a hat-trick against Manchester United

– Injury-time penalty to secure dramatic derby win at Everton

– Extra-time goal against Cardiff in the 2012 League Cup final

– Shootout goal against Cardiff to secure League Cup final glory

There were other important strikes against Arsenal, while he netted seven times in the 2007-08 Champions League campaign. Even the hat-trick against Manchester United had added cult value as they were all one-yard tap-ins. Those goalscoring instincts had never left him.

Not many can claim to have scored a hat-trick against Manchester United.

But there was Dirk @Kuyt, #onthisday in 2011… ⚽⚽⚽ pic.twitter.com/TzdKhIi54X

In many ways, Dirk Kuyt was the embodiment of the Benitez era. Tactically astute, hard-working and capable of taking it up a level on big occasions.

You don’t build a football team just by throwing 11 superstars together. Even Brazil 70 had a few in their ranks who provided solidity and balance. That was Dirk Kuyt at Liverpool. Popular, reliable and hugely valuable.

Imagine Kuyt was at his peak now. Would he make this current high-flying Liverpool team? Not as a regular starter, but Jurgen Klopp would have him as a squad player all day long.

Slight injury concern for one of the litany of superstar forwards in the squad? Two games in four days? Tough away assignment where you need to dig in but try to nick a big goal?

Premier League legend with 163 goals set to return to football at bottom-of-the-table EFL club four months after dramatic exit

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Former Tottenham Hotspur striker Jermain Defoe is poised to return to the dugout as assistant manager to Karl Robinson at League One strugglers Bromley. The ex-England marksman has agreed to join the south London club's coaching staff four months after his abrupt departure from National League side Woking.

According to talkSPORT, Defoe will serve as second-in-command once Robinson finalises his appointment to succeed Andy Woodman, who was dismissed on October 22. Ranked 10th on the Premier League's all-time scoring chart with 163 goals across 496 games for six clubs, the ex-forward is Tottenham's seventh-highest scorer. The pair have been tasked with rescuing the Ravens from the foot of the third tier.

Defoe has been out of work since leaving Woking in July after less than four months in charge following a falling-out with their director of football, failing to attend pre-season training before calling to confirm his exit.

Meanwhile, former Charlton Athletic, MK Dons and Oxford United boss Robinson was sacked by Salford City in June after losing the League Two play-off final. He previously tried to sign Defoe for Oxford after the striker left Rangers in 2022.

Bromley also interviewed ex-Cesena boss Ashley Cole and considered Matt Bloomfield after sacking 55-year-old Woodman, who ended the club's 132-year wait for Football League status in 2024 before guiding them to League One.

Speaking on the Inside the 72 podcast, Woodman said: "The easiest way I can sum this up is that when I got the job at Bromley, my first game was Wealdstone and my last game in the league was Leicester. I was pretty disappointed and pretty upset. It felt like I'd been misled, in numerous ways.

"To give five-and-a-half years of your life to a club and a fanbase that I really loved and bought into, where I gave everything I had, to then be told in no more than three or four minutes 'thanks'. That was it really.

"There's no other way of dressing it up. That's the truth. I'm disappointed, but that's football as we know it guys. Unfortunately it never, ever surprises us."

Robinson and Defoe will inherit a struggling squad sitting three points adrift of safety with just two wins from 10 league outings. Arresting a six-match winless run is the immediate priority as the Ravens navigate their maiden campaign at this level. Both coaches will now look to rebuild their reputations by steering the south Londoners clear of relegation danger.
